- Standalone `ekb_ginsert` executable wrapper, including C-compatible help/version text, default `E_KNOWLEDGE` basename, first-input basename selection before stdin defaulting, startup `OutputLevel = 0` global side effect, generated `FILES/<name>` training examples from EPCL/PCL input with C-compatible shared external variable-name mapping for compressed PCL clause input, proof-step marking, proof-distance/reference analysis, negative-example selection from KB description parameters, duplicate-name rejection before generation, generated-file reparsing through `KBParseExampleFile`, and metadata rewrite order.

- Standalone `tsm_classify` executable wrapper, including C-compatible help/version text, index/TSM option parsing, output-file handling including `-o -` and early output-file creation before later input-open failure, C-shaped two-line input/output file-open diagnostics, C `OutClose` wording on final flush failure, concatenated input scanning, `Training:`/`Test:` annotated-term parsing, annotation flattening, fixed class-weight translation, TSM build/evaluation setup, per-term classification progress, final source-weighted success summary, and WSL compare-tool coverage for help/version output plus a small non-empty stdin classification workload against an archived C reference binary linked from the existing source object and libraries.

Pending:

- No additional learning/support executable wrappers are currently known from the reviewed `PROVER`, `SIMPLE_APPS`, and standalone `EXTERNAL` application inventory; remaining work here is broader filesystem regression coverage and expanded C/Rust executable comparison.
- Expanded reference executable comparison on a larger learned-data corpus, larger direct-example protocol corpus, platform-specific file/broken-pipe suffixes, platform-specific empty-test-set floating-point spelling, and performance coverage for the TSM build/classification path.

Change-later notes:

- `ekb_create` mirrors C's partial filesystem side effects and inconsistent mkdir diagnostics. A modernized mode should make creation transactional and report the exact failing path.
- `ekb_delete` mirrors C's non-transactional delete order: metadata is removed in memory, the stored example file is unlinked, and only then are `clausepatterns` and `problems` rewritten. A modernized KB API should stage these updates or make recovery behavior explicit.
- `ekb_ginsert` mirrors C's non-transactional generated-file flow: it writes `FILES/<name>` before parsing `signature`/`clausepatterns` and before metadata integration, so later parse or write failures can leave an orphan generated example. It also preserves the single-example multi-input behavior, derives the default name only from the first non-stdin input, and resets process-global `OutputLevel` to `0` at startup despite exposing no local output-level option.
- `ekb_insert` mirrors C's copy-before-parse flow and delayed metadata writes, so failed insertion can leave `FILES/` payloads without matching metadata. It also keeps the C quirk where `--name` applies only to the first input file.
- `ExampleSetInsert` can partially mutate the numeric index before a duplicate-name failure, and `ExampleSet`'s `count` field is a maximum inserted identifier rather than the current size. Keep both accidents visible until generated-id compatibility is settled.
- C `TSMClassifySet` owns progress printing to stdout while `tsm_classify.c` writes only the final summary through `GlobalOut`; Rust preserves that split for compatibility, but a modernized API should separate classifier results from presentation.
- `tsm_classify.c` opens `GlobalOut` before inserting the default `-` input and before concatenating/parsing any input files, so `-o` can create or truncate the summary file before later input failures. Rust preserves this side effect with one explicit summary writer; transactional output should be a separate cleaned mode.
- The C source tree keeps the `tsm_classify` target commented out in `PROVER/Makefile` even though `tsm_classify.c` is still present. The comparison harness links it only inside the disposable WSL build cache; a future packaging pass should decide whether this learning utility is intentionally installed or kept as source-only compatibility material.
- The executable hardcodes the `(Sources, Class)` annotation shape through a generic weight vector with only slot `0` set. A cleaned interface should use typed field names once learned-data compatibility is established.
- `direct_examples.c` opens the output path before parsing input and writes both generated sections through `GlobalOut`; Rust preserves the early create/truncate side effect with one explicit writer rather than carrying C's paired `OutOpen`/`OpenGlobalOut` global-output setup.
- PCL-support executables including `checkproof.c`, `epclanalyse.c`, `epclextract.c`, `epcllemma.c`, `ekb_ginsert.c`, and `direct_examples.c` disable the global `ClausesHaveLocalVariables` switch so compressed PCL input shares name-to-variable mappings across clauses. Rust now preserves this for the full-PCL executable paths through explicit `PclStepParseOptions`/`ClauseParseOptions` rather than a process-global parser flag; keep future compressed-PCL call sites on explicit parser configuration instead of reintroducing hidden global state.
- `direct_examples.c` computes the negative-example budget as `proof_steps ? neg_proportion*proof_steps : neg_examples`, with C double-to-long truncation and no upper clamp. Rust preserves that branch and truncation; a modernized selector should expose separate positive and negative limits explicitly.

## Factoring

Rust files:

- `src/clauses/factor.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_factor.c`
- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_factor.h`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_factoring.c`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_factoring.h`

Implemented:

- First-order ordered factor candidate enumeration matching `ClausePosFirstOrderedFactorLiterals` / `ClausePosNextOrderedFactorLiterals`.
- First-order `ComputeOrderedFactor`, including second-literal side retry, directed unification, proof-state-owned fresh-variable-bank reset, bank-backed post-unifier maximality recheck, normalized copy excluding the second literal, and resolved/duplicate cleanup.
- First-order `ComputeAllOrderedFactors`, including Horn and `CPNoGeneration` gates, clause-set insertion, parent proof-depth/proof-size/TPTP/SOS metadata propagation, `DCOrderedFactor` derivation entries, and opt-in represented `inf_factor` proof-documentation output.
- First-order equality factor candidate enumeration matching `ClausePosFirstEqualityFactorSides` / `ClausePosNextEqualityFactorSides`.
- `ComputeEqualityFactor` and `ComputeAllEqualityFactors` for first-order complete-MGU candidates and higher-order `CsuIterator` enumeration, including higher-order pattern-oracle CSU results, the C free-variable/equational guard, proof-state-owned fresh-variable-bank reuse without an equality-factor-local reset, bank-backed `TOGreater` side check and post-unifier maximality recheck for KBO6 Lambda-order beta/eta ordering preparation, generated negative condition, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization of generated equality-factor literal lists before cleanup, Horn and `CPNoGeneration` gates, multi-CSU stack-pop insertion order, clause-set insertion, parent metadata propagation, ordinary and higher-order `DCEqFactor` derivation entries, and opt-in represented `inf_efactor` proof-documentation output.

Pending:

- Broader C trace coverage for multi-CSU equality-factor order, proof-documentation streams, and performance.

Change-later notes:

- C equality factoring pushes CSU results to a stack and the wrapper later pops them, reversing multi-CSU insertion order. Rust mirrors this with a temporary vector and `pop`; preserve or intentionally change the reversal only after HO trace tests show proof output can change safely.
- C assigns `*subst_is_ho = SubstHasHOBinding(subst)` for each accepted equality factor, then the control wrapper applies the final flag value to every factor popped for that candidate. Rust mirrors this last-accepted-factor behavior even though per-factor metadata would be a cleaner API.
- Factoring has an asymmetric fresh-variable-bank boundary: `ComputeOrderedFactor` resets the caller-owned `freshvars` before each attempt, while `ComputeEqualityFactor` consumes the caller's current counts. Rust proof-control wrappers mirror that behavior; a future cleanup could make the reset policy explicit in the C API after proof traces confirm compatibility.

## Equality Resolution

Rust files:

- `src/clauses/eqnresolution.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_eqnresolution.c`
- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_eqnresolution.h`

Implemented:

- Single-result `ComputeEqRes` MGU behavior, including the higher-order problem-mode arrow-binding subset used by destructive equality resolution.
- `ComputeAllEqnResolvents` all-resolvent generation with first-order MGU fallback outside higher-order multi mode and higher-order `CsuIterator` enumeration inside higher-order mode, including global CSU-limit use, pattern-oracle CSU results, C stack-pop insertion order, aggregate `SubstHasHOBinding` propagation to higher-order `DCEqRes`, represented proof-documentation helpers for generated resolvents, and proof-state-owned `freshvars` reuse for proof-control generation.
- C-shaped non-selected literal substitution normalization, optimized copy excluding the resolved literal, DB-lambda beta/eta normalization of copied resolvents before cleanup, false-literal removal, duplicate removal, maximal negative-literal iteration, caller-owned clause-set insertion, and destructive equality-resolution normalization with `DCDesEqRes`/higher-order `DCDesEqRes` derivation tagging. Proof-control destructive ER now also passes the proof-state `freshvars` bank through the helper path; standalone unit helpers keep a scratch fresh-normalization bank for isolated callers.

Pending:

- Broader C trace coverage for multi-CSU equality-resolution order/performance.

Change-later notes:

- C `ComputeEqRes` switches between returning one clause and filling a result stack based on whether `res_cls` is NULL. Rust keeps separate helpers so the API does not depend on a null stack argument.
- Standalone Rust `ComputeEqRes` helpers still build a scratch fresh-normalization bank so tests and non-proof-state callers do not need a paired proof-state owner. Proof-control call sites use the proof-state-owned `freshvars` bank and reset variable counts at the C `ComputeEqRes` boundary.
- C pushes higher-order CSU resolvents to `res_cls` and `ComputeAllEqnResolvents` pops that stack before insertion, reversing enumeration order. Rust mirrors this with a temporary vector and `pop`; revisit only if reference traces show cleanup can change proof output safely.
- C aggregates `subst_is_ho` over all CSU results for one selected literal before derivation entries are pushed, so one higher-order binding marks every resolvent popped from that literal as higher-order. Rust mirrors that aggregate flag even though a per-resolvent flag would be a cleaner internal API.

- Proof-control integration for selected-clause generation through the plain, simultaneous, and super-simultaneous unindexed paths, including unconditional, oriented-source, order-decreasing, and size-decreasing simultaneous mode selection, negative-unit strategy gating, `paramod_count` updates, and opt-in paramodulation creation docs.
- Proof-control selected-clause generation through caller-owned global paramodulation indexes, including C's indexed-when-available branch shape, fallback to the unindexed processed-set scan when PM indexes are absent, and opt-in paramodulation creation docs for the caller-owned indexed path.
- `ProcessClause` and `Saturate` integration through caller-owned global paramodulation indexes, preserving C's processed-survivor global-index insertion before watchlist simplification and selected-clause generation, carrying that indexed branch through the outer saturation loop, and deleting caller-owned global-index entries before reset, move-to-tmp, or backward-simplification movement removes processed clauses.
- Supported executable proof search now builds configured caller-owned global indices after `ProofStateInit`, using a cloned initialized signature and the parsed problem type so represented higher-order runs allocate ExtSup extension indexes with the configured `ext_rules_max_depth`, and drives presaturation, `ProofStateResetProcessed`, and main saturation through the indexed path for first-order and represented higher-order runs.

Pending:

- Full state-owned indexed paramodulation once a proof-session owner can hold global indices together with the mutable term bank and live signature without the cloned-signature executable bridge.
- Remaining higher-order paramodulation work: full LFHO complete-MGU/pattern coverage at every legacy direct-MGU call site beyond the newly bank-aware CSU single-mode shortcut, real higher-order unification constraints if C fills the current `CheckHOUnificationConstraints` stub, broader lambda/DB/phony-application ordering and normalization surfaces outside the bank-backed LPO4/KBO6 paths, executable proof-documentation coverage for higher-order generated clauses, and state-owned proof-control index ownership.
- The optional C `check_paramod_ordering_constraint` path remains disabled in C and unported in Rust.

Change-later notes:

- The C implementation encodes inference provenance through mutable literal flags (`EPIsPMIntoLit`, `EPFromClauseLit`) that selection heuristics later inspect. Rust currently preserves those flags for compatibility; a typed generated-literal metadata layer may be clearer after proof reconstruction is stable.
- Simultaneous paramodulation uses `TPPotentialParamod` as a mutable marker on shared terms to suppress duplicate rewrites after the first successful all-occurrences replacement. Rust preserves that C-shaped state; a future per-inference marker table would be less fragile once compatibility and benchmark baselines are fixed.
- Fresh-variable-bank seeding is duplicated with factoring for now. It should become a shared helper once clause ownership and inference wrapper APIs settle.
- C `ComputeAllParamodulantsIndexed` does not repeat the unindexed wrapper's explicit `CPNoGeneration` parent/candidate gate. Rust mirrors that indexed shape for compatibility; verify whether globally indexed `CPNoGeneration` clauses can occur before deciding whether this is a C bug worth cleaning up later.
- Rust can test and drive the indexed wrapper with caller-owned indexes over a cloned signature, including through selected-clause generation, `ProcessClause`, and `Saturate`; the supported executable path uses that bridge for first-order clause-list proof search, while full proof-control still needs a proof-session owner that can hold global indexes tied to the live term-bank signature while allowing generated clauses to mutate the term bank.
- C global indexes use live clause pointer identity, so Rust must delete entries while a clause is still in its processed set; deleting after extraction would not match the stored pointer-shaped key. Keep this C-compatible lifecycle until processed clauses have stable Rust handles.
- C `GlobalIndicesInsertClauseSet` returns before inserting into any configured index unless `bw_rw_index` exists, so PM-only and higher-order extension-only whole-set insertion are no-ops even though individual clause insertion would populate those indexes. Rust preserves this gate for watchlist/set insertion compatibility; a later state-owned index owner should decide whether to expose a cleaned all-configured-index set insertion helper.
- C indexed order-decreasing simultaneous mode decides `sim_paramod_q` while the unifier's bindings are active; Rust mirrors that for indexed mode selection, but the extra trial MGU should be benchmarked once proof-control can drive the indexed path.
- C indexed paramodulation leaves each CSU binding active while source/target order checks and `ClauseParamodConstruct` run, then the plain/simultaneous/super-simultaneous constructors allocate a fresh temporary `Subst` only for normalization and fresh-variable bookkeeping. Rust mirrors this with an explicit active `Substitution` plus a local fresh bank; a later API could make the active-unifier/normalization split a typed constructor input.
- C indexed paramodulation assigns `pminfo->subst_is_ho` for each CSU element and sets the higher-order derivation bit on every child built under that element. Rust mirrors this per-CSU tagging; if proof reconstruction later needs selected-binding provenance, represent the selected CSU explicitly instead of only a derived operation bit.
- C indexed `ClausePlainParamodConstruct` does not set `EPFromClauseLit` or `EPIsPMIntoLit`, unlike the unindexed ordered constructor. Rust preserves that indexed/plain distinction; revisit once selection and proof-reconstruction tests can prove whether the missing flags are intentional or accidental.
- C indexed `Clause*ParamodConstruct` runs `EqnListLambdaNormalize` on generated lists, while unindexed ordered `ClauseOrdered*Paramod` skips lambda normalization before resolved/duplicate cleanup. Rust preserves that split; a later cleaned inference API should normalize only after reference traces prove the search-order and proof-output effects are unobservable.
- C's non-equational predicate paramodulation keeps using side-level cursors, so `p(a) = $true` contributes both `p(a)` and `$true` as source sides even though only the predicate side can usefully overlap a negative atom. Rust now mirrors this for compatibility; after parity, an explicit predicate-resolution path could skip the dead `$true` source candidate while preserving candidate order where it matters.
- C dispatches ordinary paramodulation after `ComputeHOInferences` even in higher-order problem mode, relying on lower LFHO unification and ordering code to handle any higher-order surfaces. Rust now uses explicit KBO6-supported CSU enumeration for indexed paramodulation and a KBO6 first-order-shaped unindexed subset that permits arrow-typed selected-variable bindings while still rejecting lambda/DB/phony selected surfaces; revisit this compatibility boundary after full LFHO ordering and remaining CSU call sites are ported.
- C `NextCSUElement` uses `SubstMguComplete` as a single-unifier shortcut even in higher-order mode, relying on that wrapper to dispatch to `SubstComputeMguHO` through term-owned bank metadata. Rust now preserves the shortcut through an explicit-bank wrapper; once term ownership is no longer transitional, make the unification context explicit across all direct MGU/match call sites rather than mixing banked and unbanked wrappers.

## Disequality Decomposition

Rust files:

- `src/clauses/diseq_decomp.rs`

C source references:

- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_diseq_decomp.c`
- `eprover/CLAUSES/ccl_diseq_decomp.h`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_diseq_decomp.c`
- `eprover/CONTROL/cco_diseq_decomp.h`

Implemented:

- `ClauseDisEqDecomposition`, including compact-position selection, residual literal copy, reversed argument-pair disequality generation, resolved/duplicate cleanup, and `DCDisEqDecompose` derivation metadata.
- `ComputeDisEqDecompositions`, including the literal-count threshold, negative equational top-symbol/arity gates, max-arity gate, clause-set insertion, and generated count.

Proof-control integration:

- `generate_new_clauses` now calls the Rust higher-order ArgCong, NegExt, PosExt, inverse-recognition, Leibniz-elimination, PrimitiveEnumeration, and choice-instantiation helpers before first-order generators and the disequality-decomposition helper when C's literal-count and max-arity gates allow it. Caller-owned HO extension indexes can drive indexed ExtSup plus ExtEqRes/ExtEqFact for the grouped extensional-rule branch. Plain, simultaneous, and super-simultaneous first-order unindexed paramodulation, plus first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap paramodulation that can bind arrow-typed selected variables and copy unrelated applied-variable literals, are wired after disequality decomposition with opt-in creation docs; explicit caller-owned global-index variants can drive indexed paramodulation from selected-clause generation, `ProcessClause`, and `Saturate`, and supported executable proof search uses configured caller-owned global indexes with the parsed problem type; remaining full state-owned indexed generation branches remain pending.

Change-later notes:

- C builds disequality-decomposition argument pairs by pushing each generated negative literal at the front, so the final generated clause lists decomposed arguments in reverse source-argument order. Rust preserves that order for proof and output compatibility; a later cleaned API should separate user-facing clause order from the C construction shortcut only after reference traces allow it.
- The literal-count and max-arity gates are spread across the C control wrapper and helper-level assertions. Rust keeps those gates at the staged proof-control boundary for now; once proof-control owns all generation settings, expose them as typed configuration instead of relying on callers to reproduce C preconditions.
- Inverse-recognition proof metadata is attached inside C `ClauseRecognizeInjectivity`, while `InferInjectiveDefinition` only inserts the returned clause and the C proof-documentation call is still a TODO. Rust mirrors that placement and absence; revisit the API boundary when proof-document output is owned separately from clause construction.
- PrimitiveEnumeration keeps C's per-clause applied-variable head de-duplication, unconditional true/false substitutions, pointer-identity ordering for distinct Eq-mode return types, and post-`DCPrimEnum` Boolean simplification. Revisit these only after higher-order trace comparison shows which details are proof-search policy and which are construction artifacts.

- Supported `eprover` proof-search/CNF/prune input now keeps parsed formula owners in `ProofState::f_axioms`, applies represented formula SInE/relevance pruning before CNF, emits represented initial formula/preprocessing/CNF proof-document output at level two, archives formula sources, drains generated clauses into the clause axioms, and avoids duplicating those split clauses as ordinary initial clause docs where the post-CNF derivation survives clausal preprocessing. - Supported TSTP formula input now mutates the run problem type at record boundaries like C `WFormulaTSTPParse`, matching its higher-order context for `thf(...)` bodies and its global first-order/higher-order mixing rejection. This accepts THF bare arrow-typed term equality definitions such as `thf(def, definition, f = g).` while rejecting mixed FO/HO records before the represented-owner parser bridge lowers them.

Old-TPTP `input_formula(...)` entries in supported formula-owner executable paths now also use represented `TFormulaTPTPParse` owners, preserving C's old-dialect formula-level `=`/`!=` spelling in `--app-encode` and `--print-formulas` output and clausifying those formulas as equality literals instead of TSTP-style equivalence/XOR clauses. Non-`$distinct` TSTP `tff(...)`/`tcf(...)`/`thf(...)` app-encode formula entries now route through represented formula owners too, with `tcf(...)` bodies using the staged `TcfTSTPParse` helper before `FormulaSetAppEncode`. bridge until those compatibility details are represented explicitly.
- Regression coverage now also pins the unparenthesized top-level spelling for non-Boolean FOOL term equality/disequality with the FOOL term on either side, including `$ite(p(a),a,b) = c`, `$let(f:$i,f:=a,f) = b`, `$ite(p(a),a,b) != c`, `$let(f:$i,f:=a,f) != b`, `c = $let(f:$i,f:=a,f)`, `c != $let(f:$i,f:=a,f)`, and parameterized `$let` definitions with formal variables such as `$let(f:$i>$i, f(X):=X, f(a)) = b`, `$let(f:$i>$i, f(X):=X, f(a)) != b`, `c = $let(f:$i>$i, f(X):=X, f(a))`, and `c != $let(f:$i>$i, f(X):=X, f(a))`, through syntax-only, print-formulas, app-encode, and CNF represented-owner paths where applicable. This follows C's shared term-parser/equality path; a later full formula owner should keep source spelling, let-local formal binding, and typed term equality/disequality semantics separate instead of depending on parenthesis-sensitive parser entry points.
- Supported TSTP term-formula parsing now also accepts scoped quantified variables as non-Boolean `$ite` branches and `$let` definition RHS/body terms, recovers newly introduced untyped zero-arity `$ite` branch atoms as default individual constants when the sibling branch is already an individual term, and uses a known non-Boolean argument sort to recover fresh encoded compound branch atoms as term-valued functions. Executable formula-owner paths therefore accept C-shaped inputs such as `![X]:p($ite(q(a),X,a))`, `![X]:p($let(f:$i,f:=X,f))`, `p($ite(q(a),a,b))`, `$ite(q(a),a,b) = c`, and `tff(p_type,type,p:$i>$o). fof(arg,axiom,p($ite(q(a),f(a),b))).` Focused regression coverage now pins typed term-position FOOL arguments through `--print-formulas` and `--cnf`, preserving the C-shaped dependence on prior TFF type declarations for these predicate-argument parses.
- The supported `--app-encode` executable path now applies the same auto-mode preprocessing selection, represented proof-state SInE, and relevance-pruning phases before `FormulaSetAppEncode` that C runs before its app-encode branch. Focused coverage pins `--auto --sine=NoSInE` emitting the generated preprocessing class/configuration before rendering, `--sine=Threshold(1)` removing app-encode formula owners before rendering, and `--rel-pruning-level` filtering represented formula owners by conjecture reachability before rendering.
- Helper executables that parse real problems into formula sets for later CNF now request CNF-oriented represented-owner handling instead of print-oriented handling, so supported FOOL/lambda owners promote the returned problem type before helper clausification. Focused coverage now pins `classify_problem`, `eground`, `enormalizer`, and `epatternize` on typed TSTP formula owners with term-position `$let` payloads, and `TFormulaVarRename` copies term-valued `$let` definition children as terms rather than trying to rebuild them as formula operators.
- `enormalizer` formula targets and LTB batch problem loading now share the same wrapper-aware represented top-level `$distinct(...)` parser as `eprover`, so direct, parenthesized, and top-level negated `$distinct` formula entries are accepted consistently while embedded TCF `$distinct` remains on the C-shaped clause-parser path.
- Nonempty embedded `$distinct(...)` formulas in represented FOF/TFF/THF owners, such as `p(a) | $distinct(a,b,c)` and `p @ a | $distinct(a,b,c)`, are now pinned through executable syntax-only, print-formulas, app-encode, and CNF paths; embedded TCF `$distinct` remains intentionally on the C-shaped typed-clause parser diagnostic path.
- Direct `TFormulaExpandDistinct` empty and singleton helper expansion now returns `$true` like C's `TFormulaStackToForm`; executable `$distinct()` syntax remains rejected like C because `TSTPDistinctParse` requires a first constant argument before the comma loop.
- Singleton `$distinct(a)` expansion to `$true` is now pinned for `--app-encode`: top-level singleton truth formulas are skipped like other top-level `$true` formulas, while embedded singleton expansions such as `p(a) | $distinct(a)` render as `p(a)|$true` instead of panicking during declaration preloading.

The app-encode include path now preserves C's `ignore_include` behavior for supported TSTP and old-TPTP inputs: bare includes are echoed to stdout without loading, and selector-bearing includes are rejected.

Change-later notes:

- C represents `FormulaSet` as an intrusive doubly linked list by mutating `set`, `pred`, and `succ` fields inside each `WFormula`. Rust currently keeps formulas by value in an owned vector with stable entry ids; revisit arena/stable-handle ownership and O(1) front extraction when executable formula archives need C-like pointer identity or list performance.
- C `WFormulaGetId` combines a process-global generated-id counter with the mutable `FormulasKeepInputNames` global. Rust exposes input-name preservation as an explicit argument for the staged helper; decide later whether executable compatibility needs a global policy shim or whether call sites can stay explicit.
- C `WFormulaPrint` dispatches through the process-global `OutputFormat`, and the LOP case emits a warning before intentionally falling through to TPTP output. Rust uses an explicit `FormulaPrintFormat` for staged deterministic rendering; executable integration must decide where to reproduce the warning and global dispatch.
- C `WFormClauseToClause` allocates a fresh `Clause` from the formula payload, copies wrapper properties and source info, but does not preserve the wrapper id as the printed clause id. Rust mirrors that staged behavior; decide during proof-state integration whether clause/formula conversion should preserve a stable wrapper handle separately from generated clause ids.
- C `WFormulaOfClause` closes the encoded clause into a fresh formula wrapper but does not copy clause properties or source info. Rust mirrors that metadata drop; a later proof-document owner should decide whether compatibility output needs separate parent metadata rather than overloading the new wrapper.
- C `WFormulaTSTPPrintFlex` prints `as_formula=false` clause wrappers through `ClauseTSTPCorePrint`; typed first-order wrappers get a `tcf(...)` header but still use the raw clause core instead of `ClauseTSTPPrint`'s universally closed typed clause body. Rust mirrors the split; revisit only with reference output coverage because changing it affects proof/document text.
- C `WFormulaTSTPParse` mutates the process-global problem type before parsing each TSTP formula wrapper, so it both selects higher-order type checking for `thf(...)` and enforces the global FO/HO mixing error. Rust preserves that parser-side rejection in the executable bridge now; a later cleaned parser/session API should carry problem type explicitly without making ordinary parsing depend on hidden process-global mutation.
- C's `$distinct` formula-owner surface is split between `TSTPDistinctParse` for the pseudo-formula itself and caller-side wrapper/negation handling before later `$distinct` expansion. Rust now centralizes that top-level helper for represented owners; a later full formula parser should model `$distinct` as a typed pseudo-formula node instead of leaving each executable/helper path to recognize spellings independently.
- C `FormulaSetSimplify` interleaves formula mutation, optional term-bank GC checks, `DocFormulaModificationDefault`, and `DCFofSimplify` derivation pushes. Rust now keeps the plain staged simplifier output-free and exposes an explicit documenting wrapper that mirrors proof id/property mutation plus simplification output for changed formulas; the supported proof-search/CNF path chooses it through `clausify_formula_axioms_with_docs`, while helper and future full-parser call sites still need to opt in deliberately.
- C's `WFormulaConjectureNegate` comment says it sets `WPInitialConjecture`, but this checkout has no `WPInitialConjecture` symbol and the implementation only negates the formula, changes the role to `CPTypeNegConjecture`, documents the modification, and pushes `DCNegateConjecture`. Rust follows the executable code path and treats the comment as stale until a different upstream source proves otherwise.
- C question/conjecture preprocessing interleaves `WFormulaAnnotateQuestion`, `WFormulaConjectureNegate`, formula derivation pushes, proof-document output, and a `question_assoc` parameter. In this source revision the parameter is threaded through the annotate-question calls but not populated by `ccl_formulafunc.c`; Rust now stages the formula mutation, stores `DCAnnoQuestion`/`DCNegateConjecture` on changed wrappers, returns the opcodes as result metadata, and exposes an explicit proof-documenting wrapper that mirrors C proof-id/property mutation and is wired into the supported executable formula preprocessing path. Future upstream answer-skolem association remains owner work.
- C `WFormulaSetUnrollFOOL` records `DCEqToEq` for the Boolean-equality replacement phase but returns/counts only formulas changed by the later `do_fool_unroll` mapper; literal expansion can still mutate a formula without `DCFoolUnroll`, and this phase does not call `DocFormulaModificationDefault`. Rust now preserves that split with separate counters/result opcodes and formula-owned `DCEqToEq`/`DCFoolUnroll` entries while keeping formula proof output absent for this phase.
- C `TFormulaSetIntroduceDefs` reuses one `NumXTree` cell for polarity, virtual definition id, real blocking definition id, and archived neutral-definition pointer while interleaving formula creation docs, `DCIntroDef`/`DCFofQuote`/`DCSplitEquiv`, definition-application docs, and later `DCApplyDef` derivations. Rust now uses explicit staged definition entries, preserves archive/active insertion order, stores formula-owned `DCIntroDef`/`DCFofQuote`/`DCSplitEquiv`/`DCApplyDef` derivations with archived-definition refs where those parents are representable, exposes explicit represented proof-documenting definition output, and still exposes opcode metadata; exact pointer-stable formula ownership remains deferred.
- C `TFormulaSetDelTermpProp` silently skips wrappers with a null `tformula` while clearing the requested term flags recursively with `DEREF_NEVER`. Rust exposes this as a no-op for staged default wrappers; revisit only if executable formula-set insertion policy must assert on formula-less wrappers.
- C `TFormulaSetNamedToDBLambdas` calls `NamedToDB` over every higher-order formula even though the lower-level conversion expects named-lambda cells when it sees lambda syntax. Rust guards DB-lambda-only formulas as no-ops before calling the named-lambda converter, preserving the intended set phase while avoiding a staged-owner panic before the later DB-lambda normalization phase, and exposes the C proof-documenting simplification wrapper.
- C `FormulaSetArchive` moves originals to the archive and puts flat copies back into the source set, with `DCFofQuote` pushed only on the replacement copy. Rust preserves the movement/replacement order, exposes quote-source metadata, and stores the replacement-copy formula derivation stack entry.
- C `FormulaSetDocInital` uses the misspelled helper name, level-gates at `OutputLevel >= 2`, and assigns fresh formula proof-document ids while printing initial formula docs. Rust now mirrors the level gate, insertion order, and id assignment through `ProofDocSession` and represented formula views, and the supported `eprover` proof-search/CNF/prune path wires this into parsing/preprocessing; non-printing `--syntax-only` shares the represented parser owners but exits before proof documentation and CNF. Helper/full-parser call-site ordering still needs deliberate integration.
- C `WFormulaCNF2` mutates the wrapper formula, documents each changed CNF phase, then documents clauses split from the final formula before pushing `DCSplitConjunct`; its direct clause-wrapper shortcut only pushes `DCFofQuote`. Rust now stores those formula-owned derivation entries, exposes them in the staged result, provides explicit represented formula-phase and split-clause proof-documenting output for formula-backed wrappers and the set-level CNF drain, and routes supported `eprover` proof-search/CNF/prune formula owners through it; non-printing `--syntax-only` shares the parser owner but deliberately exits before this CNF phase, and helper routing remains owner work.
- C `TFormulaSetLiftItes` uses `map_formula`, `do_ite_unroll`, `TermFindIteSubterm`, and term-position replacement helpers over shared term-bank cells. Rust now mirrors formula-position ITE expansion, literal-side term-position ITE expansion, left-before-right literal scanning, higher-order gating, and `DCLiftIte` wrapper derivations/result metadata, but app-flattening behavior, lambda-bound subterm cases, and deeper formula-owner interactions should still be audited against C reference traces before deciding whether those C implementation details should be preserved or cleaned up.
- C `TFormulaSetLiftLets` interleaves quantified-variable renaming, f-code-keyed local-definition replacement, fresh symbol allocation over captured and formal variables, Boolean-definition equivalence construction, root `unencode_eqns`, stack-reversed insertion of generated definitions, and `DCIntroDef`/`DCApplyDef` provenance updates. Rust now mirrors that behavior in the staged formula-set owner, including generated-definition `DCIntroDef` entries and source-formula `DCApplyDef` parents, but reference traces should still cover nested lets, captured variable order, app flattening, and proof-document side effects before deciding whether C's f-code-keyed replacement map is a compatibility requirement or just an implementation shortcut.
- C `lift_lambda` gives each generated definition its own `DCIntroDef`, and `TFormulaSetLiftLambdas` also calls `WFormulaPushDerivation(form, DCIntroDef, def, NULL)` on each rewritten source formula despite `DCIntroDef` carrying no formula-argument flag, with a commented-out `DCApplyDef` alternative beside it. Rust now stages both metadata effects while validating the no-parent source-formula `DCIntroDef` entries and seeding `VarBank` counts before fresh variable generation; a cleaned proof API should eventually separate definition creation from source-formula rewrite provenance, but only after reference proof-output and numbering traces decide whether this duplicated intro-def shape is externally visible.
- C `TFormulaSetUnfoldDefSymbols` recognizes only `CPIsLambdaDef` wrappers, strips leading universal quantifiers, accepts equation and predicate-equivalence definition shapes, uses a symbol f-code map, freshens lambda binders before applying both sides, temporarily marks definition arguments as special variables, rejects self-recursive right sides and definitions with remaining free variables, intersimplifies generated definitions, recursively rewrites formulas with a 500-step counter, inserts generated flat-copy definitions into the archive, and then extracts the recognized original definitions into the archive. Rust now mirrors those staged owner effects, including generated-definition `DCFofQuote` entries, `DCApplyDef` parent entries on rewritten generated definitions and source formulas, C's source-formula proof-documenting simplification output, the post-recursion rewrite-counter decrement shape, `unfold_only_forms` predicate gate, and duplicate f-code last-definition overwrite behavior; exact pointer-level temporary special-variable flag side effects and executable formula/archive proof-session ownership should stay visible until reference traces decide what must remain compatibility behavior.
- C `ClauseSetLiftLambdas` decodes formula-shaped clause terms, calls `LiftLambdas`, pushes `DCLiftLambdas` with each generated definition, archives the generated definition formula, optionally FOOL-unrolls and simplifies a flat copy, clausifies that copy back into the same clause set, and does not add those definition clauses to `FormulaSetCNF2`'s returned clause count. Rust now stages those observable post-CNF side effects and reuses exact and generalized closed lambda-body liftings across formula-set and post-CNF clause lifting while preserving per-occurrence derivation entries. C's `find_generalization` uses an indexed `PDTree`; Rust's staged owner currently uses deterministic insertion-order scanning, so the exact index structure remains a performance and trace-order item to revisit.
- C `FormulaSetCNF2` first runs the higher-order named-to-DB, ITE lifting, LET lifting, definition-symbol unfolding, and optional lambda-to-forall normalization phases before FOOL unrolling, simplification, definition introduction, archive movement, clausification, optional post-CNF clause lambda lifting, and C-thresholded GC. Rust now stages named-to-DB, ITE lifting, LET lifting, definition-symbol unfolding, lambda-to-forall normalization, and post-CNF clause lambda lifting in those positions, preserves the archive order, stores represented formula-owned derivation entries for those phases, exposes quote sources, reports CNF-path GC collections/recovered terms, exposes represented set-level proof-document output, and routes supported `eprover` proof-search/CNF/prune formula owners through that pipeline; non-printing `--syntax-only` shares represented input ownership but stops before preprocessing/CNF, and helper routing remains owner work.
- C `FormulaSetPrettyPrintTSTP` discovers the signature through the first formula's term bank and prints all known type declarations when the set is not fully untyped. Rust takes the bank explicitly while preserving declaration-before-formula ordering; decide during executable integration whether first-entry signature coupling and declaration leakage from unrelated symbols must be emulated.
- C `WFormulaGCMarkCells` ultimately asserts on null term-bank/formula inputs through `TBGCMarkTerm`, even though the wrapper comment describes non-term formulas as a no-op. Rust treats a staged `WrappedFormula` without a formula term as a no-op during GC marking so default allocation remains useful before insertion policy is finalized; revisit if C-compatible assertion behavior becomes observable.
- C `WFormulaMarkPolarity` has the same assumed non-null formula shape as GC marking. Rust currently treats a staged wrapper without a formula term as a no-op during set-level polarity marking; revisit alongside insertion policy and exact assertion compatibility.
- C `WFormulaGetLambdaDefinedSym` accepts an `equiv(eqn(lhs, rhs), ...)` lambda-definition shape without checking that `rhs` is `$true`, but `FormulaSetDefinitionStatistics` only counts that predicate-definition shape when `rhs` is literally the shared `$true` term. Rust mirrors both shapes in their respective helper paths; keep this mismatch visible when connecting formula statistics to proof-state feature extraction.
- C `FormulaSetDefinitionStatistics` writes `int` counters through caller pointers. Rust returns a typed statistics struct and saturates the staged `i32` counters; decide during proof-state feature integration whether executable compatibility needs exact C-width behavior or wider Rust feature counters.

Proof-found and stopped/saturation list output now expands represented mixed clause/formula ancestors before children that cite them, emits formula labels in PCL/TSTP list output, and uses display-only sequential ids with represented clause-parent, formula-parent, rewrite-demodulator, and signature AC axiom parent references remapped for output. Proof-object statistics now follow represented formula-parent edges and signature AC axiom parents, including formula quote cascades and parents resolved from active formulas, the formula axiom archive, the definition formula archive, and the general formula archive, and count formula steps, formula conjectures, and initial formulas. The represented proof-object graph now keeps formula nodes, mixed clause/formula edges, definition-archive and `f_archive` formula-parent nodes, recorded formula roots, and signature AC axiom clause-parent edges; list/DOT output share a C-shaped root-backward mixed display order with separate axiom-stack reversal and C's clause-parent/formula-parent stack-pop order for direct parents, and represented TSTP proof quote/list output plus detailed list/DOT clause/formula labels thread the parsed proof problem type so higher-order proof objects keep `thf(...)`.

Pending:

- Stable clause/formula parent handles instead of compact `ident`/source references.
- Stable proof-state ownership for signature-owned AC axiom parent references.
- Ordered proof-object extraction over mixed clause/formula nodes, reference-verified C sibling ordering/interleaving and renumbering, and full PCL/TSTP derived-step printing over ordered clause/formula derivations.

Change-later notes:

- C stores raw `Clause_p` and `WFormula_p` parent pointers in derivation stacks. Rust stores compact clause identifiers paired with source tags plus compact formula identifiers for current proof-depth, inference-count, and derivation-rendering behavior, but proof reconstruction should move to stable clause/formula handles before duplicate identifiers, archived copies, and requeued clauses become harder to distinguish.
- C `WFormulaGetId` can preserve source formula names through the mutable `FormulasKeepInputNames` global, while Rust represented formula derivation refs currently render only generated `c_0_...`/`i_0_...` ids. Revisit named formula ids when real `WFormula` ownership and formula source-name policy are ported.
- C `ClausePushACResDerivation` records only `PStackGetSP(sig->ac_axioms)` rather than the AC axiom parents themselves. Rust now records compact AC axiom refs on `Signature` and expands them for context-aware derivation rendering, represented proof-object marking/statistics/graph traversal, and display-id remapping, but the derivation push still takes the C count explicitly; prefer stable proof-state parent handles once full proof-object traversal owns clauses.
- C `ClauseDerivFindFirst` and `WFormulaDerivFindFirst` follow raw dummy-quote parent pointers without a cycle or shape guard beyond the dummy-quote predicates. Rust exposes the same cascade helpers through caller-resolved compact clause/formula references and stops on missing, self, or cyclic parents because compact identifiers remain a transitional stand-in for pointer-stable handles; proof-object analysis still collapses only literal-identical dummy clause quotes until stable ownership can distinguish all archived copies.
- C `DerivStackExtractOptParents` mutates raw parent pointers in the derivation stack after chasing dummy-quote parents. Rust exposes represented in-place replacement through caller-resolved compact clause/formula references, while keeping rewrite-demodulator handles and expanded `DCACRes` parents in their existing represented form; replace the resolver API with stable proof-state handles when pointer-like derivation identity is available.
- C `DerivedCell` stores either a raw clause pointer or formula pointer plus graph metadata. Rust currently exposes borrowed derived-step views over represented clauses/formulas for the ported proof/eval predicates, but does not yet model ref counts, root/fresh flags, or ordered derivation-graph ownership; keep the borrowed view narrow until full proof extraction owns stable graph nodes.
- C proof-graph colours are raw GraphViz attribute strings chosen from proof flags, raw derivation-stack presence, clause/formula role, and parent proof flags. Rust now centralizes the same strings and rules for represented borrowed clauses/formulas; the current borrowed executable graph treats extracted graph members as proof nodes without mutating proof flags and emits mixed formula/clause DOT nodes using the shared C-shaped proof-object display order, while exact parent-proof link-colour checks and reference-verified C sibling ordering remain tied to the future ordered graph owner.
- C proof-object quote, list, and graph rendering inherit the process-global `problemType` through `ClauseTSTPPrint` and formula TSTP printers, so higher-order proof runs render relevant clauses and formulas with `thf(...)` rather than first-order wrappers. Rust now threads the parsed proof problem type into represented proof quote/list/DOT renderers; a cleaned proof-output API should keep dialect metadata explicit on the rendering request.
- C ordinary clause proof-documentation sessions inherit the process-global `problemType` for TSTP clause creation output. Rust now normalizes the current parsed problem type for executable clause initial-document sessions; a cleaned proof-documentation API should pass dialect state explicitly into every session instead of reading global state.
- C proof-object analysis and rendering run over the ordered mixed clause/formula derivation graph. Rust now resolves compact formula-parent refs for statistics, retains formula nodes/mixed edges in the represented graph, and emits formula nodes in list/DOT output using one C-shaped display order, but pointer-backed mixed-node ownership and reference-verified root/sibling ordering still wait on the ordered graph owner.
- C's final proof-object list display order is tied to its pointer-backed extraction/traversal and display-id assignment. Rust now mirrors the root-backward topological walk, separate axiom stack, and direct parent stack-pop order over represented nodes, but exact C root/sibling ordering and formula/clause interleaving should be revisited with reference proof-output traces before treating the represented compact graph as fully compatible.
- C proof-documentation pushes are interleaved with inference construction, simplification, archive movement, and global output. Rust now exposes represented opt-in proof-documentation wrappers and threads them through supported executable initialization/saturation/pre-saturation reset when proof output is enabled; the eventual proof object layer should keep metadata creation separate from rendering so compatibility shims can reproduce C streams without making them the core API.

- Core `CSSCPAProcessClause` behavior over current Rust clause sets: forced acceptance, tautology rejection, positive/negative unit and non-unit subsumption rejection, C's unit-subsumption fast path when collecting clauses subsumed by a unit, improvement by removed-weight and average-weight gates, opposite-sign unit contradiction detection, subsumed-clause removal, CSSCPA source preservation, accepted/rejected status return, and numeric `OutputLevel`-style trace gating, including C's split between truthy `if(OutputLevel)` traces and the `OUTPRINT(1)` unit-contradiction banner.
- `CSSCPALoop` command parsing as an explicit output-capturing Rust API, including numeric `output_level` persistence with C's only-0-or-1 mutation rule, ungated `state:` state-line output, the exact buffering-plea no-op sequence, `accept`/`check` dispatch, optional `from` source validation in the C `2..=15` range, optional `improve(weight_delta, average_delta)` parsing through the scanner float parser, current scanner-format clause parsing, CSSCPA source tagging, process-clause dispatch/result counting, and trace flush-boundary metadata for C's `state:` and per-clause flush points.
- Standalone `CSSCPA_filter` binary integration, including exact C-shaped full help text through the shared option renderer, version handling, verbose/output-file/silent/output-level/rant options, C's `--output-level` rejection only above 1 with negative values preserved as truthy-but-below-level-1 output, rant `stderr` side effects, default stdin input through `-`, sequential file processing over one CSSCPA state, C source-confirmed `TSTPFormat` scanner setup with a compatibility bridge for historical old `input_clause(...)` clauses, output-level persistence across files, C-compatible replay of CSSCPA state/process-clause trace flush points, final positive-unit/negative-unit/non-unit TSTP clause-set printing, C `SysError`-style two-line input/output file-open diagnostics, C `OutClose` output-stream error wording on final flush failure, testable explicit output routing, and WSL compare-tool coverage for help/version output plus a small silent stdin accept workload.

Pending:

- Expanded byte-for-byte `CSSCPA_filter` comparison against a built C executable remains pending for platform-specific system-error suffixes and non-silent trace-heavy state/check command streams.

Change-later notes:

- C keeps `terms` and `tmp_terms` as separate term banks over one shared mutable signature. Rust currently allocates a fresh tautology work bank from the live signature for each processed clause; revisit persistent scratch-bank reuse after shared signature ownership is available.
- C stores raw subsumed-clause pointers before later deletion. Rust records clause identifiers and the owning CSSCPA bucket because `ClauseSet` owns clauses by value; stable clause handles would make the deletion path closer to C and may matter if CSSCPA becomes performance-critical.
- C embeds `GlobalOut` printing and unconditional flushing inside `CSSCPAProcessClause`, including an extra flush from `print_csscpa_state` before the process-clause trailing flush. Rust keeps process-global output out of the core API by returning trace text plus flush offsets; future streaming or cleaned CSSCPA APIs should preserve these boundaries only in drop-in compatibility mode.
- C silently consumes unsupported `output_level` positive integers without changing the current output gate, treats negative CLI output levels as nonzero for `if(OutputLevel)` traces but below `OUTPRINT(1)`, and prints `state:` requests regardless of the gate. Rust preserves those numeric gate quirks; a later cleaned CLI could make unsupported levels and state-output policy explicit after compatibility runs are stable.
- C's filter uses process-global `GlobalOut`, while Rust routes output through explicit writers or a local output file. Keep the explicit API unless byte-compatible `OpenGlobalOut` side effects become observable.
- C scanner streams are lazy `FILE*` readers. Rust's filter currently reads stdin and files into scanner-owned memory before tokenization; revisit this if CSSCPA workloads expose large-input memory or latency differences.

Change-later notes:

- `EServerFree` and `EServerReset` are empty in the checked C source even though their comments promise cleanup. Rust keeps `reset` as a no-op for the compatibility surface while ordinary Rust drop ownership releases listener/session resources; decide later whether a cleaned server API should expose explicit draining/close semantics.
- `EServerInitFDSet` does not guard against `listening == -1` before `FD_SET(server->listening, rd_fds)`. Rust only registers a listener descriptor when one exists; preserve the invalid C call surface only in a low-level compatibility shim if a reference path depends on it.
- `ESessionProcessCmds` only prints `Received: ...` for queued messages and does not dispatch commands. Rust routes this through an explicit writer for testability; executable server integration should decide where the C direct-stdout behavior belongs.
- C `fd_set` handling and raw integer socket ownership are represented as safe descriptor-interest collection plus owned Rust sockets, and the legacy `e_server` executable uses safe nonblocking sockets to mirror the observed one-active-client loop. Keep this as an adapter boundary so future platform event loops can target exact `select` behavior or a cleaner poll abstraction without changing session state semantics.
- `AcceptAxiomSetName` accepts empty names and concatenates accepted token fragments across whitespace, with no special stop for command words such as `GO`, while `AxiomSetAlloc` ignores its `staged` parameter. Rust keeps both visible for now; a cleaned server API should validate names and remove dead allocation parameters after server compatibility tests exist.
- `get_directory_listings` returns `NULL` on open failure, leaks the preallocated stack in that branch, filters only `DT_REG` entries except on Solaris, and leaves output in raw directory iteration order for callers to pop. Rust preserves the visible open-failure, regular-file filtering, and unsorted stack-shaped order while avoiding the leak; sorted output and structured I/O diagnostics should wait until server-library compatibility tests cover `LIST` and `LOAD`.
- `remove_command` mutates the axiom-set stack even when it returns `ERR_AXIOM_SET_IS_STAGED_MESSAGE`: the staged target and any newer popped sets are not restored in C. Rust preserves that visible side effect for now; a later cleaned command API should make failed removal non-mutating after drop-in server tests are stable.
- `list_command` uses two different orderings: staged/unstaged memory sets print by increasing stack index, while disk files print by popping the directory-listing stack. Rust keeps that ordering split; sorted display should wait for a protocol-compatibility decision.
- `stage_command` aliases axiom-set clause/formula pointers into `StructFOFSpec` and updates `shared_ax_sp` without updating `shared_ax_f_count`. Rust currently clones the sets into the proof-control owner as a safe bridge and preserves the shared-boundary update; stable shared handles should replace the clone bridge once the full server owner model is available.
- `unstage_command` clears the axiom set's staged flag before proving that the control stack contains a matching problem set. Rust preserves that side effect; later command handling should become transactional only after compatibility tests cover desynchronized server state.
- `add_command` and `load_command` parse before duplicate-name rejection, and C routes both through a temporary-file scanner after loading text into a `DStr`. Rust preserves parse-before-duplicate and the no-default-include-directory shape with an in-memory scanner for the current parser bridge; revisit temp-file side effects, non-UTF-8 raw data, and include resolution when full interactive server tests exist.
- `StartDeductionServer` validates the command word and the consumed command argument, but does not reject trailing tokens after immediate commands or after the single `RUN` identifier. Rust preserves that dispatch shape; a cleaned protocol should reject extra tokens once byte-compatibility tests establish the current behavior.
- `RUN` captures only `AktToken(in)->literal` and then accepts one `Identifier`, unlike the broader `AcceptAxiomSetName` loop used by most axiom-set commands. Rust preserves this single-token job-name parser; a later protocol revision should use one explicit name grammar for all named commands.
- `ADD` and `RUN` read their data block from the transport after the command line has been parsed, using an exact `GO\n` terminator. Rust keeps that boundary through line-reader and TCP-message block adapters plus an injected block-reader dispatch hook; later APIs should keep block framing explicit rather than mixing command parsing with transport reads.
- `quit_command` pushes staged axiom-set names in increasing stack order and then pops that temporary stack, so cleanup unstages in reverse staged-stack order and ignores individual unstage status returns. Rust preserves the reverse cleanup order; a cleaned shutdown path should report or prevent inconsistent control-stack state after compatibility tests cover server close behavior.
- C `run_command` forks before parsing the uploaded job, prints the job name directly to stdout, sends start/finish messages through the server outstream from the child, then has the parent send `OK_SUCCESS_MESSAGE` after waiting. Rust currently stages the same logical parsing, `BatchProcessProblem` call, 30-second fallback limit, socket-style proof-output mirroring, captured global/stdout side-channel output, and success status synchronously over injected runner hooks; the exact fork boundary and parent/child output ordering still need live reference-server coverage.
- C `StartDeductionServer` sends one TCP string for each `print_to_outstream` call and sends no response for `QUIT`. Rust's socket-loop adapter preserves the non-empty-response shape for the already-staged command outputs, including no `QUIT` response, but final byte-level ordering around forked `RUN` child output still needs reference coverage.
- `e_client.c` speaks the older `e_server` protocol (`hello`, wait for `ready`, `add`, problem text, `prove`, wait for `result`) rather than the newer interactive deduction-server command set. Rust preserves that client handshake; unifying the client with `StartDeductionServer` should wait until both server executables have protocol compatibility tests.
- `e_client.c` echoes every received server message through `GlobalOut`, including the terminal `ready` and `result` messages, and opens `GlobalOut` before loading files or connecting. Rust keeps the echo and early output-file creation side effect, while routing through explicit writers.
- `e_client.c` inherits `InputOpen`'s stat-before-open diagnostics, so missing problem files report `Cannot stat file ...` and directories report `... it is not a regular file` before the final open branch. Rust preserves that split; a cleaned loader should prefer one structured open/read diagnostic after compatibility mode.
- `e_client.c` accepts privileged/reserved ports after printing a warning. Rust preserves acceptance and warning routing; a cleaned CLI should make this a structured warning after compatibility checks.
- `e_deduction_server.c` advertises stdin/stdout mode when `-p` is absent, but the reached `StartDeductionServer(..., stdout, -1)` path prints `e_deduction_server: Server mode not implemented yet for stdout` and exits without reading commands. Rust preserves that no-port message for the executable while keeping its text-session helper as an internal/test surface; a cleaned CLI should either implement stdin/stdout mode deliberately or require `-p`.
- `e_deduction_server.c` seeds `total_wtc_limit` with 30 before option parsing, so `-w 0` later disables the fallback rather than re-triggering it. Rust keeps that ordering; a cleaned configuration API should distinguish "unset" from an explicit zero limit.
- `e_deduction_server.c` has dead or misleading configuration surface: `outname` is opened but no option sets it, `app_encode` is unused in the file, and the enum still contains `OPT_PRINT_STATISTICS` without an option-table entry. Rust omits those dead surfaces for now; revisit only if reference executables expose them through linked code or generated help.
- `e_deduction_server.c` treats the first positional argument as the prover executable and ignores any later positional arguments even though usage says `[files]`. Rust preserves first-argument selection and ignores extras; a cleaned CLI should reject or repurpose extras once drop-in behavior is covered.
- `e_deduction_server.c` forks for every accepted TCP client and lets the child inherit a snapshot of the initialized batch/control state. Rust currently creates fresh term/control state for each accepted client but serves clients sequentially in one process; concurrent client handling should be added after subprocess cleanup, output ordering, and shared global-state boundaries are reference-tested.
- Help/version exit inside `process_options()` before C opens or closes `GlobalOut`, while successful execution reaches the final close. Rust preserves that split; a cleaned server API should expose explicit output ownership and shutdown semantics.
- `e_server.c` parses a domain spec and filter set but the checked service loop does not run the prover or use the parsed filters. It prints `Received: ...` and sends `wait` followed by `ready` for every TCP string, so it does not satisfy the `e_client.c` expectation of a terminal `result`. Rust preserves that placeholder protocol; a future cleaned legacy server should either implement the intended `result` workflow or clearly retire this executable after compatibility audits.
- `e_server.c` advertises `--tptp-in`, `--tptp-format`, `--tptp2-in`, and `--tptp2-format`, but `process_options` has no switch case for their option codes. In normal builds they leave the default TSTP parser unchanged; Rust preserves that no-op. A cleaned CLI should either implement real TPTP-2 parsing or remove the advertised aliases.
- `e_server.c` sends diagnostic loop text with `printf` to process stdout, while `--output-file` only affects `GlobalOut` users such as domain parsing. Rust keeps parse output separate from loop output; a later API should make this split explicit rather than depending on mixed global/stdout channels.
- `OutClose(GlobalOut)` is placed after the infinite legacy service loop in `e_server.c`, so normal server operation never reaches redirected-output close/error reporting. Rust flushes startup parse output before serving; a real server owner should define explicit shutdown and flush semantics.
- `e_server.c` accepts only one active connection and closes additional accepted sockets while the first is active. Rust now preserves that state-machine timing with a nonblocking listener/active stream bridge; live C reference tests should still decide whether exact `select` loop-marker cadence and platform-specific close diagnostics matter for this legacy server.

- `ECtrlCreateGeneric` builds one shell command by string concatenation without quoting `prover`, options, or file names. Rust preserves exact command construction and exposes opt-in shell-backed constructors for compatibility tests, but keeps structured process arguments as the default safe path; route production scheduler/server calls through shell execution only when reference traces require C `popen` behavior.
- `EPCtrlSetGetResult` ignores `select` errors, scans integer descriptors from `0..=maxfd`, and asserts on impossible result states. Rust keeps the deterministic polling behavior explicit; the later event-loop adapter should decide which of these artifacts are compatibility requirements.
- Rust's portable process-set timeout poll uses per-child reader threads and channels rather than blocking the main thread in OS `select`, because Windows child stdout handles are not `select`-compatible sockets. Keep exact descriptor readiness as a compatibility adapter concern if byte-level scheduler tests require it.

Implemented:

- A reusable `ExecuteScheduleMultiCore`-style coordinator over owned Rust schedules, including multicore time/core initialization through the ported pure helper, C-shaped schedule summary output, max-core reservation checks, injected child-process spawning through `EGPCtrlSet`, descriptor-to-schedule-index tracking, concurrent spawning while enough cores are free, generic process polling, first-success result reporting with `% Result found by ...` plus accumulated child output, schedule-exhaustion reporting, explicit C-return-value mapping for success and `SCHEDULE_DONE`, optional filtered-default-schedule retry when an exhausted search schedule leaves more than the C retry-threshold budget, schedule-cell heuristic/order selection for the executable bridge, and tests for first success, core-capacity sequencing, exhaustion, default retry, no-retry exhaustion, schedule-cell ordering preservation, the unschedulable-core edge case, worker argument filtering, and real-binary auto-schedule worker replay.
- Main `eprover` preprocessing-schedule integration for `--auto-schedule`/`--satauto-schedule` now runs the generated schedule through the reusable coordinator, spawning explicit selected-strategy worker processes from the original invocation with recursive scheduling, output-file, PID/version, and conflicting CPU-limit options stripped while preserving resource-info so captured worker output includes the ordinary preprocessing-time/resource footer. Selected preprocessing workers compute the generated search class and run the nested search schedule through the same coordinator, spawning hidden search workers with both the selected preprocessing strategy and selected search strategy fixed; exhausted nested search schedules use the filtered default-schedule retry wrapper. The parent prints the C-shaped schedule summary, worker startup/completion lines, `% Result found by ...`, the winning child output, its own resource footer when requested, and returns the winning child status; exhausted schedules emit `GaveUp`/resource-out.

Pending:

- Exact C fork child return semantics, shared already-parsed/preprocessed child state, SIGTERM `PARENT_REQUEST` handling, exact multi-layer resource-usage ordering from forked parents/children, and exact parent/child CPU accounting for nested search and default-schedule retry budgets.

Change-later notes:

- C `ExecuteScheduleMultiCore` can spin if the next schedule cell requires more cores than the total available and no subprocess is running to free capacity. Rust returns an explicit diagnostic for that state; revisit only if reference traces prove the hang is observable compatibility.
- The C API returns a schedule index only in the forked child and exits the parent on result. Rust exposes an explicit report so callers can decide whether to replay C parent-exit behavior or compose schedule execution into a larger workflow.
- C computes default-schedule retry time from total CPU including children but then re-enters `ScheduleTimesInitMultiCore`, which subtracts the current parent process CPU time again. Rust's reusable retry wrapper and nested executable bridge treat the computed remaining time as the retry schedule's remaining budget; revisit when exact re-exec parent/child CPU accounting is wired.
- C forks from the current proof-search state, while Rust's portable executable bridge re-execs the current binary as selected preprocessing and search workers using filtered original command lines plus hidden fixed-strategy controls. This avoids nonportable forked control flow on Windows, but it means nested search workers rerun parsing and preprocessing instead of inheriting already-parsed/preprocessed child state from the selected preprocessing worker.

Implemented:

- Standalone `classify_problem` binary registration and wrapper with exact C-shaped full help text, C-shaped version text, C option-table descriptions including historical typos, verbosity parsing, output-file redirection including `-o -`, default stdin input through `-`, all C option names accepted for the feature-line path, class-mask/raw-mask validation, C default classification limits, and option-driven limit overrides.
- Ported `--parse-features` consumer for ordinary `SpecFeature` lines: it parses `<name> : <features> : <old-class>`, recomputes threshold-based evaluation fields with `SpecFeaturesAddEval`, prints `SpecFeaturesPrint`, appends `SpecTypePrint` using the current class mask, and has WSL compare-tool coverage for a small standard feature-line reclassification workload.
- Ported `--parse-features --raw-class` consumer for `RawSpecFeature` lines: it parses `<name> : <raw-features> : <old-class>`, recomputes the raw class with the current raw mask, and prints `RawSpecFeaturesPrint`.
- Ported real-input `--raw-class` classification for the currently supported problem parser fragments. The executable allocates a `ProofState` with `--free-numbers`/`--free-objects` symbol properties, parses each input file or stdin through the shared clause/TPTP/TSTP parser into represented formula owners without eagerly locking TSTP input to first-order, applies silent `--sine` filtering, computes `RawSpecFeaturesCompute`, and prints the C-shaped `<name> : <RawSpecFeaturesPrint>` line while carrying the parsed problem type into the raw class. Unit coverage includes THF formula-owner input through the higher-order parser context.
- Ported standard real-input classification for the currently supported parser fragments: it computes the raw formula metadata used by C over represented formula owners, runs `FormulaSetPreprocConjectures` plus `FormulaSetCNF2` before clause preprocessing, archives/preprocesses clauses with C-shaped equation-definition unfolding controls under the `classify_problem.c` `--no-preprocessing` gate, computes `SpecFeaturesCompute`, restores formula-derived order/definition fields from the raw pass, applies `SpecFeaturesAddEval`, and prints either `<name> : <SpecFeaturesPrint> : <SpecTypePrint>`, `--specsig` signature features, or `--generate-tptp-header` syntax lines. The real-input formula-owner path also has regression coverage for TSTP include selectors feeding only selected formula owners into CNF, THF raw/CNF class strings using the returned parsed problem type after the global cell is reset, and the caller-specific equality-definition unfolding boundary for `--no-preprocessing`.
- Ported successful `--merged-classification=N` output for the currently supported parser fragments: the executable computes the raw class with the current limits, parsed problem type, and `RAW_DEFAULT_MASK`, computes the represented CNF-side class with C's default merged-classification formula-CNF constants, parsed problem type, and `DEFAULT_MASK`, prints `<name> : (NULL) : <raw-class><cnf-class>`, preserves C's `--merged-classification=-1` branch suppression, uses the C all-hyphen CNF-class fallback shape for non-positive timeout values, and runs positive-timeout standalone executable calls through a hidden child process that writes the C-width NUL-terminated class buffer and falls back to hyphens on timeout or short child output.
- Added C-shaped two-line file-open diagnostics for feature-line and real-input scanners, C `OutClose` wording on final flush failure, and unit coverage for malformed feature-line rejection without partial output.

Pending:

- Exact `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` parity remains pending beyond the parser fragments already shared with `eprover`, plus exact merged-classification POSIX `fork()` inherited-state and `RLIMIT_CPU` semantics beyond Rust's portable re-exec child and elapsed-time fallback.
- Expanded byte-for-byte comparison against a built C `classify_problem` executable remains pending for exact malformed feature-line wording, platform-specific system-error suffixes, raw parse-feature coverage, raw real-input formula edge cases, standard/specsig/header real-input edge cases, and merged classification timeout/failure behavior across real child failures.

Change-later notes:

- `classify_problem.c` initializes `raw_mask` to a 10-character string but rejects user-provided `--raw-mask` values shorter than 11 characters. Rust preserves the initialized default for the wrapper; a cleaned CLI should choose one documented raw-class mask width after compatibility is secured.
- The C option table exposes `--old-cnf`, but `process_options()` has no `OPT_DEF_CNF_OLD` case, so release builds effectively ignore it while assertion-enabled builds can trip the default case. Rust accepts it as a no-op for compatibility.
- The C help text states `--parse-features` conflicts with `--generate-tptp-header`, but the parse-feature branch simply ignores header generation. Rust keeps parse-feature output focused on the C branch behavior.
- The C executable opens the selected output stream before defaulting missing input to `-` and before reading any input, so output-file creation or truncation can occur even when a later feature-line or real-input parse fails while `-o -` remains stdout. Rust preserves that operation order; a transactional output mode should be a separate non-compatibility behavior.
- C standard real-input classification runs formula conjecture preprocessing and `FormulaSetCNF2()` before the `--no-preprocessing` branch that gates clause cleanup and equality-definition unfolding, so the advertised preprocessing switch is not a single global off switch. Other proof-state preprocessing callers can place equality-definition normalization at a different boundary. Rust now applies the represented formula-owner stage for supported parser fragments while preserving the `classify_problem` caller gate; a cleaned classifier should separate those phases in the user-facing model.
- C raw real-input classification computes raw features before clausification over formula sets. Rust now does this over represented formula owners for supported parser fragments; revisit once exact `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` coverage lands for every accepted C input shape.
- C `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` owns TSTP wrapper problem-type setup for real inputs, including `thf(...)` higher-order selection and mixed first-order/higher-order rejection; later `RawSpecFeaturesClassify` and `SpecTypePrint` read that same global residue. Rust preserves parser-side setup/rejection and now threads the parsed problem type explicitly through real-input raw/CNF/spec classification; a cleaned classifier should keep dialect state explicit.
- `print_tptp_header()` computes local depth aggregates with `ClauseSetTPTPDepthInfoAdd()` but prints the already-computed `SpecFeatureCell` depth fields. Rust preserves the visible output; a cleanup can remove the unused local depth calculation after compatibility checks.
- C's merged classification helper forks after parsing and SInE filtering, sets `RLIMIT_CPU`, communicates only the fixed-width class buffer through a pipe, and discards the child exit status except for collecting it. Rust uses an explicit hidden re-exec child for standalone runs, reparsing file inputs and piping buffered stdin inputs; decide after reference coverage whether exact inherited parsed state and CPU-time accounting are required or whether the portable child boundary is the cleaned interface.

Change-later notes:

- C parses `--miniscope-limit` into `miniscope_limit`, but the main CNF call passes a hard-coded `1048576` miniscope limit. Rust mirrors the hard-coded formula-set CNF limit; a cleaned mode should decide whether the advertised option should finally control the phase.

Rust preserves the parser-visible flag effect through explicit `ClauseParseOptions`; after parity, the dead C boolean and global parser mutation are good cleanup candidates.
- C `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` owns TSTP wrapper problem-type setup for eground inputs, including `thf(...)` higher-order selection and mixed first-order/higher-order rejection. Rust preserves that by leaving `eground`'s run problem type unset until the shared parser sees records; a cleaned grounding API should thread dialect state explicitly.
- C `GroundSetPrint` delegates through `ClausePrint`, so TSTP ground-instance wrappers depend on process-global `OutputFormat` and `problemType` rather than on explicit grounding arguments. Rust keeps those values explicit in the eground adapter; a cleaned grounding API should preserve that explicit boundary.
- C opens the selected output stream before inserting the default `-` input and before scanning any input file, so output-file creation/truncation can happen even if a later input open or parse fails. Rust preserves this order; transactional output should be a separate non-compatibility mode.
- DIMACS output inherits `ccl_grounding.c`'s split-stream behavior: non-empty non-unit clause literals are written to `stdout` even when the passed `FILE* out` is the global output file. Rust preserves that path for file output through explicit split writers; a cleaned DIMACS renderer should use a single destination outside compatibility mode.
- Equational inputs are recoded into predicate literals after a warning, so users must provide equality axioms separately. This is visible compatibility behavior, but a future UX pass should consider making the semantic shift harder to miss.
- If grounding leaves an unrecognized completion state, C falls through to `assert(false && "Unknown incompleteness?!?")`. Rust reports a generic incomplete message for the extra internal `Unknown` state; exact release-mode C behavior should be checked before deciding whether to expose or eliminate this fallback.

- C opens the selected output stream before inserting the default `-` rule file and before scanning rule or target files, so output-file creation/truncation can happen even if later input opening or parsing fails. Rust preserves this order; transactional output should be a separate non-compatibility mode.
