- `build_rw_system()` accepts every positive unit clause as a rewrite rule and force-marks all literals oriented left-to-right without consulting the ordering. Rust preserves that behavior; a future explicit rewrite-rule importer should distinguish "trusted rule direction" from "ordered demodulator" construction.
- C silently does nothing when no target file is supplied. Rust preserves that no-output execution path; a user-facing normalization tool should probably require at least one of `--terms`, `--clauses`, or `--formulas` outside compatibility mode.
- C allocates dummy clause/formula/watchlist containers and runs the broad formula preprocessing/CNF bridge just to harvest rewrite rules, relying on parser-owned global `problemType` residue for that CNF pass. Rust now preserves the broad represented formula-owner preprocessing/CNF route for supported rule inputs while carrying the parsed rule problem type explicitly; a cleaned API should expose a direct rewrite-rule loader once formula ownership is stable.
- Old-TPTP `input_formula` equality records used as rule files pass through formula CNF before `build_rw_system()` sees them, so they can become non-demodulator implication clauses rather than trusted rewrite rules. A cleaned rewrite-rule loader should reject or explain that boundary earlier instead of relying on post-CNF ignored-clause warnings.

- `batch_create_runner` temporary problem emission is staged as prepared/temp runner requests: selected problems now render C-ordered TSTP type declarations, selected clause stacks, and selected formula stacks, and can be written to a registered temporary file for later process-control handoff.
- `BatchProcessProblem` now has a direct temp-file/process-backend path: it adds the problem to `StructFOFSpec`, creates C-shaped temp runner requests, launches them through a `BatchRunnerBackend` such as `BatchProcCtrlRunnerSet`, polls live backend activity for capacity, emits success/GaveUp output, and backtracks after completion.
- `BatchStructFOFSpecInit`/`StructFOFSpecParseAxioms` and `BatchProcessFile` now have concrete filesystem-backed paths: shared includes are parsed with default-directory resolution and missing-include notices, parsed include names are registered only on success, ordinary `cnf` entries are preserved as clause-backed formulas, watchlist clauses remain in the clause set, supported `fof`/`tff`/`tcf`/`thf` formula wrappers are loaded with their detected problem type, `tff`/`tcf`/`thf` wrapper tags mark the shared signature as typed before body parsing like C, TCF watchlist formula bodies are collected into clause-backed watchlist entries after stripping leading universal prefixes, `StructFOFSpec` tracks the aggregate selected dialect across shared and concrete problem sets for runner rendering, problem files are forced to TSTP, and destination files are opened only after successful source parsing before running through the direct backend path.
- The `e_ltb_runner` executable has a wrapper over the concrete batch backend: it parses the C option surface with C-compatible help/version text and legacy option descriptions, applies verbosity and output-level globals, supports global output redirection including `-o -` and C-shaped output-file open diagnostics, supports per-problem output-directory prefixes, preserves C's output-file creation/truncation before positional-argument usage validation, preserves C's execution-path `OutClose(GlobalOut)` final flush/error check, accepts the optional prover path, parses the LTB header and repeated batch specs from a TSTP scanner, applies the global wall-clock fallback only when a spec omits a total limit, initializes shared axioms before non-variant problem execution, forwards scanner default directories, computes the post-initialization remaining total limit with C's `MAX(0, ...)` boundary, and emits the C-shaped non-variant batch summary.
- Post-batch `e_ltb_runner --interactive` mode now runs after each non-variant batch against the same initialized shared-axiom `StructFofSpec`, reads stdin blocks terminated by an exact `go.\n` line, supports C's `help`, `quit`, optional `job <ident>.` prefix, default `unnamed_job`, 30-second fallback wall-clock limit, and interactive proof-output echoing through the concrete temp-file/process backend.
- The wrapper now dispatches C's historical variant modes (`--variants27`, `--variants28`, `--variants28-ho`, `--variants28-25`, and `--variantsj11`) through the staged `BatchProcessVariants` scheduler. Parent-side variant execution uses the C variant/prover tables, emits the C-shaped variant batch completion line, and launches a hidden Rust child mode for each concrete variant problem so child stdout can be captured through the generic-process bridge.
- The hidden LTB variant child reparses the selected batch, suppresses duplicate include-acceptance parse notices, rewrites abstract include names to concrete `.ax` files for the requested variant, initializes a fresh `StructFofSpec`, runs the requested concrete problem through the concrete file/backend path, writes the configured destination file, and returns C-like success status while the parent still classifies success from captured SZS output.

Pending:

- Exact C `fork()`-return child mode for in-process variant workers remains pending.
- Reference comparisons against real CASC LTB specs should be added once the runner can execute problems, because this grammar is intentionally loose and historically tied to specific competition file shapes.

Change-later notes:

- `BatchSpecParse` is explicitly described as wonky in C: comments and newlines are structurally meaningful in the spec format, but the implementation ignores them and relies on scanner token flow. Rust mirrors the token flow for compatibility; later, a strict grammar should be considered only after reference corpus coverage exists.
- `e_ltb_runner` accepts `division.category.training_data`, while `BatchSpecPrint` emits `division.category.training_directory`. Rust preserves both spellings at their respective boundaries so the mismatch stays visible.
- Parsing includes prints `% Accepted ... for parsing` as a side effect before any actual axiom parsing. Keep this until runner output compatibility says whether the notice can move behind a verbosity flag.
- The problem-list loop starts only when the next token is `/` or the literal identifier `Problem|Problems`, then parses two filename-shaped token runs. This accommodates `Problems/...` paths but is not a real section grammar.
- `abstract_to_concrete` discards everything in the abstract name after the first `*`; variant processing should keep that truncation until CASC variant specs are covered by tests.
- C declares `StructFOFSpec` in `cco_sine.h`, implements allocation/parsing/distribution in `cco_sine.c`, and implements add/backtrack/get-problem helpers in `cco_batch_spec.c`. Rust keeps a single `control::sine` owner API; future cleanup can make the C split irrelevant once callers are fully ported.
- `StructFOFSpecBacktrackToSpec` subtracts formula-set distribution with the non-trimming path even when the problem was added with trimmed implication accounting. Rust preserves that by using untrimmed formula subtraction in backtracking; revisit only if full LTB/SInE traces prove this asymmetry is accidental and observable.
- C backtracking also calls `SigBacktrack` and term-bank GC through global owner state. Rust currently reports the signature f-count target from the `StructFofSpecBacktrackReport`; wiring actual signature/term-bank backtracking belongs with the future file-parser/term-bank ownership integration.
- `BatchProcessProblems` biases proportional total-time slices upward with `rest/(remaining)+1`, then caps by the per-problem limit only when that limit is nonzero. It can therefore pass negative wall-clock limits to `BatchProcessFile` after the total limit is already exceeded. Rust preserves this arithmetic for now.
- `BatchProcessProblems` constructs `dest_dir` paths by appending a literal `/` before the destination file without normalizing empty strings or trailing slashes. Rust preserves this path construction; later platform-aware path joining should be considered only after LTB output compatibility is covered.
- `e_ltb_runner` initializes shared axioms before calling `BatchProcessProblems`, then passes `MAX(0, spec->total_wtc_limit-(now-start))` so parsing/initialization consumes global time outside the per-problem loop. Rust's concrete `BatchProcessProblems` path expects an already-initialized `StructFofSpec`, and the executable wrapper now keeps that timing boundary explicit.
- `e_ltb_runner` opens `GlobalOut` after option parsing but before validating the remaining positional argument count, so `-o file` can create or truncate the output file even for a usage error while `-o -` routes to stdout and does not create a literal `-` file. Rust now preserves those observable side effects in the wrapper; a cleaned CLI should only remove them behind a compatibility-mode decision.
- Help/version exit inside `process_options()` before C opens or closes `GlobalOut`, while ordinary execution reaches the final close. Rust preserves that split; a cleaned runner API should return explicit output ownership and close/flush results.
- `e_ltb_runner` carries enum values and globals for obsolete or unused hooks such as `OPT_NEWSCHEDULE`, `OPT_PRINT_STATISTICS`, and `app_encode`. Rust omits those dead hooks from the executable surface for now; add them only if a reference command line proves they are observable.
- The C help text says `[Batchfile] [PATH_TO_EPROVER]`, while the fatal usage error says `<spec> [<path-to-eprover>]`. Rust now preserves both spellings in their original contexts for CLI compatibility.
- C variant modes are hard-coded to historical CASC packaging details, including relative executable paths like `./eprover-ho` and `./eprover-25`. Rust preserves those tables for parent-side variant dispatch; packaging-path behavior still needs reference tests against an actual CASC-style StarExec layout.
- C implements variant workers by returning `NULL` from `EGPCtrlCreate` in the forked child and then continuing through the same call stack into `BatchProcessFile`. Rust uses an explicit hidden child-process command instead, which is easier to audit on Windows and avoids unsafe fork-style control flow; exact fork-return semantics remain a compatibility question if byte-level traces prove the distinction observable.
- C temporarily swaps `spec->includes` with a freshly allocated concrete include stack, calls `BatchStructFOFSpecInit`, then frees and restores the original include stack. Rust keeps the original `BatchSpec` immutable and initializes from a cloned concrete include list; preserve the observable concrete `.ax` rewrite, but avoid the temporary owner mutation unless exact memory/lifetime behavior becomes relevant.
- C interactive mode separates its output stream argument from the input source but always reads jobs from stdin, uses a literal `go.\n` terminator, treats `help` and `quit` as command tokens without consuming or validating trailing input, and falls back to a 30-second per-problem limit when the batch spec omits one. Rust preserves these quirks for compatibility; a later cleaned interface should make input/output streams, command grammar, and timeout defaults explicit.
- `batch_create_runner` combines logging, SInE problem selection, temporary-file creation, type/axiom printing, process naming, and child-process creation in one helper routed through `GlobalOut`. Rust stages these as an explicit runner request for testability; later cleanup can split selection, rendering, temp-file ownership, and spawning once byte-compatible runner traces exist.
- `batch_create_runner` calls `AxFilterPrintBuf(name, 320, ax_filter)` but ignores the boolean that reports buffer overflow, so an overlong filter name can feed a truncated process name into process-control naming. Rust keeps the fixed 320-byte boundary visible and reports overflow explicitly.
- C assumes `source_files` and `dest_files` stacks have matching lengths because parsing pushes them in pairs. Rust reports an interface diagnostic for hand-built mismatched specs rather than indexing past the destination list.
- `BatchProcessProblem` leaves `handle` uninitialized if the wall-clock limit is already expired before the outer loop first executes, then tests `if(handle)`. Rust treats that path as GaveUp and still backtracks; keep this documented as an intentional cleanup of accidental C undefined behavior.
- `BatchProcessProblem` gives each child `MIN((wct_limit+1)/2, wct_limit-used)` seconds, so zero or negative per-problem limits can produce nonpositive child CPU budgets. Rust preserves the formula in the staged request path until real process-limit compatibility tests decide whether to clamp.
- `BatchProcessProblem` interleaves status construction with `GlobalOut`, optional file output, optional socket output, and interactive proof echoing. Rust stages explicit global, file-like external, and socket-like writers; a later API should separate result synthesis from output transports once byte compatibility is covered.
- The C socket path sends only the prover's proof text on success but sends a generated `% SZS status GaveUp ...` line on failure. Rust preserves that transport asymmetry for now; normalize it only after socket-client compatibility is covered.
- `BatchProcessFile` forces the scanner to TSTP format for every problem file, independent of the batch-spec parse format. Rust exposes that as a loader request field; revisit only if broader input-format compatibility proves other formats are accepted by the C runner.
- Because `BatchProcessFile` forces TSTP before `FormulaAndClauseSetParse`, legacy old-TPTP `input_clause(...)`/`input_formula(...)` records trip the downstream TSTP wrapper parsers even though the generic loop lists those tokens. Rust now tests that LTB problem loading remains TSTP-only; the separate executable parser keeps its deliberately ported mixed-dialect compatibility surface.
- `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` puts ordinary `cnf` input into the formula set as clause-backed formulas; the clause set receives watchlist clauses. Rust's concrete LTB loader preserves that split, even though a cleaned representation would likely store ordinary parsed clauses directly as clauses.
- `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` asserts that watchlist formula wrappers are clause-backed before converting them with `WFormClauseToClause`, while `WFormulaTSTPParse` does not mark TCF formula wrappers as clauses. Rust makes TCF watchlist conversion explicit in the LTB parser instead of carrying that debug/release mismatch forward; a cleaned C-compatible parser should give typed-clause watchlists a distinct clause-owner path.
- `StructFOFSpecParseAxioms` silently skips missing include files after printing `% Could not find ...`, and records parsed include names only after successful parsing. Rust preserves that behavior; later user-facing cleanup should decide whether missing shared axioms should ever become hard errors.
- `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` and `WFormulaTSTPParse` communicate first-order versus higher-order syntax through C's process-global `problemType`, and the outer wrapper dispatcher also marks the shared signature typed as soon as it sees `tff`, `tcf`, or `thf`. Rust tracks the aggregate selected-problem dialect explicitly in `StructFOFSpec` for runner temp-file rendering and preserves the typed-signature side effect in the batch loader. Future compatibility work should decide whether C's global parser residue and `StructFOFSpecBacktrackToSpec` reset behavior need byte-for-byte reproduction, or whether explicit aggregate rendering remains the preferred owner boundary.
- Scanner default-directory handling expects slash-terminated directory prefixes. Rust keeps the raw C-style directory forwarding for LTB file loading; a later path-aware API should normalize only after compatibility tests cover relative include lookup.
- `BatchProcessFile` opens the destination file only after parsing the source problem succeeds, so a parse failure does not create or truncate the destination. Rust's concrete filesystem method preserves that ordering.
- `BatchProcessFile` contains commented-out Started/Ended status lines but still flushes `stdout` after creating the scanner. Rust now models that standalone flush through the explicit batch global-output writer while leaving the commented status lines dormant unless byte-level runner traces show they matter.
- `BatchProcessVariants` mutates `spec->executable` to the current prover for each variant and restores it after the round. Rust passes the prover through the staged job record; the final runner bridge should choose deliberately between temporary mutation for compatibility and explicit per-job executable routing.
- `BatchProcessVariants` contains a CASC-28/J10 hack that allocates and initializes a fresh `StructFOFSpec` for every concrete problem instead of sharing one initialized spec across the variant round. Rust stages only the orchestration now; real include/problem loading should preserve this no-shared-axioms behavior until variant traces prove it can be simplified.
- `BatchProcessVariants` does not stop the outer variant loop when all abstract problems are solved; later rounds still print "already solved" lines. Rust preserves that loop shape in the staged scheduler, even though a cleaned scheduler would likely break early.
- `BatchProcessVariants` child success is based on the captured SZS result being Theorem or Unsatisfiable, not on the child exit status and not on Satisfiable/CounterSatisfiable statuses. Rust preserves that LTB-specific success set in the generic-process bridge.
- The generic child bridge prints the child controller startup and completion lines before replaying captured child stdout, matching the C parent-side order. A later streaming worker API should preserve that order if byte-level variant traces depend on it.
- `BatchProcessProblem` relies on `EPCtrlSetGetResult(procs, true)` for failed child cleanup and `EPCtrlSetFree(procs, true)` for final cleanup, so temporary-file lifetime is coupled to process-set lifetime. Rust exposes that coupling in `BatchProcCtrlRunnerSet`; a later scheduler API can split process polling from temporary-file ownership after byte-compatible traces exist.
- `batch_create_runner` writes type declarations for the whole signature before the filtered selected problem, not only declarations reachable from selected clauses/formulas. Rust preserves that broad declaration pass for now; a later minimized-problem writer should only be considered after child-runner compatibility tests cover typed LTB problems.
- `BatchProcessProblem` bases its spawn-capacity check on `EPCtrlSetCardinality(procs)`, which reflects failed children deleted by `EPCtrlSetGetResult`. Rust's backend path keeps this as a live `active_count()` query rather than counting historical spawn metadata; later scheduler cleanup can make that distinction explicit in a narrower process-pool abstraction.

Implemented behavior:

- Initial PCL2 `pcl_analysis` support is ported as `src/pcl2/analysis.rs`, including expression/step/protocol proof-distance computation, cached `proof_distance` updates, generation and simplification reference counter updates by C operator class, proof/non-proof reference counter split, example selection by proof-step priority and useless-generation/useless-simplification ratio, and the C loop behavior for zero negative-example budget.
- Initial PCL2 `pcl_lemmas` support is ported as `src/pcl2/lemmas.rs`, including default lemma parameters, deterministic inference weights, active/passive generation and simplification reference counters by C operator class, pure quote references, proof-tree-size caching with lemma-as-zero recursion, lemma-quality computation and comparison, and sequential, recursive, and flat lemma selection with the C sequential limit check shape.
- Initial PCL2 `pcl_propanalysis` support is ported as `src/pcl2/propanalysis.rs`, including max-step selection by standard weight, strict symbol count, literal count, and depth; aggregate FOF/positive/negative/mixed clause counts; literal and symbol-type totals; empty-clause exclusion from global counts; and C-shaped property-summary rendering using the ported clause property-info formatter.
- Initial PCL2 `pcl_proofcheck` support is ported as `src/pcl2/proofcheck.rs`, including check/prover enums, clausal precondition collection, copied parent-clause insertion into generated check problems, negated skolemized target-unit generation, FOF-parent/target warning side-channel rendering through explicit warning-aware APIs, E/TPTP problem rendering and shell-command invocation, Otter problem rendering and shell-stdin-redirection invocation, SPASS/DFG problem rendering and shell-command invocation, output-level progress/prover-trace/failed-problem rendering through explicit output APIs, assumption and split-step classification, and protocol-level checked/unchecked counting.
- A root Rust package with library and `eprover` binary targets.
- Generic binary-tree traversal support from `clb_avlgeneric`, including left-spine initialization, iterative in-order next-node traversal, empty-root handling, and a function-shaped API matching the generated C traversal pair.
- The `DStr` byte-buffer behavior from `clb_dstrings`, including allocation with one C reference, C `DStrGetRef`/`DStrFree` counter compatibility including NULL no-op wrappers and final-release reporting, C-string append truncation at the first embedded NUL, C-string text view versus full byte-buffer view separation, byte-oriented C-string append/set helpers for non-UTF-8 external data, `DStrAppendDStr`-style descriptor append through the source C-string view, byte-buffer append with signed-`int` length compatibility where nonpositive lengths are no-ops, integer append, NULL-terminated string-array append, last-character deletion including the C non-NUL deletion assertion, C-string-visible copy/core-copy prefixes, allocated trailing-NUL address lookup, reset, minimize including the allocated-empty one-byte buffer distinction, `DStrFGetS`-style fixed-chunk line reading with C-string append semantics, and the distinct C growth rules for string and byte appends.
- The `PStack` and `DStack` growth/access patterns from `clb_pstacks` and `clb_dstacks`, including explicit logical capacity doubling, reset without shrinking, DStack assertion-shaped top/below-top/element/pop access, PStack assertion-shaped pop/top/top-address/below-top/indexed access/assignment/discard helpers and signed empty top-stack-pointer behavior, value and borrowed-pointer-identity search helpers, swap-remove discard, stack copying/pushing, C-shaped binary search and merge behavior, element-wise stack rendering hooks plus the observed `PStackPrintInt(..., "%4ld.", ...)` output shape and `%p`-style pointer-address rendering, and integer average/deviation computation.
- Dynamic `PDArray` and `DDArray` storage from `clb_pdarrays` and `clb_ddarrays`, including exponential and fixed-multiple growth, zero/`NULL` initialization, mutating element access that extends arrays like the C macros, explicit raw `PDArrayEnlarge`/`DDArayEnlarge` target-size compatibility helpers with Rust guards for C's direct-call under-allocation hazard, raw `PDArrayElementClear` pointer-slot clearing without growth for already allocated indices, signed-`long` `PDArrayAdd`/`DDArrayAdd` loop wrappers where nonpositive limits are no-ops, PDArray/DDArray nonnegative-index assertion behavior, delete/store/add/increment helpers, C-shaped `DDArrayDebugPrint` fixed-width output with print-time enlargement and signed-size newline-only behavior, and `DDArraySelectPart` partition selection with C assertion-shaped preconditions, sum-then-divide final averaging, and a signed-size wrapper.
- `PDRangeArr` signed-index dynamic arrays from `clb_pdrangearrays`, including low/limit key tracking, upward and downward expansion, C-compatible offset shifts, pointer-member counting, checked deletion, copying, mutating element access that extends the range like the C macros, explicit raw `PDRangeArrEnlarge` branch-choice compatibility, and integer increment helpers.
- `FixedDArray` fixed-size integer vector helpers from `clb_fixdarrays`, including initialization, assertion-shaped indexed access/assignment, assertion-shaped same-size preconditions for component-wise add/subtract/weighted-add/min/max, ordinary copying plus C-shaped nullable copy handling, and C-shaped debug printing.
- Shared helpers from `clb_defines`, including the default `COMCHAR`/`COMCHARRAW` spellings, `KILO`/`MEGA`, `LONG_MEM`/`INTORP_MEM`-style memory estimates, `BOOL2STR`, `MAX`/`MIN` helper behavior with right-hand tie selection and unordered-comparison branch shape, `CMP`, `SWAP`-style value exchange, `ABS` for ordinary signed-long values with a panic on the C undefined-overflow minimum, boolean XOR/equivalence helpers, `WriteStr`-style one-shot raw file-descriptor output with C `strlen` truncation and signed-to-unsigned failure conversion across POSIX, MSVC UCRT, and GNU/MinGW MSVCRT fd surfaces, `TSTPOUT`/`TSTPOUTFD`-style SZS status line helpers preserving the formatted `%` versus direct `%%` comment-prefix split, and the shared `IntOrP` integer/pointer payload shape represented as a checked Rust enum for containers that mix long integer tags and pointer-like handles.
- `IntMap` multi-representation integer map behavior from `clb_intmap`, including empty/single/array/tree states, density-triggered representation switching, `get_ref` slot creation with null values, assignment and deletion return behavior, entry-count inflation for repeated null array references, C-shaped `PDRangeArr` backing growth on some array-backed read/delete misses and raw-lower array iterator scans, side-effect-free Rust range iteration over non-null values, and debug printing.
- Memory allocation facade behavior from `clb_memory` and `clb_newmem`, including secure allocation/reallocation counters, C-shaped fatal wrapper APIs with explicit `try_*` helpers, exact-size and 16-byte aligned free-list policies, newmem chunk population, secure string duplication with trailing NUL bytes, zero-initialized integer arrays, old-allocator free-list flushing, `clb_newmem` no-op flush behavior, and debug/free-list stats rendering.
- `MinHeap` binary minimum heap behavior from `clb_min_heap`, including comparator-driven ordering, integer/pointer-shaped add helpers, option-returning Rust drain pop plus C-shaped nonempty minimum pop, size/peek queries, C-shaped update/remove/increase/decrease helpers with their assertion and no-op index preconditions including signed negative-index wrappers, the C helper directions for `decr_key` and `incr_key`, optional index-setter callbacks after swaps/removals, and integer plus pointer-address debug printing.
- Object tree/map behavior from `clb_objtrees` and `clb_objmaps`, including comparison-based object storage, duplicate-preserving object insertions, mutable root-like lookups, object extraction/root extraction, assertion-shaped disjoint object-tree merges, map store/get-ref/find/extract semantics with explicit splayed lookup over null-value map slots, sorted traversal, and explicit free-with-deleter hooks.
- Partial-ordering helpers from `clb_partial_orderings`, including exact `CompareResult` and `HoOrderKind` discriminants, quasi-order conversion, C-shaped asserting inverse relation handling plus checked optional inversion, and the C comparison-symbol table.
- Permanent string registry behavior from `clb_permastrings`, including duplicate interning, owned-string store, null-shaped borrowed and owned optional lookup/store helpers, explicit global registry clearing, and returned shared strings that remain valid for Rust holders after the registry is cleared.
- `PList` doubly linked list behavior from `clb_plist`, including explicit anchor cells, C-shaped raw insertion/extraction/deletion/clear/free mutation contracts with panic-shaped invalid-handle and linked-cell preconditions, extraction and reinsertion across anchors, freed cell-slot reuse, forward/backward navigation, and mixed integer/pointer payload helpers.
- `PLocalStack` local pointer stack behavior from `clb_plocalstacks`, including caller-managed raw push/pop preconditions as explicit Rust panics, the C `ensure_space` equality-triggered growth rule, old-size-plus-space doubling, `PLocalStackTop` count semantics through an explicit compatibility alias, term-argument order helpers, and non-raw-pointer tagged-stack slot accounting.
- `PQueue` circular pointer/integer queue behavior from `clb_pqueue`, including head/tail indexing, FIFO extraction and stack-view last extraction that copy payloads without clearing absolute slots, empty extraction/look assertion behavior, raw absolute-slot element access, raw-modulo `PQueueIncIndex` slot advancement, bury-at-front insertion, C-shaped exported `PQueueGrow` layout for full rings and direct raw calls, reset by rewinding indices without shrinking or clearing absolute slots, absolute tail/index iteration, and mixed integer/pointer helper values.
- Property-bit helpers from `clb_properties`, including set/delete/flip/assign, all-bit queries, C-shaped raw masked any-set results plus Rust bool convenience checks, masked property extraction, and masked equivalence checks.
- Shared simple helpers from `clb_simple_stuff`, including C-string bytewise string distance/prefix/index helpers with embedded-NUL termination, weighted-object comparison/sorting, C-shaped JKISS random state and static-state wrapper behavior including `JKISSSeed(NULL, ...)` not affecting the exported random sequence, bounded indentation strings, null-terminated string-index/cardinality helpers, positive-only GCD, `ProverResult`, `ProblemType`, first-order/higher-order syntax conflict checks, and an executable-run reset helper for isolating C-shaped process-global problem-type state across in-process Rust runs.
- `StrTree` string-keyed map behavior from `clb_stringtrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, mutable value rewrite, extraction, deletion, and deterministic sorted traversal.
- `FloatTree` floating-point-keyed map behavior from `clb_floattrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, splaying-style root tracking that keeps the stored `-0.0`/`+0.0` representation on duplicate signed-zero access, mutable value rewrite, extraction/deletion, node queries, deterministic sorted traversal for ordered float keys, signed-zero equivalence, infinities, and deterministic NaN bucketing.
- `NumTree` numeric-keyed map behavior from `clb_numtrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, splaying-style root tracking for later root extraction, mutable value rewrite, extraction/deletion, root-like draining, node/max-key queries, debug printing, deterministic sorted traversal, and limited traversal starting at the first key greater than or equal to the limit.
- `NumXTree` numeric-keyed four-slot value map behavior from `clb_numxtrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics with defaulted extra slots, full-entry insertion, lookup, splaying-style root tracking for later root extraction, mutable value-slot rewrite, extraction/deletion, root-like draining, node/max-key queries, deterministic sorted traversal, and limited traversal starting at the first key greater than or equal to the limit.
- `PTree` pointer/identity-set behavior from `clb_ptrees`, including duplicate-preserving store semantics, lookup, splaying-style root tracking for later root extraction, binary lookup alias, extraction/deletion/root-like draining, destructive and non-destructive merge helpers, root-first stack/vector conversion, copying, shared-element and intersection helpers, equivalence/subset checks, in-order visits, and root-first debug printing with node counts.
- `QuadTree` four-part pointer/integer-keyed map behavior from `clb_quadtrees`, including pointer-then-integer pair comparison, duplicate-preserving insertion, explicit splayed/root-like lookup, mutable value lookup, extraction/deletion, deterministic sorted traversal, and mixed integer/pointer payload helpers.
- Registered persistent-memory helpers from `clb_regmem`, including process-global registration, allocation/reallocation/freeing, cleanup of all registered areas, C-shaped panic-on-invalid-handle wrappers for raw free/reallocation contracts, explicit `try_regmem_*` result helpers, `RegMemProvide` doubling behavior, and zero-filled newly provided tails.
- `SysDate` logical-time helpers from `clb_sysdate`, including creation/invalid sentinels, ordering/equality/maximum macros, increment mutation order, overflow reporting instead of undefined signed overflow, and C-shaped unsigned-long printing.
- Verbose-level helpers from `clb_verbose`, including the global `Verbose` default, nonzero level-1 gate, `>= 2` and `>= 10` gates, closure helpers for macro-like conditional execution, `VERBOUT`/`VERBOUTARG` formatting with explicit flushes, and global `ProgName`/stderr-backed wrappers for direct C macro call-site ports.
- Compact clause-position helpers from `ccl_clausecpos`, including weighted term-position packing, weighted clause-position packing over literal standard weights and right-side offsets, unpacking into full `ClausePos` cursors, compact-position subterm lookup, first/next literal compact-position iteration, and relative literal-position splitting.
- Initial new-evaluation helpers from `ccl_neweval`, including exact priority constants, process-global evaluation counter allocation, C-shaped simple evaluation cells with float heuristic storage and per-position tree-link slots, evaluation-list priority mutation, print/comment string formatting, C comparison/greater-than tie-break behavior including NaN handling through the `CMP` macro shape, and handle-backed splay-tree insertion, lookup, extraction, deletion, smallest lookup, in-order traversal, and debug printing.
- Clause-local and plain-set helpers from `ccl_clausefunc`, including index-based literal removal with cached count/weight updates, literal sign flipping with cached polarity counts, duplicate and trivially false literal cleanup with `DCNormalize` derivation metadata, plain clause-set cleanup with cached literal recounting, plain clause-set canonization and structural-weight sorting, AC-resolved negative literal cleanup, non-orientable unit simplify-reflect tests, naked Boolean-variable elimination through temporary bindings, C-shaped `DCNormalize` metadata, and true-literal tautology creation, term-formula clause collection/CNF set insertion helpers, higher-order flex-clause resolution with `DCFlexResolve`, compact-parent `ClauseIsOrphaned` and `ClauseSetDeleteOrphans` predicate behavior, injectivity-definition recognition/replacement with inverse typed-Skolem generation and duplicate-definition renaming checks, beta-normal represented choice-axiom recognition with proof-state `choice_opcodes` storage, and canonical clause comparison by structural weight.
- Clause source-info metadata from `ccl_clauseinfo`, including optional copied name/source fields, empty `-1` line/column allocation, TSTP/PCL source rendering with unknown and line/column fallbacks, null-info no-op rendering, and C-shaped generated identifier namespace/counter extraction.
- PCL/TSTP proof-documentation step helpers from `ccl_inferencedoc`, including the `PCLTypeStr` mappings for conjecture/question/negated-conjecture, the empty default string for plain/axiom and other roles, C-shaped compact/non-compact `pcl_print_start` prefixes, `pcl_print_end` comment/watchlist suffix handling, `tstp_print_end` comment/watchlist suffix handling for initial clause documentation, formula-side `pcl_formula_print_start` plus `pcl_formula_print_end`/`tstp_formula_print_end` comment terminators, reusable clause inference-expression rendering for binary/unary clause inferences, AC-resolution axiom lists, rewrite chains, and clause definition-application chains, reusable formula inference-expression rendering for introduction, parent-based formula transformations, and nested definition application, exact `OutputFormatType` discriminants, a `DocClauseCreation`-style session helper for initial, paramodulation, simultaneous-paramodulation, equality-resolution, equality-factoring, factoring, and split creation documentation in PCL/TSTP with optional TSTP term type suffixes, C's unsupported-format fallback, and PCL initial-clause stdout marker interleaving metadata, a `DocClauseModification`-style session helper for simplify-reflect, contextual simplify-reflect, AC-resolution, condensation, minimization, answer-literal evaluation, and destructive equality-resolution documentation, pre-rendered formula-view `DocFormulaCreation`/`DocFormulaModification`-style session helpers for represented formula proof-documentation branches until full `WFormula` ownership is ported, and represented `DocClauseFromForm`, `DocClauseQuote`, `DocClauseRewrite`, `DocClauseEqUnfold`, `DocIntroSplitDef`, `DocIntroSplitDefRest`, `DocClauseApplyDefs`, and `DocFormulaIntroDefs` session helpers.
- Clause-position cursors from `ccl_clausepos`, including semi-initialized allocation, clause-backed and standalone literal positions, designated/other side lookup, subterm lookup through `TermPos`, C-shaped clause-position printing, positive/maximal literal cursor search, maximal-side traversal with unoriented right-side visits, leftmost-innermost maximal-subterm traversal, and rewrite-chain sequence extraction over term rewrite links.
- Clause-position trees from `ccl_clausepos_tree`, including clause-to-compact-position association cells, stable clause-identifier comparison for cloned Rust clause snapshots, duplicate-collapsing position insertion, single-position deletion with empty-cell removal, whole-clause deletion, sorted compact-position storage, compact debug rendering of stored clause identifiers and positions, and default LOP plus explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP clause-position debug rendering.
- Initial plain clause-set owner helpers from `ccl_clausesets`, including C-shaped creation-date initialization, append-order insertion, first-entry extraction, set-to-set movement, cached - Executable `tcf(...)` formula bodies now route through the staged `TcfTSTPParse` helper before proof/CNF bridge lowering or non-`$distinct` app-encode formula-owner storage, while direct, parenthesized, and top-level negated non-watchlist `$distinct(...)` TCF bodies take the wrapper-level constant-only pseudo-formula dispatch into represented proof-state formula owners before the TCF clause-body parser. This preserves C's `|`-only TCF body restriction, rejects parenthesized conjunctions under a typed-clause universal prefix, and accepts supported TCF/THF `$distinct` formulas in print-formulas/proof-search paths through `ProofStateProcessDistinct`.
- The temporary THF bridge also accepts applied function equality such as `f @ a = b` and `b = f @ a`, plus conjunction/disjunction over applied predicates such as `p @ a & q @ a`, by parsing application before the existing equality/connective lowering.
- Beta-reducible THF lambda applications such as `(^[X: person]: p @ X) @ a` now normalize through `NamedToDB`/beta conversion before reaching the temporary clause bridge, so they can participate in supported first-order-shaped THF proof search. Supported `fof`, `tff`, and `thf` Boolean lambda equality/disequality formulas now also run the staged single-formula `LambdaToForall` helper after `NamedToDB`, converting lambda equality into quantified equivalence/inequivalence before bridge lowering, so simple extensional lambda axioms can participate in the same proof path. Lambda-bearing or DB-indexed terms that remain after that conversion still stop at the temporary bridge boundary with the explicit full higher-order formula-pipeline diagnostic.
- Ordinary FOF/TFF TSTP equality literals with a lambda-valued operand now use the same named-to-DB and lambda-to-forall path when the opposite operand is a bare declared arrow symbol, for example `f = (^[X: $i]: g @ X)` or `(^[X: $i]: g @ X) = f`. Represented `TermBank` formula parsing recovers the declared arrow symbol from the first-order `_ARITYFIX0` placeholder when the lambda side has the matching arrow type, and supported proof/CNF FOF/TFF lambda bodies now keep the represented owner so `FormulaSetCNF2` performs the higher-order lambda preprocessing instead of immediately bridge-lowering the equality into quantified first-order clauses.
- Typed quantified THF bodies can now use simple predicate application, for example `![X: person]: p @ X` and existential conjectures such as `?[X: person]: p @ X`, through the same temporary clause bridge; full higher-order quantified formula ownership is still deferred.
- Arrow-typed THF quantified variables such as `![F: person > person, X: person]: p @ (F @ X)` now parse under the executable's temporary first-order global bridge by using the higher-order type grammar for binder annotations and preserving free-variable application heads instead of looking them up as signature codes.
- Unary negation in the temporary THF bridge now consumes a following simple application before building the negation, so `~ p @ a` is treated like `~(p @ a)` for the supported first-order-shaped application fragment.
- The temporary first-order TSTP formula bridge now mirrors C's optional application marker after unary negation, accepting `fof(a, axiom, ~ @ p(a)).` in syntax, CNF lowering, and `--app-encode` paths.
- The temporary first-order TSTP formula bridge now also mirrors C's parenthesized logical-operator head application entry for typed formulas, accepting forms such as `(&) @ p(a) @ q(a)`, `(~) @ p(a)`, and existential bodies such as `?[X]:(&) @ p(X) @ q(X)` in syntax-only formula-owner printing, CNF, and `--app-encode` paths.
- The same first-order TSTP bridge now accepts simple typed predicate application starts such as `p @ a`, `(p) @ a`, `~ p @ a`, `~ (p) @ a`, `p @ a & q @ a`, and existential bodies such as `?[X]:p @ X` or `?[X]:(p) @ X` through the staged term-bank formula parser, matching C's shared `TFormulaTSTPParse` application branch for `fof(...)`/`tff(...)` bodies.
- The same bridge now leaves a repeated top-level non-associative TSTP connective as trailing input, so unparenthesized chains such as `p=>q=>r`, `p<=q<=r`, `p<=>q<=>r`, `p<~>q<~>r`, `p~&q~&r`, and `p~|q~|r` are rejected like C; parenthesized right-hand chains remain accepted.
- Parenthesized applied heads and right-hand application equality terms such as `(h @ a) @ b = c`, `((h @ a) @ b) = c`, `c = (h @ a) @ b`, `c = ((h @ a) @ b)`, `c = h @ a @ b`, and `c = (g) @ a` now route through the same staged parser from FOF/TFF/TCF wrappers, and the term-bank application parser preserves residual arrow types for partially applied heads before applying the next `@`.
- Non-`$o` partial applications with user result sorts such as Isabelle's user sort `bool` now stay on the ordinary term-equality path, so LFHOL shapes like `(col @ (lam @ A)) = A` parse as term equality rather than trying to treat `A` as a predicate formula.
- Beta-reducible parenthesized lambda applications in first-order TSTP bodies, such as `(^[X: $i]: p @ X) @ a`, `((^[X: $i]: f @ X) @ a) = b`, and `b = ((^[X: $i]: f @ X) @ a)`, now use the same staged parser plus named-to-DB/beta-normalization guard as the THF bridge before lowering; lambda-valued or DB-indexed residues still require the full higher-order formula pipeline.
- Simple non-Boolean THF application arguments can now themselves be applications, so fragments such as `p @ (f @ a)` parse and lower through the temporary clause bridge.
- The same THF bridge preserves left-associative application when a bare argument has arrow type or when a bare Boolean argument is itself an application, so `appfun @ f @ a` is parsed as `(appfun @ f) @ a` and `r @ p @ a` is rejected instead of being treated as `r @ (p @ a)`; the parenthesized form remains supported.
- Parenthesized THF logical operator heads keep their declared arrow type through application parsing, so forms such as `(&) @ (p @ a) @ (q @ a)` parse as conjunctions instead of failing after the zero-arity logical head is manufactured; parenthesized Boolean operands now stop at the matching `)` so the caller's next `@` remains part of the outer application chain.
- THF application formulas now reuse the term-bank TSTP formula precedence layer for mixed fragments such as `p @ a & q @ a => r @ a`, parsing conjunction before disjunction and allowing one following non-associative connective before the temporary CNF bridge lowers the result.
- The remaining temporary FOF/TFF/TCF bridge, plus the old `input_formula(...)` bridge only in lowered compatibility paths, also accepts C-supported formula-level `=` and `!=` when a compound formula operand leaves those tokens for the formula parser, lowering them through the existing equivalence and XOR CNF paths while ordinary term equality remains handled by the equation parser. Represented old-TPTP, FOF/TFF, and ordinary non-watchlist TCF formula-owner paths now bypass that bridge whenever the staged parser accepts the full body.
- The temporary FOF/TFF/TCF bridge now accepts top-level Boolean `$ite(...)` and `$let(...)` formula atoms through the same term-bank path C uses before predicate-literal encoding, including as unparenthesized existential bodies, formula-level equality/disequality can treat those FOOL atoms as equivalence/XOR operands instead of prematurely consuming them as term equalities, non-Boolean `$ite`/`$let` primary terms followed by `=` or `!=` remain ordinary typed equality/disequality literals instead of being rejected by the formula-primary branch, semantic clause lowering expands direct formula-position Boolean `$ite(C,T,F)` predicate literals plus the first direct-literal term-position `$ite` occurrence, rewrites direct complex-Boolean `$eq`/`$neq` formula terms to equivalence/XOR or the C `$true` shortcut before Boolean-term conversion only inside the `--fool-unroll` phase, and when `--fool-unroll` is enabled it expands the first direct-literal nontrivial Boolean formula subterm in literal arguments into C's guarded implication shapes; direct `$let` occurrences through supported quantifier/connective scopes are lifted into fresh `epred`/`esk` definitions before CNF, and conjecture lowering now negates the rewritten body while lowering generated definitions positively so they are not distributed into the negated conjecture formula or stamped with the input wrapper's role/source metadata. Parser-supported FOF/TFF Boolean `$ite` bodies and lambda application/equality bodies now bypass this bridge through represented formula owners for print/syntax input, and supported proof/CNF FOF/TFF lambda and `$ite`/`$let` bodies do the same with higher-order parsed-problem promotion; remaining unsupported FOOL spellings still use the bridge. Syntax-only `--print-formulas` preserves the formula-owner surface through pretty TSTP output, `--app-encode` preserves the original term-encoded `$ite`/`$let`, and full formula ownership is still required before this is treated as complete FOOL support.
- The temporary bridge's direct Boolean-subterm split and direct complex-Boolean `$eq`/`$neq` replacement are gated by `--fool-unroll`, while direct `$ite` and `$let` lifting preserve encoded Boolean equalities when unrolling is disabled. `$let` internals remain a separate let-lifting concern rather than ordinary FOOL subterms, matching the current staged pass boundary.
- Supported THF/FOOL `$let` formulas with parameterized definitions now treat definition-head variables as let-local formals during the bridge's TSTP free-variable rejection and Skolem dependency scans, so direct applications such as `$let(f: s > $o, f(X) := p @ X, f @ a)` lower through the current CNF bridge in both axiom and conjecture roles instead of being rejected as globally free.

Known gaps:

- `pcl_analysis` reports dangling proof-distance references as syntax diagnostics rather than reproducing C's null dereference, preserves silent ignores for dangling direct references during generation/simplification counter updates, uses deterministic PCL-id ordering for equal example-selection scores instead of qsort's unspecified tie order, and keeps f32-shaped example scoring plus the C `neg_examples == 0` no-selection loop behavior.
- `pcl_lemmas` uses deterministic zero defaults for C's uninitialized inference-weight slots, preserves the executable formula that ignores `proof_tree_w`/`proof_dag_w`, preserves the sequential selector's post-mark `res > max_number` limit check, and reports missing proof-size parents as syntax diagnostics while keeping missing reference-counter parents non-fatal.
- `pcl_propanalysis` keeps C's zero-denominator average formulas and split between empty-clause exclusion from aggregate counts and inclusion in max-step scans, but avoids C's invalid union dereferences for empty, FOF-only, or shell-containing protocols.
- `pcl_proofcheck` currently ports generated check-problem construction, E/Otter/SPASS problem rendering and shell-backed process invocation, FOF-parent/target warning side-channel rendering, output-level progress/prover-trace/failed-problem rendering, and check bookkeeping, including C's constructed command text, fixed 180-byte `fgets` success-marker and trace chunking, C-string truncation inside each chunk, and no synthetic final newline for unterminated prover output. Clausal-only handling of FOF parents, legacy Otter/SPASS truth-literal hacks, platform-specific `popen` command diagnostics/quoting, and unchecked-as-assumed split-step reporting are documented for later cleanup.
- The supported executable path now covers first-order clause-list and many formula-bridge inputs through parsing, preprocessing, proof-control initialization, parsed-problem-type global-index initialization, indexed saturation, result reporting, proof-object/statistics output, resource-limit handling, and a large portion of CLI option effects. Remaining completion work is concentrated in full `FormulaAndClauseSetParse`/formula-owner parity, full higher-order unification/ordering/inference surfaces, exact multi-process scheduler/server fork semantics, state-owned global indexes/proof-session ownership, exact proof-document interleaving, and reference/performance comparison against the C executables.
- The Windows/WSL reference harness now archives the C support-tool binaries from the normal upstream build and exposes a `compare-tools` command that compares selected Rust release `.exe` support tools against those references. The supported surfaces include direct normalized help-output comparison for every archived standalone tool, long-form version-output comparison for tools whose C option tables expose `--version`, functional option/remaining-argument comparison for `ex_commandline`, functional stdin or materialized-fixture term/DAG/protocol/learning workloads for `CSSCPA_filter`, `e_axfilter`, `e_deduction_server`, `e_stratpar`, `term2dag`, `termprops`, `edpll`, `eground`, `enormalizer`, `checkproof`, `classify_problem`, `direct_examples`, `epatternize`, `epclanalyse`, `epclextract`, `epcllemma`, and `tsm_classify`, and archived-source linking for C utilities whose upstream Makefile targets are commented out, covering the repeated executable byte-comparison gap without requiring each support tool's full problem-processing corpus yet.
- Scanner support includes both automatic `include_key` stream splicing and explicit `ScannerParseInclude`-style include parsing. The supported executable TPTP/TSTP and old-TPTP bridge now dispatches explicit include scanners with selector filtering, empty-selector suppression, `TPTP` fallback, missing-selector diagnostics, relative include resolution from the input file directory even when the top-level path was provided in Windows-native syntax, and the C snapshot's repeated-include behavior: the threaded skip tree is consulted but not populated on the visible formula parser path, so repeated include declarations are preserved. Remaining parser work is to route every future full formula-owner input path through the same explicit include policy instead of relying on the automatic scanner-splicing compatibility hook.
- `InitIO` stores the program name for later diagnostics, but Rust diagnostic rendering still mostly passes program names explicitly until the executable-level fatal-error path is completed.
- `IntMap` preserves the C representation-state decisions with safe Rust-owned storage, including hidden `PDRangeArr` growth during some read/delete misses, inflated null-slot entry counts, constant-memory storage accounting for array/tree/single map shapes, and the insertion-order-sensitive `IMSingle` array/tree transition; those quirks should be evaluated later as compatibility risks versus cleanup opportunities.
- `Memory`/`NewMem` expose safe byte-buffer allocation and accounting rather than raw `malloc` pointers; the freelist and chunk policies, fatal allocation surface, and `clb_newmem` no-op flush are modeled for sizing/reuse behavior, but allocator-address identity, exact debug poison words, and true uninitialized memory are intentionally not exposed.
- `PLocalTaggedStack` models the portable non-`TAGGED_POINTERS` two-slot representation; raw low-bit pointer tagging is intentionally not used in safe Rust and should be reconsidered only if profiling proves the extra slot accounting matters.
- `PQueue` preserves the observable circular-buffer behavior, including the exported `PQueueGrow` layout. Direct non-full growth remains a compatibility-only raw method because C's copy logic can make uninitialized backing slots appear live.
- `CompareResult::NotGreaterEqual` and `CompareResult::NotLessEqual` are cache-only C enum values with no entries in `POCompareSymbol`; Rust returns `None` for those symbols instead of allowing out-of-bounds array access.
- `StrTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` to preserve sorted traversal and lookup semantics, with explicit recent-root tracking for insertion, duplicate insertion, and successful splayed lookup; exact C miss-splay locality should be revisited if profiling shows this index on hot paths.
- `FloatTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` with an internal total-order float key; exact C splay-root locality and C's accidental behavior for NaN keys are not modeled beyond deterministic handling, though duplicate signed-zero accesses now track the stored key representation like the C node root.
- `NumTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` for the same reason; exact splay-root locality is not modeled beyond tracking a recent root-like key for extraction/draining.
- `NumXTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` for the same reason; exact splay-root locality is not modeled beyond tracking a recent root-like key for extraction/draining.
- `ObjTree` and `ObjMap` model comparison-function behavior with `Ord` keys and safe owned values, including successful recent-root tracking; exact C miss-splay locality, raw pointer ownership transfer, and allocator reuse are not modeled.
- OS wrapper helpers from `clb_os_wrapper`, including resource-limit result discriminants, Linux `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` soft/hard-limit wrappers for the current resource-limit surface, Windows Job Object process-memory limits behind safe wrappers, cooperative Windows CPU-deadline support through `Kernel32` process-time queries instead of native process-time quota termination, wall-clock microsecond/millisecond/second helpers, Linux `clock()` and Windows `Kernel32` process CPU-time queries behind narrow unsafe external-library boundaries, Windows page-size/physical-memory/peak-working-set queries, Linux `sysconf` core-count/page-size/physical-memory queries with existing fallbacks, Linux `getrusage` resource-footer counters with `/proc` fallback, monotonic process-relative fallback clock for unsupported targets, memory striding, C `fopen` mode mapping, secure open/close diagnostics, and feature-gated C-shaped `PERF_CTR_*` performance-counter timing/statistics support.
- `PTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeSet` for deterministic ordered set semantics; exact C splay-root locality and pointer-address ordering should be revisited once stable arena/handle identity is wired into terms.
- `QuadTree` currently uses Rust `BTreeMap` and caller-provided ordered pointer identities; exact C splay-root locality and raw pointer-address ordering should be revisited when comparison caches are integrated with term storage.
- `RegMem` uses explicit opaque handles and initialized byte buffers instead of raw registered pointers and uninitialized `SecureMalloc` memory; call sites that need typed static scratch space should wrap those handles at the ownership boundary. The C `RegMemFree`/`RegMemRealloc` path ignores failed pointer-tree deletion before freeing/reallocating, so Rust's public C-shaped wrappers treat unregistered handles as programming errors and the `try_regmem_*` helpers keep the recoverable form available for ownership adapters.
- `SysDate` uses an LP64-shaped `i64` raw value to match the WSL/Linux C reference build; if a native Windows C reference becomes relevant, C `long` width and `SysDatePrint` output need a target-specific audit.
- Verbose output helpers are wired into the executable's parsed `--verbose` option and now expose the C macro's global `ProgName`/stderr shape, but future progress-reporting call sites still need to use them as the parser/prover pipeline is ported.
- The executable run path applies output-level state, redirects initial print-info output, parses supported clause-list inputs into `ProofState`, loads configured watchlist file/inline state for that clause-list path, initializes proof control, emits C-style initial AC scan/status comments during proof-state initialization when AC handling is enabled, runs the staged first-order saturation loop, and maps proof/saturation/limit outcomes to C-style exit statuses. Full `GlobalOut`-style routing through parsing, preprocessing, proof search, and proof/statistics printing still awaits the complete proof pipeline.
- `--cpu-limit`, `--soft-cpu-limit`, and `--memory-limit` update the C-shaped hard, soft, schedule, memory, and derived heuristic-limit state. Supported proof-search runs now arm a cooperative CPU-clock deadline from the soft limit when present, otherwise the hard limit; soft deadline expiry returns the C-shaped user-resource outcome and `RESOURCE_OUT` exit status, while hard deadline expiry now returns through the hard CPU-limit finalizer with C's doubled-`COMCHAR` `ResourceOut` banner, stderr diagnostic, no-output-file direct-banner-before-buffered-stdout ordering, and `CPU_LIMIT_ERROR` exit status. Linux builds now expose C-shaped `getrlimit`/`setrlimit` soft/hard/full-limit wrappers, install the `SIGXCPU` handler in normal builds, apply C's initial `RLIMIT_CPU` choice (soft limit when present, otherwise hard limit), warn on reduced/failed CPU-limit setup with the C `SetSoftRlimitErr` message shapes including `strerror(errno)` text for unmasked failures, print C's separate `perror("eprover")` line before warning when core-dump suppression cannot be applied, capture the system hard CPU limit during signal setup, reset `RLIMIT_CPU` to that system limit on a soft timeout signal before rearming the hard limit, record the active configured-output descriptor for signal-time hard-timeout output, directly write C's hard-timeout banner/SZS and fatal stderr text from the normal hard `SIGXCPU` trampoline before exiting with `CPU_LIMIT_ERROR`, expose C's scheduler SIGTERM count-plus-default-reset handler shape, and apply the C `RLIMIT_DATA` memory behavior including the duplicated `RLIMIT_AS` branch bug that still passes `RLIMIT_DATA` and the failed-`RLIMIT_DATA` warning mask; native Windows keeps finite `--cpu-limit` on the cooperative process-CPU deadline because `JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_PROCESS_TIME` terminates with `STATUS_QUOTA_EXCEEDED` before C-shaped timeout output can be emitted, while nonzero `--memory-limit` still uses a retained Job Object process-memory limit when the current process can be assigned to one, warning on setup failures. Reference-verified signal delivery/output-file behavior and non-Linux hard-timeout shutdown remain tied to the platform shutdown abstraction. `--resources-info` writes the C preprocessing-time and footer shapes using `os_wrapper`; native Windows reports current-process user/system CPU time and peak working-set pages via `Kernel32`, and Linux now uses `getrusage(RUSAGE_SELF/RUSAGE_CHILDREN)` to sum user/system time while preserving C's parent-only raw `ru_maxrss` field under the historic "pages" label, with `/proc` fallback if the POSIX call fails. Exact maximum-resident-set unit matching remains target-dependent.
- Supported executable comment output now uses the reference WSL/Linux C build's `COMCHAR` raw prefix `%` for preprocessing diagnostics, result banners, SZS statuses, proof-object framing, answer tuples, proof-state section headings, statistics, and resource footer lines. C can be compiled with `UNIX_COMMENTS` to switch `COMCHAR` to `#`; Rust should make that an explicit compatibility mode only if such a build becomes a supported target.

- `che_to_weightgen` can generate pure weight arrays and ordering weight metadata, apply late `TOWeightsParse`-style user overrides to pure generated arrays, parse predefined KBO weight strings into an OCB, install generated weights plus late user overrides into OCBs, and compute precedence-dependent KBO weights from either generated total precedence ranks or the parsed partial OCB matrix. - `che_dagweight` direct `ProofState`/`OCB` ownership remains pending for the refined-DAG C compute path; explicit OCB-backed compute helpers preserve C's conditional maximal-term marking when callers already own a mutable clause, and `RDAGWeight` now registers a banked WFCB callback for mutable-clause HCB evaluation. `DAGWeight`, `RDAGWeight2`, and `RDAGWeight3` keep immutable callbacks because their C compute paths do not call `ClauseCondMarkMaximalTerms`.
- `che_diversityweight` direct `ProofState`/`OCB` ownership remains pending; its explicit OCB-backed compute helper preserves C's conditional maximal-term marking when callers already own a mutable clause, and it now registers a banked WFCB callback for mutable-clause HCB evaluation. `che_orientweight` now also registers banked WFCB callbacks for mutable-clause HCB evaluation.
- `che_termweight`, `che_prefixweight`, `che_tfidfweight`, `che_levweight`, `che_strucweight`, and `che_treeweight` direct `ProofState` ownership remains pending; the Rust WFCB/parsers use the current axiom-backed parse context and explicit `&TermBank`, with lazy related-conjecture initialization. - `GlobalOutFD` is exact for stdout, Unix file targets, and native Windows file targets. The Windows path duplicates the owned Rust file handle into a C-runtime file descriptor behind a narrowly scoped external-DLL boundary so the descriptor has independent close ownership, using UCRT on MSVC and MSVCRT on GNU/MinGW; unsupported targets still use an explicit unknown descriptor sentinel until a matching descriptor abstraction is chosen.
- `TCPChannelAlloc`/`TCPChannelClose` are represented with owned `Read + Write` streams instead of raw integer socket descriptors; this preserves queue and transfer behavior for Rust callers but defers exact descriptor lifetime, `close(2)` diagnostics, and `select`/`fd_set` integration until server control code is ported.
- `CreateServerSock`/`Listen`/`CreateClientSock` are represented by safe `TcpListener`/`TcpStream` wrappers rather than raw integer socket descriptors; Linux and Windows server-socket creation now set `SO_REUSEADDR`, bind, and apply the C backlog before wrapping the listening socket, and client connection preserves C's returned final-address outcome while closing earlier successes through Rust ownership. Other-platform reuse semantics, descriptor-number identity, close diagnostics, C's earlier-success descriptor leak, and exact `gai_strerror` diagnostics still need platform-specific abstractions if server/client programs require byte-identical behavior.

- `cpr_varset` uses one raw pointer type for both the atom-set sentinel and extractable list cells. Rust exposes separate owner and cell-handle types, but still preserves front insertion, arbitrary extraction, duplicates, and invalid `PLiteralNoLit` extraction as an invariant failure.
- `cpr_propsig` lookup is backed by safe owned strings and a deterministic Rust map rather than C's `StrTree` node storage. This preserves name/code behavior and print order, but does not preserve lookup-time tree reorganization or shared raw `char*` identity between the stack and tree.
- `cpr_propclauses` conversion is ported with an explicit `TermBank` parameter rather than C's `eqn->bank` back-pointer, and normalization follows the documented absolute-code ordering rather than the apparent `p_atom_compare` typo that makes the C `qsort` comparator non-strict.
- `cpr_dpllformula` parsing is exposed with an explicit Rust `TermBank` rather than C's borrowed-signature temporary bank, and active-clause indexes store clause indices rather than raw pointers. Formula printing preserves the C atom-debug typo that prints positive occurrence counts in both columns.
- `cpr_dpll` currently ports only the implemented state shell. The C `deactivate_clauses` and `shorten_clauses` helpers are empty stubs, `DPLLRetractLastAss` is declared but has no definition in `cpr_dpll.c`, and Rust does not invent the missing propagation/backtracking behavior in this slice.
- `pcl_expressions` ports expression-tree parsing/printing and is used by the current full-step, mini-step, protocol, proofcheck, and lemma-analysis surfaces. It preserves the C expression-position parser mismatch and TSTP position omission.
- `pcl_idents` stores live components in a Rust `Vec<i64>` rather than a sentinel-terminated `PDArray`, while preserving the sentinel at comparison boundaries used by the current full-step and protocol surfaces.
- `pcl_miniclauses` stores safe shared `Term` handles rather than borrowed raw `Term_p` arrays, uses `usize` rather than C's truncating `short literal_no`, preserves C's clause-property loss, and follows the C temporary-clause printing path used by `MiniClausePrint` and the current mini-step/protocol surfaces. Its Rust `MiniClausePrint` equivalent makes C's hidden output-format/problem-type globals explicit. Direct compact printer optimization remains deferred.
- `pcl_miniprotocol` stores steps in a safe `Vec<Option<PclMiniStep>>` rather than a raw-pointer `PDArray`, rejects duplicate ids by id rather than asserting same-pointer reinsertion, exposes scanner-comment forwarding through explicit output-aware parsing, and preserves mini-protocol quirks including positive-id-only protocol parsing, no-separator protocol printing, contiguous-suffix fast proof marking, and deterministic id-based precondition collection instead of pointer-tree ordering.
- `pcl_ministeps` uses an explicit shell-parse option rather than C's process-global `SupportShellPCL`, requires the caller-owned term bank for formula/clause rendering instead of storing `TB_p`, and preserves mini-protocol quirks including numeric-only ids, SQ-string-only extras, id-zero parse/free mismatch, and shell TSTP double-comma output.
- `pcl_positions` stores term paths in a Rust `Vec<i64>` rather than C's nullable `PDArray`, and preserves the legacy printer shape that omits dots before term-position components. Broader PCL2 protocol integration remains deferred.
- `pcl_protocol` stores steps in a sorted `Vec<PclStep>` rather than a raw-pointer `PTree` with cached `PStack`, rejects duplicates without reproducing C's pre-error count increment, exposes scanner-comment forwarding through explicit output-aware parsing, reports dangling precondition references as syntax diagnostics rather than allowing null parent pointers, preserves FOF stripping's justification-only reset without setting `PCLIsInitial`, and uses deterministic PCL-id precondition ordering instead of pointer-tree ordering.
- `pcl_steps` stores clausal logical content behind a Rust `Box<Clause>` rather than a raw union arm, requires a caller-owned term bank for formula/clause rendering instead of storing `TB_p`, and uses explicit shell-parse options rather than C's process-global `SupportShellPCL`. It preserves the stale `PCLType1` comment mismatch as documentation only, the `que` parser diagnostic omission, the `PCLTypeAxiom` versus `PCLIsInitial` TSTP-role distinction, full-step extras accepting `Name|PosInt` beyond mini-step `SQString`, formula-backed TPTP output without a final period, and shell logical-format omission markers plus warning side-channel wrappers.
- `ccl_subsumption` ClauseSet-owned FV-index lifecycle integration and remaining PCL/documentation side effects are still deferred until clause sets, FV indexes, documentation, and proof-state ownership are available. The simplify-reflect lookup path now reuses the represented demodulator-index unit helpers when indexed unit sets cover their clauses, and the strong-unit forward-subsumption option reaches proof-control forward subsumption and positive simplify-reflect through session state rather than a C-style process global.
- Simple types are represented with safe shared handles rather than raw `Type_p`; this preserves pointer identity for Rust-held values, but allocator address reuse and exact pointer ordering may differ from C.
- Term cells are represented with safe shared handles and interior mutability rather than raw flexible-array `TermCell` allocations. Ordinary variable binding dereference, global no-cache applied-variable `TermDeref` expansion, scoped bank-local no-WHNF applied-variable expansion, C-shaped `TermCheckConsistency` branch-cycle detection with an explicit progress-output wrapper, the DB-lambda `ApplyTerms`/`FreshVarWArgs`/`FlattenApps`/`UnfoldLambda`/`drop_args`/`find_min_db`/`LambdaEtaExpandDBTopLevel`/`LambdaEtaExpandDB`/`LambdaEtaReduceDB`/`LambdaNormalizeDB`/`CloseWithDBVar`/`CloseWithTypePrefix`/`ShiftDB`/`WHNF_step`/explicit-bank `WHNF_deref`/beta-normalization subset needed by pruning, comparison-local LFHO KBO6 weak-head dereferencing for `DEREF_ALWAYS`, no-bank KBO6 Lambda-order local weak-head reduction for simple DB-lambda applications, bank-backed KBO6 Lambda-order beta/eta comparison preparation, and the `MakeRewrittenTerm` retained-argument construction used by `TBTermPosReplace` are present, but WHNF cache behavior, binding caches, owner-bank metadata, and full LFHO bank insertion remain deferred until the complete lambda-normalization path is ported.
- `TermPosDebugPrint` supports both C modes for currently ported term surfaces: null-signature address output and term-bank-backed non-null-signature output that prints each superterm as `DEREF_NEVER...DEREF_ALWAYS` before the child index. The term-backed mode now inherits first-order `$let`/FOOL rendering and higher-order FOOL/lambda rendering; higher-order `$let` remains C-shaped ordinary application because `do_ho_print` does not call `print_let`.
- `TermFree`/`TermTopFree` ownership transfer is not exposed yet because Rust term handles are reference-counted; future term-bank integration must define when unshared term trees are intentionally consumed versus borrowed.
- Variable banks currently keep the default type handle from construction rather than a live mutable pointer to the whole `TypeBank`; parser integration should ensure variable banks are built after the shared type bank has the required default and user sorts.

## C Behaviors To Revisit After Compatibility

These notes are not permission to diverge during porting. They identify inherited C behaviors that may be good cleanup candidates after the Rust executable is demonstrably drop-in compatible.

- `DStr`, `PStack`, `DStack`, `PDArray`, `DDArray`, and `PDRangeArr` preserve C growth, assertion-shaped access, and mutating-access patterns. Later APIs may want clearer separation between read-only access, string-literal-specific delimiter stripping, and access that allocates or extends storage.
- `PDArray` element/assignment/increment helpers preserve C assertion failure behavior for negative indices; future checked accessors should live beside the compatibility methods.
- `DDArray` element/assignment helpers preserve C assertion failure behavior for negative indices; future checked accessors should live beside the compatibility methods.
- `PDRangeArr` element/assignment/increment helpers preserve C's mutating read shape for representable signed indices, while deletion stays checked and non-growing; the raw exported enlarge branch is also represented separately from ordinary checked access. Future lookup APIs should keep non-mutating range probes separate from the compatibility methods.
- `DDArraySelectPart` preserves C assertion failure behavior for invalid percentile/range requests; future checked statistics helpers should validate before calling the compatibility-shaped partition method.
- `PStack` pop/top/top-address/below-top/indexed access/assignment/discard helpers preserve C assertion failure behavior, and `PStackGetTopSP` preserves the signed `-1` empty-stack value. The current Rust `pop()` remains an option-returning owner-drain helper used throughout the port, while `pop_nonempty()` carries the C `PStackPop` precondition.
- `DStack` top/below-top/element/pop preserve C assertion failure behavior for invalid access; future Rust-only checked access should live beside those compatibility methods rather than weakening them.
- `clb_avlgeneric` is an obsolete name for generic binary-search-tree traversal; Rust keeps that naming only at the module boundary and uses handle-based traversal internally.
- `PStack`/`DStack` discard helpers intentionally use swap-remove behavior, which is efficient but order-destroying; keep auditing callers before exposing order-preserving variants.
- `ProofStateAnalyseGC` accumulates into `gc_count` and `gc_used_count` on every call instead of resetting or returning a pure report. Rust preserves this for compatibility, but later reporting APIs should make cumulative versus one-shot GC analysis explicit.
- `PList` raw-pointer anchors and extracted-cell ownership are represented with checked arena handles plus freed-slot reuse to match allocator-shaped reuse, and C `void` mutations now become panic-shaped raw compatibility operations in safe Rust. Stale handles can become valid for new cells; after list-heavy clause/formula code is ported, revisit whether a generational freelist, detached-cell owner type, or payload-borrowing list should replace this compatibility shape.
- `PLocalStack` intentionally keeps the C split between `ensure_space` and raw push/pop, including panic-shaped missed-capacity and empty-pop preconditions in the safe Rust compatibility surface. Later term traversal code may want scoped helper APIs that make missing ensures or empty pops impossible without losing the C growth profile; `PLocalStackTop` should also get a clearer Rust-only count name once compatibility wrappers are stable.
- `PQueue` exposes absolute internal ring indices, raw absolute-slot access, modulo advancement that can alias invalid raw indices, and the public grow routine that is only coherent for the full-ring state. Later callers should keep those details encapsulated behind safer traversal/capacity APIs.
- `FixedDArray` mirrors C assertion-style size and indexed-access contracts; callers fed by user input may eventually need recoverable checked paths beside the compatibility helpers.
- `MinHeap` preserves the C helper directions where `decr_key` drops down and `incr_key` bubbles up, despite those names appearing reversed for a conventional min-heap. It also preserves the uneven invalid-index shape where `remove` asserts, `update`/`incr` no-op for negative signed indices and empty root index `0` but assert positive out-of-range indices, and `decr` asserts negative signed indices but can no-op on positive out-of-range leaf-shaped indices. Rename or wrap these helpers only after all heap users are audited.
- `ObjTree` and `ObjMap` replace comparison-function/raw-pointer storage with ordered Rust keys and explicit ownership; revisit this once term, clause, and substitution arenas define stable handle identity and deleter responsibilities.
- `ClausePositiveSimplifyReflect` and `ClauseNegativeSimplifyReflect` are coupled to demodulator indexes and proof-documentation side effects in C. The Rust plain-set path keeps the literal-removal behavior linear while recording `DCSR` and exposes opt-in proof-doc wrappers through explicit sessions; revisit the indexed lookup and stable-parent coupling once indexes and proof documentation are owned by proof state.
- `ClauseInfoPrint` labels field `d6` as variable occurrences, but computes it through `ClauseWeight(..., max_term_multiplier=0, vweight=1, fweight=1, ...)`, including C's equality-predicate correction and orientation/maximality effects. Rust preserves the printed value; a later diagnostic-oriented API should either rename the field or compute direct variable occurrences.
- `StrongUnitForwardSubsumption` is process-global in C and changes positive-unit simplify behavior. Rust routes configured proof search through a `ProofControl` session flag plus explicit helper parameters; revisit only if later strategy scheduling needs C-global sharing semantics.
- `ClauseClauseSubsumptionCalls`, `ClauseClauseSubsumptionCallsRec`, `ClauseClauseSubsumptionSuccesses`, and `UnitClauseClauseSubsumptionCalls` are process-global mutable counters in C. Rust uses atomics for compatibility-shaped observation; move these into explicit proof/session statistics once the prover state owns saturation.
- `ClauseSetFindSubsumedClause` and `ClauseSetFindUnitSubsumedClause` accept a raw `Clause_p set_position` inside the set's linked list. Rust temporarily models this as a start index over the plain owner; replace it with stable clause handles when proof-state clause ownership is ported.
- FV-indexed subsumption in C finds leaf candidates through `PTree` root/left/right traversal and raw clause-pointer identity. Rust's current `FvIndex` searches cloned clause snapshots in deterministic identifier-keyed `BTreeMap` order so safe value moves can still delete indexed payloads; replace this with stable clause handles before treating indexed search order as proof-state-visible.
- `ClauseSet` owns the `fvindex` pointer in C and query wrappers silently branch on that embedded pointer. Rust now has an owned optional FV anchor and keeps explicit-anchor wrappers for transition tests; stable clause handles are still needed before long-lived FV-index payloads can stop cloning clause snapshots or depending on clause identifiers as their deletion key.
- `ccl_neweval` comparison treats equal `eval_count` values as fully equal before looking at heuristic values, and otherwise compares heuristic before falling back to FIFO count. Rust preserves that branch order; later typed evaluation handles should make duplicate counts impossible outside deliberate C-shaped imports.
- `ccl_neweval` stores heuristic values as `float` even though WFCB evaluation functions return `double`, so assignment truncates precision before ordering. Rust stores `f32` in `SimpleEvalCell`; revisit this only after reference tests establish whether higher precision changes clause selection.
