- `ccl_neweval` permits freeing evaluation cells without checking whether a tree still references them. Rust exposes the same detached-node/freeing shape through handle removal, but later clause-set owners should prefer ownership APIs that prevent dangling evaluation handles.
- Low-level OS-facing wrappers use POSIX `getrlimit`/`setrlimit`, `clock`, `sysconf`, `gettimeofday`, `getrusage`, `strerror`, and close-style APIs in C; Rust uses scoped unsafe external-library boundaries for Linux `getrlimit`/`setrlimit`, `strerror`, `clock`, `sysconf`, `signal`, Unix `close`, resource-footer `getrusage`, and Windows `Kernel32`/`CloseHandle` process timing, page size, physical memory, peak-working-set reporting, input-close ownership, and Job Object memory limiting, and keeps safe Linux `/proc` parsing only as a fallback. A retroactive unsafe-boundary audit confirms the current `unsafe` inventory is confined to runtime PicoSAT loading/ABI calls, native OS/CRT/DLL FFI wrappers, and their safe wrapper modules under the crate-level `#![deny(unsafe_code)]` policy; the runtime PicoSAT solver wrapper now has fake-ABI lifecycle coverage for init, trace enablement, sentinel clause export, solving, core extraction, reset, and drop without requiring a packaged solver library. Resource-limit APIs outside Linux and Windows still return explicit unsupported results. Keep future unsafe code similarly confined to external DLL/shared-library interop with documented invariants.
- `StrTree`, `FloatTree`, `NumTree`, `NumXTree`, `PTree`, and `QuadTree` model ordered semantics with safe Rust containers while documenting remaining splay-locality gaps; hot indexing paths should be benchmarked before deciding whether to recreate exact splay behavior.
- `RegMem` in C stores raw allocation pointers in a global `PTree` and frees them at process shutdown; the Rust handle registry preserves cleanup semantics, but later code should prefer explicit owner structs where persistent scratch memory can be scoped. `RegMemProvide` also relies on GNU `void*` byte arithmetic and zeroes only grown tails; Rust uses safe byte slices and fully initialized buffers until profiling proves a specialized scratch allocator is necessary.
- `IntMap` keeps C density transitions, null-slot quirks, and hidden read/delete-time array growth; the mutating lookup shape is a likely cleanup target if compatibility tests show no observable dependency.
- `ClauseIsTautologyReal` uses a fixed `MAX_EQ_TAUTOLOGY_CHECK_LITNO` cutoff of 1000 negative literals and falls back to only trivial tautology detection beyond that point; after compatibility is secured, this cost/coverage tradeoff should be revisited against real benchmark clauses.
- `ccl_tautologies` computes ground normal forms by repeatedly copying unshared terms, structurally matching complete rule sides, and reinserting changed sides into the term bank; this is intentionally simple C behavior, but a future rewrite-indexed or term-bank-aware normalizer may be worth profiling.
- `ClauseIsTautologyReal(..., copy=false)` in C mutates and then frees the caller-provided clause pointer; the Rust API keeps caller ownership explicit by using a shallow work clone for the no-copy term path.
- `clb_memory` and `clb_newmem` rely on exact-size frees, debug poisoning words, fatal out-of-memory control flow, distinct old/new flush behavior, and raw reuse of uninitialized blocks; Rust keeps initialized buffers, C-shaped fatal wrappers, recoverable `try_*` helpers, and explicit policies, so term/clause arena work should decide whether a specialized typed allocator is needed for comparable performance.
- Permanent strings use `Arc<str>` to keep Rust handles valid after registry clearing, while C `PermaStringsFree` invalidates every returned raw pointer and `PermaStringStore` consumes/frees the caller-owned input even on duplicate insertion. Later term/signature ownership should decide whether stable arena handles are a better identity model.
- `AtomSetFree` in `cpr_varset` drains a circular list cell-by-cell, and the public API permits `PLiteralNoLit` insertion even though extraction asserts on that payload. Rust keeps this behavior for compatibility; revisit a typed nonzero literal wrapper or contiguous worklist representation after DPLL caller behavior and performance are measured.
- `PropSigAtomNumber` in `cpr_propsig` exposes the raw stack pointer including the reserved null slot, while `PropSigGetAtomEnc` has lookup-time `StrTreeFind` reorganization and `PropSigInsertAtom` stores one raw name pointer in two containers. Rust keeps the public count and behavior but uses safe string ownership; later APIs should separate usable atom count, lookup-performance policy, and name interning explicitly.
- `p_atom_compare` in `cpr_propclauses` appears to compare `ABS(*a1)` to itself, making sort order depend on library `qsort` behavior instead of the documented atom-code order. Rust uses the documented order for a deterministic DPLL base; revisit this only with reference-output tests if legacy DPLL behavior turns out to depend on the typo.
- `DPLLRegisterClauseLiteral` in `cpr_dpllformula` grows atom storage using the signed literal while indexing by `ABS(lit)`, and `DPLLFormulaPrint` prints `pos_occur` twice instead of the negative occurrence count. Rust grows by absolute atom code to avoid a memory hazard and preserves the print typo only in legacy rendering; revisit both once the DPLL path has reference tests.
- `cpr_dpll` has no real propagation today: `deactivate_clauses` and `shorten_clauses` return zero, `DPLLAssignVar` therefore reports failure for every allocated assignment, and the negative-assignment branch still touches the same positive/negative active sets as the positive branch after negating the assignment. Real DPLL work should replace these stubs with reference-tested propagation and add the missing retraction implementation deliberately.
- `PCLExprParse` in `pcl_expressions` checks for an opening parenthesis before calling `PCL2PosParse`, which then expects a positive integer and fails without consuming the parenthesis; `PCLExprPrintTSTP` also ignores stored positions. Rust preserves both surfaces until reference PCL traces clarify whether this is dead code or a compatibility bug.
- `PCLIdParse` in `pcl_idents` parses fullstop-separated identifier components despite the source comment saying the list is separated by spaces, and `PCLIdCompare` subtracts `long` components before truncating to `int`. Rust preserves those compatibility surfaces in the current helper, but cleaned proof-object APIs should use explicit syntax comments and lexicographic ordering.
- `PCL2PosPrint` in `pcl_positions` omits dots before term-position components even though `PCL2PosParse` requires dotted components, and the header's optional-literal wording does not match the parser's mandatory leading positive integer. Rust preserves the output behavior and parser contract until PCL2 trace compatibility is established.
- `clb_simple_stuff` includes historical global-state behavior in `ProblemType` and JKISS random generation; Rust currently resets `ProblemType` around executable `run()` calls while preserving C-shaped first-order state during parsing and solving, but these states should move into an explicit proof-session context before parallel parsing or solving is introduced.
- Error handling is currently represented with structured `Diagnostic` values in many Rust paths; final executable compatibility still needs exact fatal-error stream, wording, and exit-status behavior.
- Partial-ordering symbol rendering should be audited once ordering output is wired in, because the C table intentionally omits printable strings for cache-only comparison results.
- `SysDateInc` mutates the date before its assertion, so incrementing the invalid sentinel produces the creation date and then fails; Rust exposes an asserting compatibility wrapper plus a reporting helper for diagnostic paths. The C signed-overflow case is undefined, while Rust reports or panics on overflow without wrapping.
- `SysDatePrint` prints signed `SysDate` with `%5lu`, effectively treating negative dates as unsigned `long` bit patterns on the reference platform. Keep this only for compatibility-sensitive rendering.
- `clb_verbose` relies on process-global mutable verbosity and direct `stderr` side effects. Rust currently centralizes this behind atomic state and writer-parameterized helpers; later session code should decide whether verbosity belongs in explicit run/session state.
- TSTP-mode executable parsing now also accepts legacy old-TPTP `input_clause(...)` and `input_formula(...)` records in the same entry loop, including app-encode's include-ignore path and ordinary include-selector recursion, matching C `FormulaAndClauseSetParse`'s mixed non-LOP dispatch. This mixed-dialect surface is a compatibility rule; a later full input owner should make dialect selection explicit and isolate old-wrapper acceptance.
- `eprover.c` routes nearly all ordinary output through process-global `GlobalOut` and separately caches `fileno(GlobalOut)` for low-memory/signal descriptor writes. It also opens/truncates the configured output path before most executable work, treats `-o -` as stdout, and reports open failures through C's two-line `SysError` shape. Rust keeps the initial run wrapper test-capturable for stdout, opens configured files locally, and mirrors the descriptor side channel where the platform can do so with safe ownership; once the full parser/prover pipeline owns output, decide whether these output-routing and descriptor-cache behaviors remain compatibility-only or become explicit session state.
- `eprover.c` drives syntax-only parsing through the normal proof-state scanner and formula/clause parser pipeline. Rust's non-printing `--syntax-only` now uses the proof-state formula-owner path for supported inputs and exits before preprocessing/CNF/search, `--print-formulas` now renders supported proof-state `f_axioms`, and `--app-encode` now writes supported formula entries from proof-state `f_axioms` while echoing and ignoring bare app-encode includes like C. Remaining full higher-order and helper-style callers should be rejoined with the full proof-state/formula/list/`let`/`ite` parser owners once they are ported.
- C handles old TPTP `input_formula(...)` and TSTP `fof(...)`/`tff(...)`/`tcf(...)`/`thf(...)` parsing through term-encoded `TFormula` owners and the CNF pipeline before clauses enter proof state. Rust's executable-level formula parser is deliberately narrower and currently performs C-shaped wrapper-role validation, old-TPTP formula role defaulting, represented old-TPTP same-precedence right-recursive binary operator parsing with quantifiers scoped to the next elementary formula unless parenthesized, old-TPTP FOOL-primary existential-body parsing through that elementary-formula path, represented old-TPTP equality/disequality literals in proof-search/CNF/prune/syntax-only/print/app-encode formula-owner paths, represented non-printing syntax-only role preservation, old-TPTP proof-search closure for supported `<~>`, `~&`, and `~|` connectives, top-level THF type-declaration signature mutation, first-order-shaped, application-based, and quantified predicate-application THF formula lowering, typed THF quantified variable declarations, and explicit full higher-order formula-pipeline diagnostics for lambda-valued/full higher-order fragments, C-shaped TSTP free-variable rejection, direct grouped/unparenthesized precedence-limited connective, grouped conjunctions/disjunctions with supported existential conjuncts or disjuncts, conjunctive disjuncts containing supported existential conjuncts, simple universal-scope preservation, direct positive atomic, typed TFF/TCF quantified variables with explicit temporary scoped name shadow/restore behavior, including same-sort same-name quantifier shadowing needed before the full C variable-renaming pipeline is available, `$true`/`$false` truth-constant lowering, unparenthesized unitary including FOOL-primary existential bodies, and parenthesized existential Skolemization including nested existential scopes and quantified operands in positive or negative polarity, supported negated-universal/existential conjecture lowering, implication/equivalence-side supported existential lowering, negative-polarity NAND/NOR existential lowering, negated-universal Skolemization for supported conjecture clauses, atomic, truth-constant, positive/direct-negated/parenthesized-negated `$distinct`, unitary, and parenthesized existential conjecture negation, grouped implication-side, grouped equivalence-side, parenthesized-negation, first-order type-declaration signature mutation, same-type `$distinct` expansion over constants, including C's variable-looking zero-arity constants and supported top-level TCF/THF wrapper bodies, FOF/TFF/THF embedded `$distinct` expansion, and C-shaped embedded TCF `$distinct` rejection through the typed-clause literal parser, plus limited CNF distribution only where the clause result is unambiguous over supported fragments. Positive universal and nested existential scopes under a positive existential are bridged with an explicit active-universal stack (TSTP rejects globally free variables before lowering, while old TPTP bridge entries still seed that stack from globally free formula variables), filtered to variables that occur in the existential body, and grouped conjunction/disjunction/existential, negated-universal/existential, implication/equivalence/NAND/NOR operands now reuse that polarity-aware lowering. The parser also preserves supported connective variants through parsing so app-encoded formula output can keep C's source spellings before semantic clause lowering. It mirrors C's mutable scoped external-name environment only for supported quantifier scopes, and first-order type declarations now retain C's skipped `$true` formula placeholder for supported proof-search/CNF/prune/syntax-only formula-owner, nonempty, and app-encode declaration behavior. Remaining bridge-backed callers should be replaced with the real formula owner, simplification, negation-normal-form conversion, dependency-aware Skolemization, definition introduction, explicit scoped binding objects rather than parser-global variable-bank state, typed formula placeholders, broader TCF-specific formula ownership beyond the C clause-body grammar, and CNF extraction path before treating FOF/TFF/TCF/THF input as complete.
- The first-order application shim for `p @ a`, `(p) @ a`, `(h @ a) @ b = c`, `((h @ a) @ b) = c`, `c = (h @ a) @ b`, `c = ((h @ a) @ b)`, `c = h @ a @ b`, `c = (g) @ a`, `(&) @ p(a) @ q(a)`, `(~) @ p(a)`, and beta-reducible `(^[X: $i]: p @ X) @ a` / `((^[X: $i]: f @ X) @ a) = b` is intentionally typed and narrow: it recognizes C token patterns before the ordinary atom, parenthesized-formula, or equality-RHS path, then reuses the staged term-bank TSTP parser and bridge lowering. A full formula owner should replace this executable lookahead with normal formula application parsing and explicit typed predicate/logical-head/lambda normalization.
- The temporary THF application bridge recovers declared application heads when the executable's first-order parse-local problem-type state would create `_ARITYFIX0` placeholders for `p @ a`, and executable proof/prune parsing now reactivates the aggregate parsed dialect before auto-classification, preprocessing, and term indexing. Replace these compatibility shims by threading the wrapper/problem type through term parsing and proof-state setup when the full formula owner/parser path lands.
- C's `quantified_tform_tstp_parse` reads each binder with `TBTermParse`, so typed binder annotations depend on the active process-global problem type and the resulting free variable may later serve as an application head. Rust's temporary TSTP formula parser now uses explicit higher-order type parsing for binder annotations and bypasses signature lookup for free-variable application heads to support arrow-typed THF binders under the executable's first-order bridge; the full parser should carry dialect/problem-type context explicitly instead of relying on process-global `problemType` or this local compatibility shim.
- Parser-supported formula-level Boolean `$ite`/`$let` owners, typed Boolean FOOL equality/disequality formulas, FOOL term arguments, and top-level/connective/negated/quantified non-Boolean FOOL equality/disequality operands now route through represented formula owners for print/syntax, app-encode, and proof/CNF paths, with proof/CNF inputs promoted to higher-order when FOOL terms remain so the represented `FormulaSetCNF2` route performs ITE/LET lifting. Remaining bridge-backed FOOL spellings still lower direct positive Boolean `$ite(C,T,F)` predicate literals through the C `do_ite_unroll` formula-position shape `(~C|T)&(C|F)`, and direct literals containing a term-position `$ite` lower the first occurrence on the left side before the right side into `(~C|L[T])&(C|L[F])`, matching C's direct literal-side scan for the supported bridge. Direct `$let` occurrences through supported quantifier/connective scopes lift before CNF by allocating fresh `epred`/`esk` definitions over captured and formal variables, replacing body occurrences, and clausifying Boolean definitions as equivalences or term definitions as equalities with free variables in generated definition clauses carrying the expected universal clause meaning; conjecture lowering keeps those generated definitions positive while negating only the rewritten conjecture body. App-encode and syntax-only printing still keep the original term encoding, while non-printing syntax-only exits before CNF lifting. The simple formula parser has a special primary-formula branch, reused by unparenthesized existential-body parsing, so equality and disequality around Boolean FOOL atoms are represented as equivalence and XOR instead of term equality, while non-Boolean `$ite`/`$let` primary terms remain valid only when immediately followed by `=` or `!=` and then lower as ordinary term literals. A full formula owner should model these as typed formula/term nodes, with explicit predicate-literal, `ite`/`let`, and term equality/disequality lowering steps instead of depending on predicate-position checks to recognize them.
- Formula-owner CNF helper coverage now includes typed top-level term-valued `$let` equality sources such as `($let(f:$i, f := a, f) = b)` for `classify_problem`, `eground`, `enormalizer`, and `epatternize`, plus a direct `FormulaSetCNF2` regression that runs LET lifting together with FOOL unrolling over generated term-definition wrappers. Executable proof-search coverage now also includes represented existential FOF FOOL conjectures with formula-position `$ite` bodies and term-valued `$let` equality bodies.
- TSTP formula-level `=`/`!=` in the temporary executable bridge now also covers ordinary atomic predicate left operands when the right side starts with a formula-only operand token such as a parenthesized formula, quantifier, negation, truth constant, or `$distinct`, and any typed Boolean atomic-left operand regardless of whether the right side starts as an ordinary atom, `$ite`, or `$let`. This mirrors C's `literal_tform_tstp_parse` plus outer `TFormulaTSTPParse` operator split for forms like `p(a) = (q(a)|r(a))` and `p(a) != ![X]:q(X)`, as well as C `EqnParseInfix`'s Boolean-left `TFormulaTSTPParse` RHS branch for typed `p(a) = q(a)`, `p(a) = $ite(...)`, and `p(a) != $let(...)`, while preserving ordinary term equality when the left side is not Boolean and the right side starts as a term. A full formula owner should replace this lookahead shim with a typed formula parser that records whether `=`/`!=` was source-spelled term equality or Boolean equivalence/XOR.
- C distinguishes the real formula Boolean type `$o` from user sorts that may be named `bool`. In `TFormulaTSTPParse`/`EqnParseInfix`, a non-`$o` application followed by `=`/`!=` remains term equality even if a user sort is named `bool`; the Rust bridge mirrors this with a local term-equality detour, but the full formula owner should encode the source token flow and type distinction directly.
- The C formula owner treats `$let` definition-head variables as scoped formals while later lifting collects formal and captured variables into generated definitions. Rust now preserves parser-supported represented first-order print/syntax/proof-CNF `$let` owners, including direct Boolean bodies, term arguments, and non-Boolean equality operands; proof/CNF routing promotes those owners to higher-order so represented `FormulaSetCNF2` carries LET lifting, generated-definition metadata, and proof-document side effects. Supported `--app-encode` shares represented parsing ownership but exits before lifting, and the temporary bridge keeps the same free-variable/dependency scans for remaining full higher-order and helper paths. The full owner should still make let-local formal scope, captured-variable dependencies, generated-definition derivation/archive metadata, and broader owner-side lifting explicit instead of inferring them from term-encoded cells.
- The app-encode formula-owner path renders internal Boolean `$let` cells back to a TPTP-style `$let(...)` while recursively app-encoding formula-valued definitions and bodies. Because let-local symbols still live in the shared parser signature, their symbol declarations can leak into the top-level app-encoded declaration prelude; a full parser-owned formula path should make let-local declaration scope explicit before deciding whether to preserve that leakage for compatibility.
- Old TPTP `TFormulaTPTPParse` has no usual precedence ladder for binary formula operators and gives quantifiers only an elementary body by default. Rust preserves that for `input_formula(...)`; a later cleaned parser should keep dialect-specific precedence and quantifier scope visible in the API instead of sharing one generic FOF helper across old TPTP and TSTP.
- C handles `-` input through `CreateScanner(StreamTypeFile, "-", ...)`, preserving stdin as a file-typed stream with source name `-`. The Rust syntax-only path now preserves that file-shaped source identity while still reading stdin into an in-memory stream first, so true streaming behavior should be revisited after file-scanner parity is complete.
- `InitIO` stores `TPTP_dir` in process-global state, calls `InitError(progname)` to update the shared diagnostic program name, and `ExitIO` clears only the directory field. Rust initializes and clears the include-root state around each executable `run()` and now propagates `init_io` program names into the shared error renderer so tests and repeated in-process runs see C-shaped diagnostic names; keep the include-root guard as a compatibility shim until the parser/session owner can carry include roots explicitly.
- `eprover.c` concatenates the hard/soft CPU-limit diagnostic fragments without spaces (`softtime` and `hardtime`). Rust preserves those messages for compatibility; they are obvious cleanup candidates once exact diagnostic wording can be relaxed.
- `--proof-graph` documentation describes graph levels 1-3, but the C option handler accepts any integer and stores it as `print_derivation = arg + 1`. Rust records that C-shaped value and now renders represented clause-side DOT graphs for graph modes, including detailed derivation/source-info labels for represented clause nodes at graph levels above 1; out-of-range graph modes still need a compatibility decision once full derivation printing is ported.
- `--proof-object=0` still raises C's internal `print_derivation` selector to `POList`, but the final proof-object block is gated by `PrintProofObject`, so it prints no proof object unless another proof-output option has enabled that gate. Rust preserves the no-output surface; keep the split gate/selector model visible when full derivation output is ported.
- `--answers` has a process default of one answer but an optional-argument default of `2147483647`, so spelling `--answers` without a value changes the search contract dramatically. Rust records that shape; later user-facing configuration may want a clearer spelling for unlimited or many-answer search.
- `--cnf` is documented as nearly equivalent to `--print-saturated=eigEIG --processed-clauses-limit=0`, but the C handler actually sets the descriptor to `teigEIG`, including type declarations, sets `proc_limit = 0`, and exits after CNF output rather than treating the limit as resource exhaustion. Rust now records and bridges that behavior for supported clause-list input and supported formula-owner input after represented CNF conversion; keep the help text discrepancy in mind when full help output is ported.
- `--filter-saturated` advertises an optional default descriptor of `Fc`, but the C handler validates filter descriptors against `eigEIGaA`, so using the option without an explicit compatible value trips the same usage error. Rust preserves the handler result; this default/validator mismatch should be cleaned up after compatibility is established.
- `SetMemoryLimit()` calls `SetSoftRlimitErr(RLIMIT_DATA, mem_limit, "RLIMIT_AS")` inside the `RLIMIT_AS` branch instead of passing `RLIMIT_AS` as the resource. Rust now mirrors that duplicated `RLIMIT_DATA` behavior on Linux; fix the resource mismatch only if exact C bugs are later relaxed.
- `set_limits()` mixes a raw `perror("eprover")` line with ordinary `Warning(...)` output when `RLIMIT_CORE` setup fails. Rust preserves that two-line shape through a resource-setup message path; keep this split explicit when the final executable diagnostic layer is unified.
- `--pcl-shell-level` has a process default of `0` but an optional-argument default of `1`, so spelling the option without a value changes the proof-output suppression level. Rust records this; a later user-facing interface may want a clearer `default` versus `enabled` distinction.
- TPTP and TSTP output switches do not reset equation-printing globals consistently: `--tptp-out` and `--tptp-format` clear full-equational representation and disable infix printing, while `--tstp-out` and `--tstp-format` re-enable infix printing but leave any earlier full-equational setting untouched. Rust preserves this ordering-sensitive behavior for compatibility; normalize it only after reference-output tests cover the affected modes.
- `--print-types` flips C's process-global `TermPrintTypes` flag, which full conventional term printers read implicitly while compact term-bank abbreviation printing ignores it. Rust threads this as an explicit print option for supported clause output and app-encoded formula output; keep it explicit when porting proof/formula printers unless byte-compatible global-state emulation is required.
- `--auto-schedule` and `--satauto-schedule` are guarded by the same `strategy_scheduling` flag, so the first one wins and later schedule options are silently ignored. In particular, `--satauto-schedule --auto-schedule=4` never sets SInE to `Auto`. Rust records this order dependence; later configuration code may want explicit conflict diagnostics after compatibility is established.
- C's `ExecuteScheduleMultiCore` forks child prover processes from the current proof-search state and the parent prints the first winning child output before exiting with that child's status. Rust now models the parent-side process supervisor by re-executing the current binary as selected preprocessing/search workers and replaying the winning worker output/status through both scheduling layers, but it does not yet inherit already-parsed/preprocessed state into children or reproduce exact forked CPU/resource accounting. Keep those concerns isolated while exact fork-state compatibility is audited.
- `--goal-defs` has a help string that says negative-side goal definitions are "to be implemented", but the C option handler still records and applies a `Neg` mode by setting only `add_goal_defs_neg`. Rust preserves the handler state and now applies the selected polarity in the represented clause-level goal-definition transform; revisit the user-visible mode list only after compatibility output and strategy tests cover the mode surface.
- `--ac-handling` has a process default of `ACDiscardAll`, while spelling `--ac-handling` with no argument changes the state to `ACKeepUnits`. The C help text also describes the default as equivalent to `DiscardAll`. Rust preserves both defaults; a later configuration layer may want to make the implicit argument clearer.
- `--no-eq-unfolding` disables unfolding by storing `LONG_MIN` in `eqdef_incrlimit` instead of using a separate boolean. Rust records this with the reference LP64-shaped `i64::MIN`; if native Windows C compatibility matters, audit C `long` width before making the sentinel externally visible.
- `ParseIntLimited` accepts the exact LP64 `LONG_MIN` token by parsing the magnitude through the scanner's unsigned numeric field before assigning back to `long`, while treating `-0` as underflow. Rust now mirrors that boundary so generated strategies with `eqdef_incrlimit: -9223372036854775808` parse instead of aborting before search.
- `--literal-selection-strategy` help tells users to run `-W none` for a full selector list, but `none` is not a valid selector; the C code intentionally falls into the invalid-argument diagnostic to print all names. Rust preserves that validation path, but the final help output may want a dedicated list command.
- `--filter-orphans-limit`, `--forward-contract-limit`, `--delete-bad-limit`, `--split-clauses`, `--disequality-decomposition`, and `--disequality-decomp-maxarity` all have optional-argument defaults that differ from their process defaults, so spelling the option without `=value` actively changes proof search. Rust records those defaults; later configuration docs should distinguish process defaults from option-present defaults.
- `--define-weight-function` and `--define-heuristic` accumulate raw definition strings during C option parsing and are postprocessed only later when heuristic definitions are installed. Rust preserves the parse-order list shape; later heuristic code should make the postprocessing phase explicit.
- `--free-numbers` sets integer, rational, and float symbols free as one combined property mask. Rust records and now applies the combined mask in supported proof-state parsing, including non-printing syntax-only; revisit whether user-facing configuration should expose those numeric classes separately after full parser/type-checker integration.
- `--definitional-cnf` has a process default of `24`, while `--miniscope-limit` has a process default of `1048576` but an option-present default of `2147483648`, and the C help prose mentions another built-in miniscope default. Rust records the handler defaults; later CNF documentation should clarify which defaults are compatibility requirements.
- `--app-encode` is not just a passive encoding flag in C: it enables the app-encoded formula output path, writes to `stdout` even when `GlobalOut` is redirected, diverts bare includes through `ignore_include` by echoing them to stdout without loading the referenced files and rejecting selector syntax, expands `$distinct`, runs auto-mode preprocessing selection, SInE, and relevance pruning, then app-encodes the remaining formula set, skips top-level `$true` formulas, preserves source-level binary connective spellings in the printed formula path, parses clause owners before rendering only the formula set, and exits before pruning or proof search. Rust now preserves that print-and-exit shape for supported TPTP/TSTP formula entries by populating proof-state `f_axioms` and rendering them with `FormulaSetAppEncode`, including app-encode include echo/no-load behavior, generated auto preprocessing class/configuration output before the print-only branch, explicit `--print-types` suffixes on app-encoded term output, represented old-TPTP `input_formula(...)` app-encode owners that keep old-dialect formula equality/disequality as `=`/`!=`, represented non-`$distinct` TSTP `tff(...)`/`tcf(...)`/`thf(...)` and plain/application/typed-equality/unparenthesized-quantified-body-equality/bare-negated-left-equality/application-left-equality/applied-lambda FOF app-encode owners that share the staged `WFormula` app-encoding path, represented top-level `$distinct(...)` bodies expanded through proof-state `$distinct` processing before rendering, and represented formula-owner SInE/relevance pruning before rendering. Parsed-but-discarded non-watchlist `cnf(...)`/`input_clause(...)` entries may still affect shared-signature declarations and `--error-on-empty`, and watchlist-only app-encode input remains empty for `--error-on-empty` like C's `ProofStateAxNo` check. A later full implementation should decide whether the stdout-bypass, print-only auto classifier/scheduler work, clause-side declaration leakage, remaining FOF app-encode bridge shims, embedded `$distinct` bridge expansion, and spelling-preserving formula-rendering behavior should remain compatibility-only quirks.
- Type-only `--app-encode` inputs now use the same skipped `$true` owner shape, so app-encoded type/symbol declarations can print without adding visible formula entries.
- `--arg-cong`, `--neg-ext`, and `--pos-ext` all accept `off` in the C handler, while help/error text talks mostly about `all` and `max`; the invalid `--arg-cong` diagnostic even names `neg-ext` and says "excepts". Rust preserves the accepted values and diagnostic wording for now. In the checked `ComputeHOInferences` source, `pos_ext` is only reached under the `neg_ext` gate, so `pos_ext` alone is effectively ignored; Rust mirrors that by running PosExt only when `neg_ext` also enables the C gate until higher-order reference traces decide whether to fix it.
- `ComputeNegExt` creates a fresh typed Skolem symbol for each nonempty prefix application instead of reusing one witness across all prefixes. Rust preserves that emitted-clause and symbol sequence for compatibility; revisit only with higher-order traces that cover generated Skolem names and proof shape.
- `term_drop_last_arg` reconstructs a PosExt prefix with a widened arrow type built from the dropped argument type and the current residual type, and returns the phony-application head directly for binary phony applications. Rust preserves that type-shaping behavior for represented PosExt; later term-bank APIs should make prefix/witness type ownership clearer after higher-order traces pin down sharing effects.
- `EliminateLeibnizEquality` only scans non-equational predicate literals whose left side is an applied free variable, builds a full DB-lambda binding for each eligible argument position, and can record a `DCNormalize` derivation entry before `DCLeibnizElim` when encoded equality is lifted. Rust preserves those shapes for compatibility; revisit the accepted literal class and derivation layering only after higher-order Leibniz reference traces exist.
- `--classification-timeout-portion` is missing a `break` in `eprover.c`, so a numerically valid value falls through to `--preinstantiate-induction` and is then rejected as a non-boolean argument. Rust preserves the validation result; decide later whether the timeout option should become usable behind a compatibility switch.
- `--prim-enum-mode` accepts `logsym` in the `eprover.c` option switch, while the HCB parser/pretty-printer uses `logsymbol`. Rust preserves the executable option spelling; reconcile the two names only after strategy-file parsing and CLI compatibility are both covered.
- C `strategy_io` handles `--parse-strategy`, `--select-strategy`, and `--print-strategy` after input parsing, optional preprocessing scheduling, and CNF setup, and `--print-strategy` exits from that point. Rust now preserves the parse-before-select HCB stack side effect by carrying a parsed strategy file's `heuristic_def` into proof-control HCB installation before the final selected parameter cell adds its own definition. Rust intentionally prints strategies before entering input parsing for the current executable bridge so the option does not depend on unsupported parser/preprocessing state; revisit the timing once formula parsing and strategy scheduling are ported if any side effect before printing becomes compatibility-relevant.
- `che_new_autoschedule` includes the generated `schedule.vars` C initializer directly and scans `conf_map` linearly for every named strategy lookup. Rust currently includes the same generated source as compatibility data and parses only `conf_map`; a generated Rust table or build-time extractor may be cleaner and faster after strategy-table reference tests pin the exact formatting and update workflow.
- `cco_sine` embeds generated raw-class Auto SInE limits and parallel class/filter arrays directly in source. Rust preserves that table for the current report surface; after full generalized SInE compatibility is covered, prefer deriving it from checked generated data or shared schedule metadata instead of maintaining another hand-copied table.
- C `ProofStateSinE` moves selected raw clause/formula pointers out of their old sets and into fresh proof-state owners after every filter type. Rust now applies threshold, GSinE, and `LambdaDef` to represented proof-state clause and formula owners in the supported proof-search/CNF/prune path, moving clauses by selected identifier and formulas by stable wrapper entry id through explicit source/destination sets. Non-printing `--syntax-only` reaches represented owners but exits before SInE. Remaining clause-bridge paths and future pointer-owner-discovered duplicate relinking still need stable clause/formula handles before every executable path is fully C-shaped.
- The Rust executable currently preserves a local `NoSInE` no-op sentinel used by existing auto-mode replay tests before calling the C-shaped resolver. The checked C source does not list `NoSInE` in `AxFilterDefaultSet`, so verify this against the intended strategy-file surface before treating it as a public compatibility guarantee.
- `che_hcb` string macros are permissive around invalid raw enum values: `EIT2STR` maps anything other than `AllLits` or `MaxLits` to `off`, `UM2STR` maps anything other than `SingleUnif` to `multi`, and `PEM2STR` is the only one that renders out-of-range values as `unknown`. Rust keeps raw helpers for imported C-shaped data; later typed configuration should avoid representing invalid enum states.
- `che_hcb` initializes BCE, predicate-elimination, and `lambda_demod` fields but the C print/parse paths deliberately skip those fields in the current source; Rust preserves the stored defaults and omitted strategy-file surface until full proof-control integration decides whether exposing them would be a compatibility break or a cleanup.
- `che_hcb` `HeuristicParmsParseInto` is an ordered sparse override parser: missing expected fields leave existing values untouched and later expected fields may still be parsed. Rust preserves this instead of normalizing the strategy block into order-independent key/value parsing.
- `che_hcb` `split_clauses` is a raw `SplitClassType` bitmask, not a closed enum: combinations such as `SplitHorn | SplitNonHorn` are accepted by both the executable option handler and strategy parser. Rust now preserves arbitrary C-width bit patterns in `SplitClassType`; future APIs should expose named flag helpers without rejecting legacy masks.
- `che_hcb` stores `sine`, `heuristic_name`, and `heuristic_def` through the C permanent-string store when parsing, while `HeuristicParmsFree` frees only the parameter cell. Rust currently owns the optional strings directly; revisit only if strategy parsing shows observable string identity/lifetime behavior or allocation cost.
- `che_hcb` prints absent `sine` as `"None"` and absent `heuristic_def` as `""`, and then parses those values back as present strings. Rust preserves this non-idempotent default print/parse shape for strategy compatibility.
- `che_hcb` parses `mem_limit` with `ParseIntMax`, which negates positive decimal literals before assigning to unsigned `rlim_t`, so positive strategy values wrap to large unsigned limits on LP64-shaped targets. Rust keeps the wrapping conversion isolated behind the parser.
- `finalize_auto_parms` computes an automatic `delete_bad_limit` as `(float)(mem_limit-2)*0.7`, narrowing the unsigned resource limit to single precision before assigning to `long long`, and prints the selected heuristic parameters before that adjustment. Rust preserves that precision loss and output ordering in the finalization helpers; a cleaned resource policy should avoid deriving integer limits through `float`.
- `che_hcb` `HCBFree` deliberately does not free WFCBs because they are owned by `WFCBAdmin`; the Rust HCB stores admin handles and only runs its own optional HCB data cleanup. Keep that ownership split when clause evaluation is wired through the admin.
- `che_hcb` `HCBAddWFCB` stores cumulative switch counts (`steps` added to the prior last switch), not per-WFCB step widths, and switches the selector from single-weight to standard as soon as a second WFCB is added. Rust preserves the cumulative schedule.
- `che_hcb` `HCBClauseEvaluate` asserts a clause has no existing evaluations, allocates exactly one evaluation slot per WFCB, and then writes evaluations in HCB WFCB-list order. Rust preserves the one-slot-per-WFCB storage and also keeps the explicit adapter for callers that have not moved to clause-owned evaluations yet.
- `che_hcb` `HCBStandardClauseSelect` advances `select_count/current_eval` after finding/removing the current best candidate, using equality against cumulative switch boundaries and resetting only when `current_eval == wfcb_no`. Rust preserves that state update in the low-level injected-predicate helper, while proof-control drives the ported compact-parent orphan check by default from a proof-state liveness snapshot.
- `che_hcb` `HCBClauseSetDelProp` appears to use the inner-loop variable `j` as the `select_switch` array index for that same loop's bound. Rust preserves that compiled behavior in the compatibility helper; repair the likely typo only behind reference tests or a deliberate compatibility break.
- `che_hcbadmin` stores heuristic names and HCB pointers in parallel stacks and searches from the end, so duplicate heuristic names intentionally redefine earlier ones. Rust preserves last-definition lookup; a map-based registry would need to retain redefinition ordering.
- `HeuristicDefListParse` returns the current stack size if it parses nothing, but returns the zero-based index of the last parsed definition when it does parse. Rust preserves this mixed return shape; expose a clearer parsed-count helper only as an additional API.
- `parse_single_wfcb_item` accepts both `2*Weight` and `2.Weight` forms, and treats an identifier followed by `(` as an inline anonymous weight-function definition rather than a named lookup. Rust preserves both grammar shortcuts; parser cleanup should keep strategy-file compatibility mappings.
- `GetHeuristic` only validates trailing tokens for inline definitions that start with `(`; named lookup consumes the first identifier and ignores remaining tokens. Rust preserves this asymmetry, including treating `Name=(...)` as a lookup for `Name` rather than a definition.
- `GetHeuristic` calls `HeuristicDefParse` before checking for trailing tokens, so a malformed inline definition such as `(1*W) extra` can add or shadow the `Default` HCB before the syntax error is reported. Rust preserves the side effect; a cleaned parser should make this transactional only behind a compatibility decision.
- `SelectNoLiterals` and `SelectNoGeneration` have the same no-op body in C and rely on `DoLiteralSelection` to have already cleared `EPIsSelected`. Rust preserves both names as separate selectors so the proof-process `SelectNoGeneration` checks can still distinguish the strategy even though the selector body is identical.
- `PSelectFirstVariableLiteral` only selects positive literals after finding a negative pure-variable literal; it does not select the `X!=Y` literal despite the C function comment. Rust follows the body, not the comment.
- `SelectLargestOrientable`, `PSelectLargestOrientable`, `MSelectLargestOrientable`, `SelectSmallestOrientable`, `PSelectSmallestOrientable`, and `MSelectSmallestOrientable` call maximal-term marking for its orientation side effects, then prefer oriented negative literals over unoriented negatives. The smallest variants' comments say the no-orientable fallback selects the largest literal, but the C code uses a strict smallest-standard-weight comparison; Rust follows the code.
- `PSelectGroundNegativeLiteral` temporarily selects positive literals while scanning, but clears every selected bit if no negative ground literal exists. Rust keeps that source behavior; change it only if reference strategy tests prove the comment-intended behavior is safe.
- `lit_sel_diff_weight` is a macro that recomputes `100*EqnStandardDiff+EqnStandardWeight` at each comparison site. Rust shares the helper but preserves strict first-best tie behavior; later performance work can cache side weights if selector-level reference tests keep tie order stable.
- `PSelectOptimalLiteral` and `PSelectMinOptimalLiteral` only select positive literals in their fallback paths; when a ground negative candidate exists, the C bodies select only that negative literal despite comments saying positives are selected too. Rust preserves the bodies and records the comments as cleanup candidates after reference strategy tests exist.
- `SelectCondOptimalLiteral` and `PSelectCondOptimalLiteral` use the raw gate `TermStandardWeight <= TermWeight(vweight=0, fweight=3)` on positive literals, with right-side inclusion only for equational literals. `SelectAllCondOptimalLiteral` starts from `found=true`, so a clause with no positive literals clears selected bits instead of falling through to optimal selection. Rust preserves that source-level gate and documents the prose/formula mismatch for later cleanup.
- The depth-restricted optimal selectors are named `SelectOptimalRestrDepth2`, `SelectOptimalRestrPDepth2`, and `SelectOptimalRestrNDepth2` in the C strategy table even though the function names are `SelectDepth2OptimalLiteral`, `SelectPDepth2OptimalLiteral`, and `SelectNDepth2OptimalLiteral`. Rust follows the public table names.
- `SelectUnlessUniqMax`, `SelectUnlessPosMax`, `SelectUnlessUniqPosMax`, and `SelectUnlessUniqMaxPos` all depend on maximality marking. Rust keeps a bankless `DoLiteralSelection` helper for selectors that do not need the term bank and exposes `do_literal_selection_with_bank` for proof-control paths that own the live term bank. That banked wrapper now uses mutable-bank maximal marking for the table-visible ordering-dependent selector families, including largest/smallest-orientable, unless-maximal, complex unique-max Horn, MaxLComplex, base NewComplex, MinInfpos, AHP/VGNonCR, and CQ/UnlessPDom selectors. Full C selector-name coverage is present; the lower-level bankless helper only reports a diagnostic if a banked/ordering-dependent selector is reached without the required ordering/term-bank context, while legacy immutable selector helpers remain for compatibility callers outside proof-control. The `UniqPosMax` and `UniqMaxPos` variants intentionally differ when one positive maximal and another negative maximal literal coexist.
- `PSelectComplex` and `PSelectLComplex` only select positive literals in the diff-weight fallback path, not when a pure-variable or ground negative candidate is found. Rust preserves the C bodies and records the comment mismatch for future strategy documentation.
- `SelectComplexPreferNEQ` and `SelectComplexPreferEQ` stop scanning at the first lower-priority or non-improving negative literal instead of continuing to search later candidates. Rust keeps the early-break scan because it is observable in selector-level traces.
- `SelectDiversificationLiterals` and `SelectDiversificationPreferIntoLiterals` use a process-global `literal_weight_counter` that increments for every literal, including positives, then takes negative-literal weights modulo `clause->neg_lit_no`; the prefer-into variant first prioritizes `EPIsPMIntoLit`. Rust uses an atomic global for safe shared state, but parallel proof search should revisit whether this C-wide sequence should remain global, become proof-control-local, or gain deterministic reset policy.
- `SelectAntiRROptimalLiteral` has an explicit zero-negative-literal guard, while `PSelectAntiRROptimalLiteral` relies on `DoLiteralSelection` to avoid no-negative calls. Rust keeps that asymmetry because direct selector-call behavior differs in debug builds.
- `PSelectStrongRRNonRROptimalLiteral` clears all selected bits when a clause is range-restricted but not strongly range-restricted. That is redundant after `DoLiteralSelection` clears state before dispatch, but Rust preserves the source-level cleanup for direct selector calls.
- `GetLitSelName` maps back from function pointers by scanning the selector table, so two table entries sharing a function pointer would print as the first name. The current C no-op entries use distinct functions with identical bodies; Rust should keep reverse selector names table-driven rather than inferred from behavior.
- `ProofControlAlloc` eagerly initializes PicoSAT with trace generation even before any SAT checks are requested, and `ProofControlResetSATSolver` tears that solver down and immediately creates another one. Rust advances the same lifecycle state around completed internal SATCheck runs by default, can install a runtime-loaded proof-control PicoSAT backend from `E_RUST_PORT_PICOSAT_LIBRARY` or bundled executable-relative PicoSAT discovery, and still keeps default solver allocation lazy unless that backend is selected; decide whether a future always-PicoSAT default should preserve C eager allocation as a compatibility shim or keep lazy SAT-check initialization.
- `ProofControlFree` owns the OCB and admin registries but deliberately does not free `control->hcb` separately because it is one of the HCBs owned by `control->hcbs`. Rust keeps the active HCB as a borrowed admin handle; future proof-control APIs should retain that ownership split instead of cloning HCB schedules into multiple owners.
- `ProofControlInit` copies the executable `FVIndexParms` into the control object and then zeroes `symbol_slack` when splitting is disabled. Rust can build an initial `ProofControl` directly from parsed config, but the split-dependent FV-index normalization should stay in the later proof-state initialization path where C has access to finalized heuristic parameters.
- `ccl_splitting` defines `SplitAll` as `7`, which does not include the later `SplitPositive` (`8`) or `SplitMixed` (`16`) bits even though the name reads like a full mask. Rust preserves the value and should not "fix" it before clause-splitting callers are audited.
- `che_wfcb` uses a raw `void*` data pointer plus a paired exit callback and calls that exit only when data is non-`NULL`. Rust models this with typed optional data and `Drop`; later heuristic constructors should prefer ordinary owned data types while preserving explicit cleanup timing where it affects resource use.
- C priority callbacks recover the term bank indirectly through literal/equation ownership, while current Rust WFCB priority callbacks receive `&TermBank` explicitly. Keep the explicit parameter until clause/literal ownership can provide a typed bank back-pointer without global state.
- `che_random` parses seeds with `ParseInt` and assigns them to `unsigned int`, so negative seed literals wrap modulo 2^32 before the nonzero override check. Rust preserves that conversion for parser-supplied seeds; revisit only if user-facing strategy parsing later rejects negative seed values intentionally.
- `che_wfcbadmin` intentionally returns the most recently registered WFCB for duplicate names, allowing user definitions to shadow predefined ones. It also has historical parser-table naming quirks around `ConjectureSymbolWeight` and `ConjectureGeneralSymbolWeight`; Rust keeps the exact table order and dispatches every current parser entry.
- `che_wfcbadmin` `WeightFunDefParse` duplicates explicit names before `WFCBAdminAddWFCB`, whose implementation duplicates them again, and also relies on the add function duplicating a stack-local anonymous-name buffer. Rust stores owned `String` names directly; revisit only if allocation profiles show strategy parsing overhead matters.
- `cle_indexfunctions` exposes `IndexDynamic` as a name-table entry, but `GetIndexType("IndexDynamic")` returns a bitmask of all concrete index choices rather than an enum discriminant. Rust preserves that helper behavior; a cleaned typed API should keep the compatibility adapter separate from any future enum-only representation.
- `cle_indexfunctions` uses a file-static `index_counter` only for TSM index debug identities and never resets it on free/admin teardown. Rust preserves monotonic process-local ids with an atomic counter; revisit only if exact debug-output identity becomes test-visible.
- `cle_indexfunctions` stores shared `PatternSubst_p` pointers in both indexes and index terms and assumes they stay total and stable after insertion. Mutating a substitution behind an object tree would invalidate ordering. Rust snapshots substitutions for safe ordered keys until the eventual TSM admin ownership model can make that stability contract explicit.
- `cle_indexfunctions` comments say `IndexTermAlloc` gets a term reference and `IndexTermFree` changes the bank, but the implementation only stores the term pointer and frees the small wrapper after asserting the bank pointer. Rust uses owned handles and explicit term-bank insertion without modeling that misleading no-op free surface.
- `cle_annotations` `AnnotationListPrint` initializes a separator but never changes it after printing each annotation, so adjacent entries are concatenated without commas. Rust preserves that output; a later cleaned printer should only add separators behind compatibility-aware output tests.
- `cle_annotations` `AnnotationCombine` loops with `i <= length` even though parsed annotations store `length` values at zero-based indices `0..length-1`; this reads and writes the first slot beyond the parsed values and can grow the backing `DDArray`. Rust preserves that one-past combine effect for compatibility.
- `cle_annoterms` declares `AnnoTermAllocNoRef` and `AnnoSetFreeNoRef` but this source unit does not define them, and `AnnoSetAlloc` leaves `annotation_no` uninitialized while eagerly creating equality/or-list signature symbols. Rust preserves the special-code creation side effect but does not expose the undefined no-ref helpers or uninitialized field surface unless a real caller needs them.
- `cle_annoterms` `AnnoSetParse` reads annotated terms while the current token is `TermStartToken`; a trailing ordinary term-start token without `:` is a syntax error, not a clean terminator. Rust preserves that loop shape through the checked term-bank parser, including list-literal starts only when the signature has list support.
- `cle_annoterms` `AnnoSetFlatten` documents a remaining-term count but never increments the local `count`, so it always returns zero. Rust preserves that return value for the in-memory flatten helper.
- `cle_annoterms` `AnnoSetRemoveExceptIdentList` iterates using the traversal stack size (`PStackGetSP(stack)`) instead of the `set_idents` stack size. Rust exposes both the useful full-id-list helper and an explicit traversal-bound compatibility helper so reference tests can reproduce the C accident without making ordinary callers depend on it.
- `cle_flatannoterms` `FlatAnnoSetEvalAverage` sums each unique flat term's `eval` once but divides by the sum of `sources`, while `FlatAnnoSetEvalWeightedAverage` uses `eval_weight`. Rust preserves the two distinct formulas; a later statistics API should avoid naming the first one as a plain term average unless compatibility is explicit.
- `cle_numfeatures.h` defines `FEATURE_NUMBER` twice, and `FeaturesAlloc` initializes only the dynamic arity-distribution arrays while leaving maxima and feature slots to later parse/compute calls. Rust initializes the cell to safe zero/`-1` defaults, but repeated compute calls still follow the C shape of adding into existing arity-distribution arrays instead of clearing them first.
- `cle_numfeatures` uses a signed relative-difference formula `(v1-v2)/(2*max(abs(v1),abs(v2)))`, so distances are bounded and directional before squaring rather than using a conventional absolute relative difference. Rust preserves the formula.
- `cle_examplerep` `ExampleSetInsert` stores the numeric id entry before attempting the name entry. If the name insert fails, the id entry remains, `count` is not advanced, and later extraction of that duplicate-name id can delete the original name mapping. Rust preserves this dual-index side effect.
- `cle_examplerep` duplicate parse diagnostics spell "conficts" and pass the numeric ident to a `%s` format slot in C. Rust keeps the typo in its compatibility diagnostic but formats the ident safely.
- `cle_examplerep` `ExampleSetSelectByDist` divides the average distance by `set_size` without a distinct empty-set branch; the practical result for an empty set is no selections after a NaN average. Rust returns the same no-selection outcome.
- `cle_kbinsert` `ParseExampleClause` duplicates the first annotation input into a proof-count flag and a proof-distance slot, and records the annotation length as one past the last assigned slot including the count slot. Rust preserves that layout for learned-data compatibility.
- `cle_kbinsert` `ParseExampleClause` can return `NULL` when pattern computation is too expensive. Rust keeps the outer `Option` result but currently always returns `Some` for successfully parsed clauses because the ported pattern helper has no exposed cost cutoff yet.
- `cle_kbinsert` `KBAxiomsInsert` ignores `ExampleSetInsert` failure, so duplicate names can leave a numeric id entry present while `set->count` stays behind. Rust preserves this through the existing `ExampleSet::insert` behavior.
- `cle_kbinsert` `KBParseExampleFile` relies on temporary term-bank/signature lifetime tricks around feature extraction and example-clause parsing. Rust ports the stream shape with explicit second-phase parser and destination term banks; future KB-session ownership should decide whether to recreate C's shared `res_sig` mutation or keep the explicit owner boundary.
- `cle_termtops` uses `term->binding` as a temporary cache on arbitrary cutoff or marked subterms, not only variables. Rust preserves that by setting and clearing `Term::binding` around `AltTermTop`, `CSTermTop`, and `ESTermTop`; avoid calling generic dereference helpers while these temporary bindings are live.
- `cle_termtops` leaves `TPOpFlag` markers on original source terms after `CSTermTop`/`ESTermTop`; it only clears temporary bindings. Rust preserves those marker side effects because later compact/extended term-top calls can observe them.
- `cle_termtops` gives `term_set_prop_at_level` no negative-depth guard. In particular, `CSTermTop(term, -1)` performs no clear, sets no new markers on ordinary finite trees, and can still act on stale `TPOpFlag` markers. Rust preserves this edge case.
- `cle_termtops` top-copy paths allocate non-variable result cells with only arity and f-code initialized; type, weight, and property fields are not copied. Rust mirrors that shape for term-top outputs and relies on later term-bank insertion/type inference when a shared typed term is required.
- `cle_clauseenc` stores clause-list representations in an untyped `PStack` alternating `Eqn*` and `PatEqnDirection` integers; odd trailing stack entries would be ignored by the `PStackGetSP(list)/2` arity calculation. Rust exposes a typed ordered slice of literal/direction pairs, so malformed odd stack shapes are not representable.
- `cle_clauseenc` `FlatRecodeRecClauseRep` is intentionally simple and reconstructs temporary equations from recursive encoded terms before flat re-encoding them. Rust preserves the same observable order and polarity while returning a `SYNTAX_ERROR` diagnostic instead of relying on fatal `Error()` for malformed recursive encodings.
- `cle_clauseenc` allocates `$orN`, `$or`, and `$cnil` representation cells without assigning types. Rust assigns the Boolean type to those container cells before term-bank insertion so recursive encodings still work when `$or` has already been introduced as the fixed logical Boolean connective.
- `cle_clauseenc` `ParseClauseTermRep` asserts LOP scanner mode, accepts an empty literal list if `<` is already next, and consumes the terminator as `<-.` with `AcceptInpTokNoSkip` for the hyphen. Rust preserves those observable parse results with an explicit LOP diagnostic instead of an assertion abort; later cleanup can reconsider whether the adjacency requirement and empty-list acceptance are intended user-facing grammar.
- `cle_clauseenc` reaches `EqnListParse` through the full C `TBTermParse`, including `TermStartToken` variants controlled by list/application support. Rust now wires `ParseClauseTermRep` through the checked term-bank parser and preserves list-literal starts only when the signature has list support.
- `cle_annotations` `AnnotationCombine` loops through `i <= length`, so it reads and writes one slot past the parsed/printed range. `AnnotationEval` then evaluates only slots `1..length-1`, leaving that one-past slot unused unless another helper reads it directly. Rust preserves this one-past slot behavior.
- `cle_annoterms` `AnnoSetFlatten` documents a remaining-term count return value, but the C implementation never increments the local count and always returns zero. Rust preserves the zero return.
- `cle_annoterms` `AnnoSetRemoveExceptIdentList` uses the NumTree traversal stack's stack pointer as the loop bound instead of the caller-provided `set_idents` stack. Rust now exposes this as a separately named traversal-bound compatibility helper while keeping the normal helper on the useful full-id-list behavior.
- `cle_kbdesc` compares parsed KB versions with plain `strcmp`, so version ordering is lexicographic rather than semantic. It also concatenates `" update from"` and `E_URL` without an intervening space in the fatal diagnostic. Rust preserves both details.
- `cle_kbdesc` `KBFileName` always appends `"/"` between the base and file component, even on native Windows. Rust preserves the slash convention because KB directories are part of the learned-data format.
- `cle_patterns` stores a raw `Sig_p` in each pattern substitution and observes later signature mutations. The current Rust `PatternSubst` keeps a cloned signature snapshot, which is adequate for stable signatures used in the ported helpers but should become an explicit shared signature/session handle before TSM construction can mutate signatures while pattern substitutions are live.
- `cle_patterns` `pat_symbol_compare` checks `PatSymbolIsBound(subst1, f2)` in the second one-bound branch instead of checking `subst2`. Rust preserves this comparison quirk for compatibility; revisit only after representative pattern ordering is covered by reference tests.
- `cle_patterns` gives newly normalized function symbols in ascending per-arity order and normalized variables by decrementing from `NORM_VAR_INIT`; alternate variables are treated as already stable and are not entered in `var_subst`. Rust preserves those allocation orders.
- `cle_patterns` `PatternSubstGetOriginalSymbol` classifies alternate-variable-looking negative ids before scanning `var_subst`, so the first normalized variable id can be returned unchanged; when it does scan `var_subst`, it uses `fun_subst->size` as the loop bound and returns the positive array index rather than the original negative variable f-code. Rust preserves these quirks and documents them as cleanup candidates.
- `cle_patterns` `PatternTranslateSig` temporarily writes through source-term variable bindings, then clears every traversed source variable binding to `NULL`, even if a variable had a pre-existing binding. It also inserts translated symbols into the destination signature without declaring their types. Rust mirrors the clobbering behavior with safe handles and leaves type-declaration cleanup for a later compatibility-reviewed pass.
- `cle_patterns` representative search uses `EPIsUsed`, `EPLPatMinimal`, and `EPRPatMinimal` as transient scratch flags on the original literals and aborts with result `0` when a choice set exceeds `PATTERN_SEARCH_BRANCHLIMIT`. Rust keeps the scratch state local while preserving the returned order/count; revisit only if a future caller depends on observing those transient flags after an aborted search.
- `cle_tsm.h` declares `TSMFindPartLimit`, but this checkout has no matching implementation in `cle_tsm.c` or elsewhere. Rust leaves it unimplemented and documents it as header-only C surface until a real caller or reference implementation appears.
- `cle_tsm` `TSMRemainderEntropy` divides by `global_count` without an empty-partition guard, so an empty partition returns NaN while reporting zero parts. Rust preserves that numeric result for the standalone entropy helper.
- `cle_tsm` partitioning uses `FlatAnnoTerm.next` as scratch links when assigning entries to partition buckets, and caches keys as `key+1` by term `entry_no` so zero remains the no-cache sentinel. Rust preserves bucket order and one-based cache behavior with cloned partition chains instead of mutating the source flat set.
- `cle_tsm` relative index evaluation can produce positive infinity for a perfect split because the denominator `distribution_entropy - information_gain` reaches zero. Rust preserves this floating-point result.
- `cle_tsm` `TSMFindOptimalIndex` uses strict `>` comparisons, so earlier candidates win ties. It also short-circuits single concrete arity/symbol/identity indexes without normalizing the caller-provided depth to zero. Rust preserves both behaviors.
- `cle_tsm` `TSMAdminAlloc` creates `emptytsm` before `admin->subst` is set, so the C empty index is allocated with a null substitution pointer even though non-empty indexes require a total stable substitution. Rust uses a placeholder substitution for the empty index because `IndexEmpty` never inspects it; revisit only if a future debug/introspection path exposes the placeholder.
- `cle_tsm` `TSMAdminAlloc` initializes `tsmstack`/`cachestack` only for `TSMTypeRecurrentLocal` and leaves `cachestack` otherwise unused; recurrent-local builds append a fixed arity/symbol/top stack and matching caches. Rust represents both stacks as empty vectors outside recurrent-local mode and preserves the recurrent-local stack/cache order.
- `cle_tsm` shares child TSM pointers across `TSA` nodes: flat children point to the shared empty TSM, recurrent children point back to the root, recurrent-local children point into the stack, and only recursive children are owned through the parent `TSAFree` path. Rust uses `TsmId` arena handles to preserve the sharing without raw pointer cycles; revisit the arena/API shape once proof-state ownership can hold learned maps long term.
- `cle_tsm` `TSMAdminAlloc` uses `TBAlloc(sig)` for a private index bank that shares the live C signature pointer and then nulls `index_bank->sig` before freeing the bank. Rust currently owns a cloned signature in the private `TermBank`; use an explicit shared signature/session handle before proof-search symbol generation can race with learned-map construction.
- `cle_tsm` `tsm_rec_eval_no_weight` still calls the weighted `tsm_rec_eval` for matched child nodes and for recurrent unmapped child descent, so "no weight" applies only to the current matched node. Rust preserves that mixed weighting.
- `cle_tsm` `TSMPrintRek` sends `TSMIndexPrint` to `stdout` instead of the caller-provided `FILE* out` and has no cycle guard for recurrent maps. Rust exposes string debug helpers rather than reproducing the stdout routing bug; exact debug-stream compatibility should be revisited only if these printers become user-visible.
- `cle_classification` prints progress text directly to `stdout` from all three helper layers (`Evaluation`, `Termeval`, `OKOK`/`FAIL`, and the term), mixing computation and diagnostics. Rust exposes writer-based helpers so callers can preserve the text without hard-wiring global output.
- `cle_classification` `TSMClassifiedTermCheck` compares the predicted `-1/+1` classification directly with `FlatAnnoTerm.eval`; this only succeeds when the flat annotation eval is already a class label rather than an arbitrary score. Rust preserves the comparison.
- `cle_tsmio` `get_default_eval` uses `KB_ANNOTATION_NO == 7`, loops over annotation slots `3..=7`, but then calls `AnnotationEval` with length `7`, so slot `7` is accumulated and normalized but not included in the returned weighted sum. Rust preserves the unused one-past slot.
- `cle_tsmio` stores `AnnotationCount` in a C `long` while computing the default evaluation, truncating any fractional annotation count before weighting and dividing. Rust preserves the truncation even though annotation counts are represented as doubles.
- `cle_tsmio` `ExampleSetPrepare` declares its local return variable as `long` even though the helper and exported function return `double`, so fractional default evaluations are truncated before returning. Rust preserves the exported helper's truncating result.
- `cle_tsmio` `rec_get_highest_weight` and `level_get_highest_weight` initialize the result to `1e12` and then take `MAX` with all actual weights, so they effectively return `1e12` rather than the highest stored training weight. Rust preserves this value for `TSMFromKB` parity; a cleaned-up learned-map API should decide whether unmapped weights should reflect real training weights.
- `cle_tsmio` file-based `ExampleSetFromKB` opens `signature` and `problems` with comments ignored, mutates the supplied signature from the signature file, optionally recodes the supplied annotation set from recursive to flat clause encoding, and then delegates to `ExampleSetPrepare`. Rust preserves that path but reports file/parser failures as `Result` diagnostics instead of aborting.
- `cle_tsmio` `TSMFromKB` parses `clausepatterns` before parsing the KB `signature` file because the temporary C term bank shares the caller's raw `Sig_p`. Rust `TermBank` owns its signature, so the port parses clause patterns in a temporary bank, then parses the KB signature into that bank and copies the resulting signature back to the caller before building the admin. Revisit this once signatures use an explicit shared session handle.
- `cle_tsmio` `TSMFromKB` calls `VERBOUT("TSM created\n")` after construction. Rust now emits the same gated global `ProgName`/stderr diagnostic on the public path and keeps an injected-writer variant for compatibility tests.
- `che_learning` `tsm_param_init` folds positive evaluation weights into `pos_sum` and every non-positive weight, including zero, into `neg_sum`, then uses `1` as the scale only when `pos_sum - neg_sum` is exactly zero. Rust preserves this normalization for parsed TSM parameters.
- `che_learning` comments document a trailing `subsum_w` for `TSMRWeightParse`, but the C parser stops after `f_gen_w` and immediately accepts the closing bracket. Rust preserves the implemented grammar; adding a subsumption weight should wait for compatibility tests or an explicit strategy-format migration.
- `che_learning` `TSMWeightCompute`/`TSMRWeightCompute` lazily call `TSMFromKB` on first scoring and then reuse a pattern substitution plus the proof state's shared term bank for clause representations. Rust preserves the lazy behavior with a private cloned evaluation bank and copied clauses for now; revisit this when proof-state term-bank ownership becomes a shared session handle.
- `che_funweights` type-vector initialization allocates `type_freqs` from the current signature type count before `ClauseAddTypeDistribution` calls `GetHeadType`; under LFHO, phony applications can synthesize dropped head types while counting. Rust performs the lazy type-vector build against a cloned mutable signature for now; revisit the ownership and array-bound assumptions when higher-order proof-state signatures are fully integrated.
- `che_funweights` has two relevance-level initializers with a subtle special-symbol difference: `init_relevance_vector` excludes special symbols from relevance discovery and later gives them the default irrelevant penalty, while `init_relevance_vector2` forces special symbols to effective level `1`. Rust preserves the split; any cleanup should wait for clause-selection reference tests because it changes heuristic scores for built-in and special symbols.
- `che_funweights` stores the OCB on the WFCB data cell and has `GenericFunWeightCompute`/`SymOffsetWeightCompute` mutate clause orientation/maximality inside otherwise ordinary scoring callbacks. Rust now exposes banked WFCB callbacks for proof-control evaluation and keeps explicit mutable-clause helpers plus immutable fallback scoring; remove the fallback split only after every scoring site can thread the OCB, mutable owner bank, and mutable clause without aliasing.
- `che_termweight` uses a private `TBAlloc(data->ocb->sig)` evaluation bank that shares the live C signature pointer, while Rust currently owns a cloned signature in the private `TermBank`. This is compatible for the current axiom-backed WFCB tests, but proof-search integration should revisit the ownership model if generated symbols can appear after the relative-term evaluator initializes.
- `che_termweight` updates candidate subterm frequencies by normalizing each newly inserted candidate subterm, finding its representative in the evaluation bank, and then inserting the representative's existing frequency under the candidate subterm's own `entry_no`; `NumTreeStore` does not replace existing keys. Rust preserves this first-mapping-wins behavior, but it is worth reviewing later because the frequency tree mixes conjecture representative keys with candidate-term keys.
- `che_termweights` `ComputeTopGeneralizations` records occurrences from child argument slots `1..arity`, skipping slot 0. Rust preserves this for the already-ported related-term consumers, but the helper should be reviewed once higher-order application heads and top-generalization strategy effects are covered by reference tests.
- `che_prefixweight` scores `PDTreeMatchPrefix` as matched and remaining LR-traversal cells along a single discrimination-tree path, not as a count of stored conjecture terms that are prefixes. Rust now preserves the observable score through the shared `PdTree` trie subset, the trie deletion helper mirrors C's ref-count/prune shape for code-only entries, `PDTreeStorage` preserves C's constant-memory estimate including the stale parent function-alternative storage after deletion, executable runs scope the age/size constraint globals from the parsed PDT options, and demodulator search stand-ins record C-shaped search-init query state plus search-exit active-query/cursor clearing, represented size/date constraint caches, compact occurrence payloads, variable type-UID, variable-edge weight adjustment, repeated-variable query-slice checks, successful-child visit accounting, and conservative per-node/path pruning where stored variables consume whole query subtrees. Full demodulator search, live `ClausePos` leaf storage/deletion, remaining substitution-aware traversal/backtracking state beyond compact variable-edge weight adjustment, exact incremental tree traversal beyond the compact cursor, and lambda eta reduce/expand indexing behavior should be revisited when discrimination-tree indexing is ported end to end.
- `che_tfidfweight` parses `tf_mult`/`tf_fact` and uses `data->tf_fact` in scoring, but `ConjectureTermTfIdfWeightInit` does not assign the parsed value into the parameter cell after `SizeMalloc`. Rust stores and uses the parsed multiplier deterministically; decide later whether exact C compatibility needs a bug-compatibility mode for this uninitialized field.
- `che_tfidfweight` uses `documents->clause_count` as the IDF document count, but `PDTreeInsertTerm` increments it for every inserted document subterm rather than for every clause. Rust preserves this inserted-subterm count through `PdTree::term_count()`; revisit whether a future cleaned-up strategy should count clauses or unique document terms after compatibility is secured.
- `--fixpoint-oracle` help text says it turns the pattern oracle on or off, and `--max-unifiers` help text says "Maximal number of imitations". Rust uses clearer internal field names but keeps the option behavior; full help output should decide whether to preserve or correct those strings.
- `--assume-completeness` and `--assume-incompleteness` write independent C globals, so both can be true if both options are supplied. Rust records both bits; later diagnostics may want to warn on the contradictory-looking combination after compatibility is covered.
- C's executable-level incomplete-calculus gate also calls `SigHasUnimplementedInterpretedSymbols`. Rust now wires the signature-level helper for supported clause-list runs, but interpreted arithmetic itself remains a later parser/preprocessing/proof-control integration item.
- `--term-ordering` help mentions `Auto`, but the C handler rejects `Auto` and `RPO`, accepts `LPOCopy`/`LPO4Copy`, and uses an invalid-argument diagnostic that does not mention the copy variants. Rust preserves that executable option surface and diagnostic while leaving the shared ordering enum/name-table RPO exposure in the strategy-parameter path; full help output should decide whether to preserve or correct the remaining help/runtime mismatch.
- `--order-weight-generation` and `--order-precedence-generation` help suggest `-w none` and `-G none` to print valid methods, but `none` is rejected because the handler treats `NoMethod` as failure. Rust preserves the list-through-error behavior; a future CLI could expose an explicit list command after compatibility.
- `--lpo-recursion-limit` in `eprover.c` is missing a `break`, so it also executes `--restrict-literal-comparisons` and sets literal comparison to `None`. Rust records this fall-through side effect; remove it only behind reference tests if exact compatibility is relaxed.
- `--lpo-recursion-limit` emits a warning for values above `20000` in C. Rust now records explicitly provided limits, applies them to the process-wide LPO ordering state for configured executable runs, and emits this warning for valid runs, early `--help`/`--version` exits, and later option errors before the final diagnostic; C's parse-time process-global mutation before a fatal option error remains unmodeled because the executable exits immediately.
- `--strong-destructive-er` explicitly sets both destructive-ER bits in the C handler, while `--destructive-er-aggressive` sets only the aggressive bit despite help text saying it implies `--destructive-er`. Rust records the handler state; revisit whether the help text or the handler should win after compatibility tests cover equality resolution.
- `--forward-context-sr-aggressive` sets both forward contextual simplify-reflect bits, so the aggressive mode really does imply the non-aggressive mode. Rust records that implication; later configuration code may want to model this as one enum or an implication check instead of two independent booleans.
