- `--strong-rw-inst` appears among inference/demodulation options in `e_options.h`, but the C handler writes `order_params.rewrite_strong_rhs_inst`. Rust stores it with term-ordering state to match the destination; revisit the public configuration grouping after ordering and rewriting are integrated.
- `--satcheck`, `--satcheck-proc-interval`, `--satcheck-gen-interval`, `--satcheck-ttinsert-interval`, and `--satcheck-decision-limit` all have optional-argument defaults that differ from their process defaults, so spelling the option without `=value` actively changes SAT-check state. Rust records this distinction; later user-facing docs should make the enabled-default behavior explicit.
- The invalid-grounding diagnostic in `eprover.c` says `--sat-check` even though the actual long option is `--satcheck`. Rust preserves the diagnostic wording for now; full help and diagnostics should decide whether to keep or correct that mismatch.
- SATCheck in C delegates to PicoSAT, applies its decision limit through the solver, exports either all clauses or the non-pure subset through the solver interface, and extracts PicoSAT's unsat core into the generated empty clause. The extracted core is pushed in solver order but consumed with `PStackPopP`, so the generated empty-clause derivation records core parents in reverse stack order. Rust currently uses safe all/non-pure solver-clause export vectors plus an internal DPLL solver, deletion-minimizes the exported non-pure clause set after UNSAT, keeps exported-size statistics separate from minimized-core statistics, mirrors the reverse derivation-parent order, and records the represented SATCheck empty clause in the proof-state extraction-root stack. The SAT clause-set layer and proof-control SATCheck dispatch can also use the runtime-loaded PicoSAT wrapper, reset it to a fresh solver state around each exported SATCheck or fixed-limit core query, and map solver core positions back through the exported-subset stack when a caller, `E_RUST_PORT_PICOSAT_LIBRARY`, or bundled executable-relative library discovery installs a runtime solver; making PicoSAT mandatory/default for every SATCheck run still depends on packaging and the eager-allocation compatibility decision.
- `Saturate` compares signed `generated_limit` and `tb_insert_limit` against unsigned C counters. Negative values therefore behave as very large unsigned sentinels, and `generated_count - backward_rewritten_count` is unsigned arithmetic. Rust preserves this in the staged loop; a later cleaned-up limit API should only change it behind compatibility tests.
- C uses watchlist pointer presence to distinguish "no watchlist" from "active watchlist now empty". Rust proof states allocate an empty optional watchlist by default, so the port tracks explicit watchlist activation after loading. Keep this distinction visible until the proof-session owner can represent inactive, file-backed, inline, and active-empty watchlist states directly.
- `--static-watchlist` intentionally falls through to the normal `--watchlist` handler after setting the static bit. Rust records that source-selection side effect; later UI code should make staticness an attribute of the watchlist source instead of a separate loose flag if compatibility allows.
- Watchlist inline mode uses the sentinel string `Use inline watchlist type`, and the optional-argument default supplies the quoted form `'Use inline watchlist type'`; the C handler accepts both. Rust preserves both spellings, but a future configuration API should prefer an enum over sentinel filenames.
- `FormulaAndClauseSetParse` writes `watchlist`-typed TPTP/TSTP input directly into the proof-state watchlist set, including through includes and selector filtering, and `ProofStateLoadWatchlist` parses external watchlist files into that same existing set rather than replacing it. Rust preserves the append/preload behavior for supported input; a future proof-session API should expose normal input, inline watchlist input, and file watchlist source policy explicitly.
- `FVIndexParms` defaults to `FVIACFold` with `use_perm_vectors=false`, while the help text still describes `AC` feature types and `Perm` indexing as defaults. Rust records the initializer, not the stale help prose; revisit help wording when FV-index behavior is wired in.
- `--subsumption-indexing=None` changes only the feature type to `FVINoFeatures`, and `--subsumption-indexing=Direct` changes only `use_perm_vectors=false`; neither clears `eliminate_uninformative`. Rust preserves this order dependence, including `PermOpt` followed by `Direct` leaving the elimination flag set.
- C `fvi_param_init` computes and installs the FV-index permutation vector regardless of the stored `use_perm_vectors` bit, and the definition-store anchor receives the vector computed from the active proof-state spec rather than from its own AC-fold spec. Rust preserves those effective inputs in the reusable installable anchor bundle; revisit this once indexed subsumption has reference-level coverage.
- `ccl_proofstate` `ProofStateResetClauseSets` frees `f_ax_archive` twice and skips `definition_store`, its definition formula archive, and `f_archive` even though its comment says all clause and formula sets are emptied. Rust preserves those omissions; decide later whether proof-state reset should explicitly include definitions and formula archives after definition handling and state reuse are ported.
- `ccl_proofstate` allocates `terms` and `tmp_terms` against one shared mutable `Sig_p`, then nulls both bank signature pointers before freeing that signature. Rust currently ports only the main proof-state term bank; the eventual temporary-bank slice should use an explicit shared proof-session signature handle rather than cloning mutable signatures.
- `ProofStateIsUntyped` ignores axioms, watchlist clauses, and formulas. Rust preserves this processed/unprocessed-only domain; only broaden it behind reference tests if callers actually intended a whole-state type scan.
- `ProofStateLoadWatchlist` emits initial watchlist documentation through global output after activation. Rust now mirrors this in the supported executable proof-search path while keeping the lower-level state loader output-free; revisit that split only if proof-session ownership needs exact C coupling.
- `ProofStatePrint` emits both positive processed unit sets under one `Processed positive unit clauses` heading. Rust preserves that text and order; later user-facing diagnostics can split the categories only outside compatibility output.
- `ProofStateInitWatchlist` inserts the rebuilt watchlist into `wlindices` after the local indexed reinsertion, and dynamic `check_watchlist` deletes removed watched clauses from that same global index. Rust now ports bank-backed maximal-term marking, owned FV-index rebuild, local dynamic removal/archive mutation, and caller-owned watchlist global-index insertion/deletion through supported executable/proof-control bridges; move that owner into a long-lived proof-session/state structure when the self-reference problem is resolved.
- `ProofStateInit` reweights source axioms with the built-in `Uniq` HCB, leaves those evaluations attached to `state->axioms`, then copies clauses preserving identifiers and properties before evaluating the copies with the active HCB. Rust preserves this dual-HCB behavior; later cleanup can separate the temporary ordering view from persistent source-axiom evaluation only after reference tests cover callers that inspect `state->axioms`.
- `ProofStateInit` emits its initial-copy `eval` proof quote before pushing `DCCnfQuote`, so C can clear `CPInputFormula` and assign the copied clause a fresh proof-documentation id while derivation metadata still points at the original source axiom. Rust preserves stable state ids in the plain helper and mirrors proof-id mutation through the explicit documenting wrapper; level-six executable coverage now pins the initial eval quote and following id stream, but keep this state-id/proof-id split explicit for non-output callers.
- `document_processing` can assign a fresh proof-documentation id to the selected clause before processed-set insertion when OutputLevel reaches the target quote level. Rust mirrors that proof-id mutation only in the documenting `ProcessClause` wrappers; the output-only saturation/executable path emits the C OutputLevel 1 selected-clause text while keeping plain state ids stable. Keep the state-id/proof-id split explicit until executable proof-output reference tests decide how session-owned proof ids should surface to non-output callers.
- `check_watchlist` packs the FV query before sorting/standard-weighting the candidate clause in C. Rust sorts and weights before invoking reusable indexed helpers that pack internally; keep this under reference tests before generalizing the watchlist lookup path.
- `check_watchlist` emits proof-documentation quotes for removed watched clauses and for the new clause that subsumed a dynamic watchlist entry, and separately prints the OutputLevel 1 reduction message. Rust now mirrors the represented quotes through the explicit documenting wrapper, threads that wrapper through opt-in `ProcessClause` documentation, threads output-only reduction text through supported executable initialization/saturation, and deletes removed watched clauses from caller-owned watchlist global indices when supplied while keeping the plain helpers output-free.
- `prefer_initial_clauses` shifts every active-HCB evaluation by `-PrioLargestReasonable` after evaluating initial copies. Rust preserves the numeric rewrite; a future strategy layer could expose this as a named priority tier if compatibility permits.
- `ProofStateInit` scans `unprocessed` for AC axioms after initial copies and SOS marking, not the source axiom set. The active flag follows `ClauseSetScanAC`'s commutativity-only boolean, so associativity-only inputs can set `FPAssociative` without setting `ac_handling_active`; Rust preserves this split and uses the represented scan/status output in supported executable proof-state initialization.
- Dynamic `check_ac_status` prints the updated signature AC status and an activation message only when a selected clause newly enables AC handling. Rust keeps the plain helper output-free, exposes opt-in proof-documentation and output-only wrappers, and supported executable saturation now threads the output/proof-documentation wrapper through selected-clause AC checks when proof output is enabled.
- `ProofStateInit` ends by freeing and reinitializing `state->gindices` with fingerprint-index names from `control->heuristic_parms` and the process-global `problemType`. Rust exposes this as a caller-owned `GlobalIndices` helper with an explicit `ProblemType`; fold it into proof-session ownership later rather than forcing a self-referential `ProofState`.
- `ProofStateResetProcessedSet` flat-copies processed clauses after archiving originals, preserving identifiers and properties such as `CPIsProcessed` on the requeued copy while dropping evaluations through the copy path and recording `DCCnfQuote` to the archived original; when GC selection is recorded, the archived original is marked with `DCCnfEvalGC`, and C's later `move_eval` proof quote assigns the requeued copy a fresh proof-documentation id. Rust preserves the duplicate-identifier archive/unprocessed shape in the plain helper, mirrors the proof-id mutation through the explicit documenting wrapper, and uses that wrapper from supported executable pre-saturation reset when proof output is enabled; revisit only after broader proof-output and clause-state consumers are covered by reference tests.
- `ProofStateMoveSetToTmp` preserves clause evaluations while moving originals to `tmp_store` and clearing only `CPIsOriented`; the reset path reevaluates the flat copy instead. Rust preserves this split so cleanup code can distinguish lightweight temporary movement from requeueing.
- `ProofStateResetProcessedSet` and `ProofStateMoveSetToTmp` delete globally indexed clauses from `state->gindices` before moving them in C. Rust now mirrors this for explicit caller-owned global indices by deleting while the clause is still in its processed set; keep the same ordering when the eventual proof-state-owned index owner replaces the temporary bridge.
- `ForwardModifyClause` takes a `context_sr` argument but does not read it; C performs contextual simplify-reflect in the later `forward_contract_keep` path. Rust preserves that split in the staged forward-contraction wrappers; revisit the helper boundary again when generated-clause routing and higher-order contraction share the same path.
- `ForwardModifyClause` has higher-order-only `NormalizeEquations` and `ClausePruneArgs` hooks around rewriting, local rewriting, and simplify-reflect. Rust now ports both hooks for the represented path, lets legacy KBO/LPO/KBO6 ordering handle first-order-shaped higher-order clauses and demodulators, lets KBO6 ordering follow C's `LFHO_ORDER` branch for visible LFHO DB/lambda/phony surfaces, allows KBO6 `LAMBDA_ORDER` for no-bank-safe terms with no exposed lambda surface after local weak-head reduction of simple DB-lambda applications, and lets the represented KBO6 `LAMBDA_ORDER` forward-modify path accept lambda-bearing surfaces after threading bank-backed beta/eta comparison through rewrite rule checks, local-rewrite orientation, and post-rewrite orientation. Bank-backed LPO4 covers visible LFHO LPO4 surfaces plus recursive WHNF/beta/eta preparation where a mutable live term bank is available; visible higher-order ordering surfaces outside the bank-backed LPO4/KBO6 paths remain guarded separately, and selector/scoring callbacks outside the banked proof-control paths that still receive only immutable term-bank access still need the bank-backed ordering wrapper before this is complete everywhere.
- `ForwardModifyClause` rewrite documentation from demodulation, minimization documentation after superfluous-literal cleanup, condensation documentation after successful condensation, and simplify-reflect documentation after removed literals are wired through `proof_state_forward_modify_clause_with_docs` for represented proof-doc sessions. Destructive-ER renderers are available, but proof-control still needs global-output/session integration at those C mutation points. The `StrongUnitForwardSubsumption` simplify-reflect switch is now represented by proof-control session configuration.
- `forward_contract_keep` calls C `BooleanSimplification` before empty/AC/tautology/subsumption checks and has higher-order `ResolveFlexClause` plus naked-Boolean elimination hooks. Rust now ports the first-order/local BooleanSimplification hook for decoded Boolean formulas, including two-argument `and`/`or`, unary decoded `and`/`or` constant-to-DB-lambda cases, closed-lambda decoded quantifier matrix removal, normalize derivation metadata, trivial-count deletion when simplification yields a true literal, and proof-control ordering before empty/AC/tautology/subsumption checks. Rust also wires the higher-order flex-resolution, naked-Boolean elimination, `ClausePruneArgs`, LFHO KBO6, bank-backed LPO4, first-order-shaped legacy KBO/LPO, no-bank-safe Lambda-order KBO6 including locally reducible DB-lambda applications, represented bank-backed Lambda-order forward-modify hooks, and final bank-backed maximal marking at the C call sites; full higher-order forward contraction still needs selector/scoring paths outside the banked proof-control reweight/eval flows plus visible higher-order ordering surfaces outside the bank-backed LPO4/KBO6 paths resolved before the ordinary enabled-simplification path can run without diagnostics.
- `ForwardContractSet` mutates a live clause set while keeping the next pointer before possible deletion or extraction. Rust preserves the visible order and early-empty tail behavior by draining/rebuilding the set; replace this with stable in-set clause handles before using the helper on sets with long-lived FV anchors or global-index membership.
- C `FVPackedClause_p` aliases the same live clause pointer that later literal selection and maximal marking mutate. Rust's owned `FvPackedClause` clone is packed after final mutation in the staged forward-contraction wrapper; replace this with stable clause handles before relying on pointer-alias timing or packed-clause identity.
- `cleanup_unprocessed_clauses` uses `ProofStateStorage`, a C byte estimate over selected clause sets and term-bank storage, to trigger delete-bad cleanup. Rust now uses the same selected proof-state domains with C constant-memory `ClauseSetStorage` and `TBStorage` estimates, including represented FV-index and demodulator-index storage, and carries branch metadata so saturation can render C's orphan, forward-contraction/reweight, and delete-bad progress messages; remaining byte-accounting differences should be tied to owners that are still unrepresented rather than the selected processed-set demodulator indexes.
- `cleanup_unprocessed_clauses` and HCB clause selection now use proof-control compact-parent liveness snapshots by default for orphan deletion. Rust keeps predicate-backed hooks because compact `ident/source` parent references still cannot distinguish all duplicate archived/requeued clause identities.
- When compact parent refs collide between live processed clauses and archived/dead copies, Rust treats the ref as live to approximate C's pointer-specific parent check. Replace this live-wins compatibility rule with stable clause handles before proof-object traversal depends on archived/requeued duplicate identifiers.
- `insert_new_clauses` snapshots `tmp_store` member and literal totals into generated counters before any contraction, deletion, replacement inference, or splitting. Rust preserves that boundary and separately adds destructive equality-resolution and C-style controlled-splitting replacement counts to `generated_count`; revisit literal accounting once end-to-end reference tests cover reused split-definition clauses.
- `insert_new_clauses` treats `CPIsIRVictim` clauses specially by running a `FullRewrite` `ForwardModifyClause` pass, ignoring that pass's boolean result, clearing `CPIsIRVictim`, and then running the normal forward-demodification pass. Rust preserves the double-pass shape; collapse it only after inference-replacement reference tests cover `CPLimitedRW` and triviality timing.
- `insert_new_clauses` returns an empty generated clause before evaluating or draining `eval_store`, so earlier survivors may remain pending in `eval_store` and later generated clauses may remain in `tmp_store`. Rust preserves this partial-state return because the outer proof-search loop can observe it.
- Aggressive forward subsumption inside `insert_new_clauses` updates `aggressive_forward_subsumed_count` instead of `proc_forward_subsumed_count`. Rust keeps the separate counter so generated-clause filtering remains distinguishable from processed-clause forward contraction.
- `eval_clause_set` mutates clause evaluations despite its C side-effect comment saying `-`. Rust exposes the mutation through banked HCB evaluation and keeps eval-store clause order stable.
- C adds evaluations to clauses already in `eval_store`, leaving the eval-store trees without entries until later extraction/reinsertion. Rust reinserts each evaluated clause into the same set to keep `ClauseSet` evaluation indices internally valid.
- `insert_new_clauses` clears `CPIsOriented` before selection/eval-store insertion and then clears it again while moving evaluated clauses to `unprocessed`. Rust preserves the second clear in the eval-store drain tail; revisit duplication only after selection and proof-output reference tests are in place.
- `insert_new_clauses` stores `proc_non_trivial_count` in each admitted clause's `create_date`; Rust bridges unsigned proof-state counters into the signed clause date field by saturating at `i64::MAX`. Revisit the counter/date type split after reference tests cover large-date behavior.
- `select_on_proc_only` bypasses `DoLiteralSelection` during generated-clause admission and only clears selected literal flags. Rust preserves the C flag-level behavior; later compatibility-relaxed APIs could make the processing-time selection phase more explicit.
- C assumes literal-selection dispatch is infallible once options have parsed. Rust now covers table-visible selector bodies for the supported banked wrapper; the lower-level helper can still report a diagnostic for unknown names or for ordering-dependent selectors called without the required OCB/term-bank context.
- The generated-clause destructive equality-resolution and controlled-splitting gates both replace the current clause by pushing new clauses into `tmp_store` and adding to generated counters. Rust now ports the first-order destructive equality-resolution gate, including opt-in modification proof docs, plus fresh-definition and reused-definition controlled splitting with arity-zero fresh and reusable non-fresh split-definition parents archived in the dedicated proof-state definition formula archive, represented on the produced clauses, and used for opt-in split proof-documentation output; lower-level split construction remains separate from the executable saturation proof-documentation session.
- `replacing_inferences` runs destructive equality resolution under `er_varlit_destructive` without requiring `er_aggressive`, then calls `insert_new_clauses` only after the selected clause is replaced. Rust preserves this phase split; do not merge it with generated-clause aggressive ER until reference tests cover selected-clause replacement timing.
- C `replacing_inferences` nulls `pclause->clause`, unpacks the `FVPackedClause_p`, and returns either NULL, an empty clause, or the original clause pointer. Rust consumes the owned packed clause and returns an explicit survivor/replaced enum; replace this with stable clause handles when the full processed-clause loop can preserve C aliasing.
- Higher-order `ImmediateClausification` in `replacing_inferences` can replace a selected clause before destructive ER or splitting. Rust now preserves the `EqnIsClausifiable` trigger and no-op fallthrough for non-clausifiable higher-order selected clauses, and clausifiable selected clauses now route through direct term-level `TFormulaClauseEncode`/`WTFormulaConjunctiveNF3`/`TFormulaToCNF` dynamic CNF with `DCDynamicCNF` derivations and original-clause archival. Full parity still needs the staged `FormulaSet`/`WFormula` preprocessing owner wired into proof-state dynamic CNF plus exact formula-owner proof-document parents around staged `WFormulaCNF2`.
- `ProcessClause` normalizes selected-clause variables with `state->freshvars`, a separate variable bank paired with `state->terms->vars`. Rust now mirrors that owner split so normalization cannot bind a term-bank variable to itself; after compatibility is stable, consider whether the shadow-paired bank API can be made more explicit.
- `ClauseSetIndexedInsert` inserts the same `FVPackedClause_p` alias that later gets unpacked by `ProcessClause`. Rust's processed-set helper consumes an owned clause and rebuilds any FV-index entry from that value; replace this with stable selected-clause handles when processed-set ownership and generated-inference routing are unified.
- `ProcessClause` deletes the exact archived-copy pointer if forward contraction consumes a selected clause after proof-object GC archive copying. Rust deletes by archived identifier while clauses are owned values; use stable archive handles if duplicate archived identifiers become observable.
- The backward simplification calls inside `ProcessClause` delete processed clauses from `state->gindices` and emit proof-documentation quotes before archiving dead originals or moving quoted copies into `tmp_store`. The relevant C helper comments still mention child killing, but the current bodies archive or requeue the affected clauses without traversing child links. Rust ports the local set/archive/tmp-store movement and statistics, mirrors global-index deletion for explicit caller-owned indices, and exposes represented backward-subsumption and simplifiable-movement quotes through opt-in docs wrappers.
- `ProcessClause` now runs represented higher-order ArgCong, NegExt, PosExt, inverse recognition, Leibniz elimination, and PrimitiveEnumeration before the staged first-order `generate_new_clauses` helpers and preflights the remaining unported higher-order generation branches with specific diagnostics. Plain, simultaneous, and super-simultaneous first-order unindexed paramodulation plus first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap paramodulation are wired with opt-in creation docs, explicit caller-owned indexed generation/process-clause/saturation helpers can drive indexed higher-order CSU paramodulation with matching paramodulation docs, and supported executable proof search drives configured global PM indexes through that bridge with the parsed problem type; full proof search still needs state-owned global-index integration and remaining lambda/DB/phony/full LFHO unindexed paramodulation surfaces remain diagnostic.
- `simplify_watchlist` uses `state->wlindices.bw_rw_index` when present and updates `wlindices` after reinserting simplified watched copies. Rust now mirrors that branch over caller-owned watchlist global indices: a configured backward-rewrite index supplies candidate watched clauses, originals are deleted before archive movement, simplified watched copies are reinserted, and the owned watchlist scan remains the fallback only when no backward-rewrite index is configured. Long-lived proof-session/state ownership remains pending.
- `simplify_watchlist` emits proof-documentation output for simplification/minimization moves. Rust now exposes the represented rewrite and minimization quotes through `proof_state_simplify_watchlist_with_docs` and threads that wrapper through opt-in `ProcessClause` documentation while preserving the output-free plain helpers.
- The processed-clause insertion tail immediately follows with `GlobalIndicesInsertClause`, optional watchlist simplification, generating inferences, temp-bank GC, deterministic tmp-store sorting, and `insert_new_clauses`. Rust currently ports local set insertion, caller-owned global-index insertion/deletion around supported indexed saturation, local watchlist simplification, selected-clause generation's live term-bank fresh-variable counter pre-seed, represented higher-order ArgCong/NegExt/PosExt/inverse-recognition/Leibniz elimination/PrimitiveEnumeration plus caller-owned ExtSup/ExtEqRes/ExtEqFact helpers and staged first-order equality-factor/equality-resolution/disequality/plain and simultaneous generation including opt-in equality-factor/equality-resolution/paramodulation creation docs, unindexed paramodulation, first-order-shaped higher-order KBO6 selected-overlap paramodulation, and caller-owned indexed plain/simultaneous/super-simultaneous higher-order CSU paramodulation, the explicit caller-owned indexed generation/process-clause/saturation variants with paramodulation docs, C-position tmp-store sorting, docs-aware selected-clause destructive equality-resolution replacement docs plus represented split proof docs, and docs-aware insertion of generated/backward-simplified clauses through opt-in `ProcessClause` wrappers. Keep the remaining side effects separate until state-owned proof-session global indices, remaining higher-order generation branches, remaining lambda/DB/phony/full LFHO paramodulation surfaces, and temp-term-bank ownership are represented.
- The evaluated-clause `insert_new_clauses` tail emits an `"eval"` proof quote in C after HCB evaluation and before unprocessed insertion, so proof-documentation can clear `CPInputFormula` and assign each generated clause a fresh proof id at the final queueing boundary. Rust preserves stable state ids in the plain helper and mirrors proof-id mutation through the explicit documenting wrapper, including when `ProcessClause` owns the opt-in docs session; keep this state-id/proof-id split explicit until executable generated-clause proof-output tests cover this path.
- `GlobalIndicesInsertClauseSet` returns early when `bw_rw_index` is null, so PM-only index configurations would not mark or insert clauses through the set helper. Rust preserves this gate; revisit if full global-index configuration makes PM-only sets meaningful.
- `GlobalIndicesReset` rebuilds optional indexes without clearing `CPIsGlobalIndexed` on live clauses. C reset call paths free owning sets first; Rust should keep flag cleanup explicit if reset becomes callable while indexed clauses are still alive.
- `ccl_global_indices` stores optional subterm, overlap, and LFHO extension indexes as raw pointers tied to the proof-state signature and allocates extension indexes by reading the process-global `problemType`. Rust uses a borrowed-signature shell plus an explicit `ProblemType` initializer, including for the proof-control initialization tail; move this behind an explicit shared proof-session handle when proof-state global-index ownership is wired.
- Rust `GlobalIndices` clause insert/delete take an explicit `&TermBank` so the PM overlap split helper can evaluate `EqnIsEquLit`; replace this temporary parameter with typed literal or clause owner-bank access once available.
- `CollectExtSupFromPos` checks `MAYBE_NORMALIZE_APP_VAR(handle->lterm)` before accepting both the left and right from positions. Rust preserves the left-term check for the right side; revisit only with LFHO extension-superposition reference tests because changing it can alter indexed inference sources.
- `do_ext_sup` avoids one positive-top duplicate through a raw clause-pointer tiebreak, emits disagreement conditions without unifying the selected subterms, and manually sets proof size/depth/SOS without copying TPTP type. Rust uses deterministic clause identifiers for the tiebreak and preserves the no-unification and metadata shape; revisit only after LFHO extensional-superposition traces make duplicate order or proof-output details observable.
- `ccl_ext_index` now distinguishes already pattern-shaped applied free variables from non-pattern applied free variables using term-bank metadata, so non-pattern applications can contribute eligible descendants. It still does not call the eta-reducing `NormalizePatternAppVar` rewrite before indexing; add that once LFHO pattern normalization has a caller-visible Rust API.
- `ccl_ext_index` delete paths use `IntMapGetRef`, which may materialize empty symbol slots while deleting. Rust removes empty function-code entries from its `BTreeMap`; revisit if exact storage accounting or diagnostic tree shape becomes observable.
- `--fvindex-maxfeatures` first rejects values `<= 0` with a custom error, then performs a broader nonnegative range check. Rust preserves the custom positive-value diagnostic; a later typed configuration layer can collapse this into one validation path after compatibility.
- `--fp-no-size-constr` is advertised in `e_options.h` but has no `eprover.c` switch case in this checkout. In release C builds it is effectively a no-op; in debug builds it reaches the generic `assert(false && "Unknown option")`. Rust accepts it as a no-op and documents the mismatch for later cleanup.
- Fingerprint index validation accepts the names in `FPIndexNames`, including `NoIndex`; `GetFPIndexFunction("NoIndex")` returns `NULL`, so `eprover.c` has to special-case that name even though it is also present in the printed list. Rust validates against the same name list.
- Several `e_options.h` help strings contain spelling mistakes such as `correcly`, `peoblem`, `formuas`, `deriviation`, and `exampels`. Rust keeps corrected short descriptions while now covering the C option-name and argument-metadata surface; a future help-output compatibility pass should decide explicitly between byte-compatible help prose and cleaned wording.
- Scanner and command-line code intentionally preserve C quirks already covered by tests, while remaining incomplete for full streaming file/include stacks and byte-identical help formatting.
- `FileVarsGetBool` in C appears to misuse `strcmp`, making `true` read as false and nearly any non-`true` value read as true; Rust preserves this for compatibility, but it is a prime cleanup target once callers and reference behavior are audited.
- `FileVarsParse` has no explicit EOF guard while collecting value tokens before `;`; Rust reports a syntax error for a missing semicolon rather than reproducing the apparent EOF loop risk.
- `cio_initio` leaves an existing `TPTP_dir` in place when `InitIO` is called again with no `TPTP` environment variable and `ExitIO` clears only that directory, not the program name. Rust mirrors this global-state shape while storing the program name in both the I/O facade and the shared error renderer; collapse that duplication only after compatibility wrappers no longer need both surfaces.
- `cio_fileops` keeps C's slash-only path splitting and read-open based `FileExists` race; once native Windows drop-in behavior is tested, decide whether these helpers stay compatibility shims or gain explicit platform-aware wrappers.
- `cio_tempfile` keeps C's process-global temporary-file registry and `TMPDIR`/`/tmp` policy. Later session code should decide whether temp-file ownership should move to scoped run state while preserving signal/atexit cleanup compatibility.
- `ScannerSetFormat(AutoFormat)` guesses the format from only the current token, classifying exact `fof|cnf|tff|thf|tcf|include` as TSTP, exact `input_clause|input_formula` as old TPTP, and everything else as LOP. Rust preserves that narrow first-token heuristic; revisit it only behind a compatibility-aware format-detection layer.
- C `parse_spec` also lets this auto-detected scanner format mutate global output state: a TSTP auto-detected input file flips `OutputFormat` to TSTP and fills an unset `DocOutputFormat` with TSTP, while any still-unset documentation format defaults to PCL. That makes output policy depend on input-file order and parser side effects. Rust preserves the user-visible behavior for supported clause-list executable paths through per-run config mutation, but a later cleanup could separate detection from output policy behind explicit compatibility tests.
- `eprover.c` prints a preprocessing-configuration diagnostic to `stdout` after parsing even though the surrounding output uses `GlobalOut`; the apparent debug guard is commented out, and unset `sine` reaches `%s` as a null pointer on the C side. Rust preserves the reference stdout leak for supported non-syntax paths and prints `(null)` for the unset SInE string, but later output cleanup should route or suppress this only behind compatibility-aware tests.
- C scanner streams read files lazily through a `FILE*` lookahead window, while Rust explicit file streams currently load the file bytes before scanning. The scanner include path now uses the same owned `InputStreamStack` abstraction as standalone stream callers, but large-problem performance should still be rechecked before the executable parser relies on eager file bytes.
- `ScannerParseInclude` is the include path used by formula parsing, while the C scanner's automatic `include_key` stream-splicing path is initialized to `NULL` and has no setter in this checkout. The executable path threads a `skip_includes` tree into nested include parsing, but this checkout does not show that tree being populated before `ScannerParseInclude` consults it; Rust now keeps repeated include declarations observable in the supported executable parser to match that source snapshot. Make any future repeated-include suppression policy an explicit compatibility-breaking cleanup or a deliberately seeded caller policy when the full input pipeline is ported. Rust exposes the automatic path through an explicit constructor for compatibility testing; revisit whether it needs a broader public switch when parser integration reaches every include caller.
- `cio_network` has several cleanup candidates: `TCPMsgRead` prints debug progress directly to stdout, treats an empty-payload message as a closed connection after reading the header, and appends payload data through C-string APIs after partial reads. Rust keeps the wire format, empty-payload status quirk, initialized-byte C-string truncation, and exact progress-line text through explicit traced helpers while still avoiding C's uninitialized short-read tail.
- `cio_multiplexer` has a likely source typo: `TCPChannelSendMsg` stores messages in `channel->out` and callers set write readiness based on `TCPChannelHasOutMsg`, but `TCPChannelWrite` checks and drains `channel->in`. Rust now exposes that inbound-draining behavior explicitly while keeping the ordinary write method outbound-correct; executable-facing multiplexer owners should choose deliberately after C server/client path tests exist.
- `cio_signals` mixes async signal-handler work with non-async-signal-safe operations such as cleanup, formatted diagnostics, resource-limit calls, and process exit. Rust now installs the Linux `SIGXCPU` trampoline only in normal builds, keeps the state machine testable, exposes C-shaped hard-timeout and unexpected-signal output through non-signal finalizers, and mirrors the normal Linux handler path with direct descriptor writes plus hard-timeout exit; later executable integration should decide which shutdown behavior belongs in real signal handlers and which should move to cooperative polling.
- `cte_functypes` rational normalization now mirrors LP64 `strtoll` positive-overflow saturation for rational components while rejecting the `LONG_MIN` absolute-value path that can overflow or divide by zero in C release builds. If huge negative rational components become externally relevant, compare against the C reference before deciding whether to reproduce that unsafe edge case.
- `cte_simpletypes` uses pointer-address ordering in `TypesCmp`, and the C source notes this causes clause-sorting differences. Rust preserves handle identity comparisons where needed, but later term/type-bank integration should avoid making proof search depend on allocator-address order unless reference compatibility requires it.
- `cte_simplesorts.c` includes `cte_functypes.c` directly rather than the header; Rust uses the normal module boundary. Keep an eye on duplicate-definition assumptions if build/link behavior is ever compared at C object-file granularity.
- `cte_termtrees` orders both higher-order types and term arguments by raw pointer address, so ordering can vary with allocator layout. Rust mirrors handle-identity ordering for sharing behavior, but later proof-search tests should identify whether any user-visible output depends on this order.
- `cte_termcellstore` hashes terms by f-code and shifted raw argument pointers, and its bucket distribution is therefore allocator-shaped. Rust uses the same handle identity inputs; benchmark term-bank hot paths before replacing this with a more deterministic structural hash.
- `TermCellStoreGCSweep` deletes terms whose `TPGarbageFlag` bits equal the requested state, so passing the empty state recovers unmarked cells. Rust preserves that behavior; future GC integration should make the mark-state contract explicit at call sites.
- `TermCellStorePrintDistrib` in C prints all 32768 buckets, including empty buckets. Rust keeps that exhaustive output; callers may eventually need a summarized diagnostic wrapper for routine debugging.
- `cte_termpos` stores explicit positions as alternating term pointers and child indices, and `TermPosGetSubterm` trusts the final stored superterm instead of re-walking from the root argument. Rust mirrors that live-pointer shape; future public APIs may want a validated path representation at module boundaries.
- `TermPosPrint` in C prints a newline after every dotted component except the first, so deeper positions can render across multiple lines. Rust preserves this formatting quirk in `print_string`; normalize only behind compatibility-aware display wrappers.
- `TermPosDebugPrint` uses a nullable `Sig_p` as a mode switch: null prints raw term addresses, non-null prints each stored superterm twice with different dereference modes and inherits the full problem-type-specific `TermPrint` surface. Rust keeps explicit address and term-bank-backed APIs; after compatibility is complete, callers may want separate diagnostic views instead of pointer-address output, overloaded mode selection, and printer-specific formula rendering.
- `cte_termcpos.h` declares `TermCPosFromTermPos` and `TermPosFromTermCPos`, but the paired C source does not define them. Rust provides explicit conversion helpers for internal use; compare linker/caller expectations before treating them as C-compatible exported surface.
- `cte_termtypes` mixes semantic term fields with allocation/tree-link fields in one flexible-array struct and mutates shared-term properties during traversals. Rust keeps the observable mutability but separates storage through safe handles; once term banks are complete, revisit whether property mutation should be scoped to explicit traversal contexts.
- `cte_termtypes` applied-variable dereference allocates and inserts normalized cache terms through the owning term bank. Rust now mirrors the top-node expansion shape and the rule that applied-variable dereferencing does not consume `DEREF_ONCE`, using no-cache expanded terms for the global `term_deref` helper; owner-bank cache freshness, cache-backed sharing, and wiring cache-backed WHNF dereference into global dereferencing remain separate LFHO integration work.
- `TermArrayNoDuplicates` in `cte_termfunc` copies and sorts the input array, but then compares adjacent entries in the original array rather than the sorted copy. Rust preserves the observable adjacent-only check for now; revisit when callers that depend on true duplicate detection are ported.
- `print_cons_list` in `cte_termfunc` depends on the global `SigSupportLists` and `TermPrintLists` switches, prints only proper `$cons` chains ending in `$nil`, and does not dereference the tail while validating list shape. Rust keeps list support explicit on `Signature` and preserves the proper-tail assertion; once parser/executable list options are complete, decide whether global formatting toggles need a compatibility shim or should stay explicit.
- `tb_parse_cons_list` in `cte_termbanks` uses a stack of tail placeholders while building a shared list, whereas the unshared `parse_cons_list` mutates the list spine directly. Rust builds the intended `$cons(head, tail)` spine from the parsed elements and inserts from `$nil` upward; compare this against reference parser traces before preserving any placeholder-stack edge behavior.
- `TermAddTypeDistribution` calls `GetHeadType`, which can insert a dropped phony-application head type into the signature type bank while counting a distribution that is documented as side-effect free. Rust requires mutable signature access and preserves that sharing side effect; later read-only statistics APIs should make this mutation explicit or pre-share application result types.
- `cte_termfunc` mixes pure unshared-tree helpers with term-bank, formula-printing, and higher-order application helpers. Rust now ports the local helper surface, unshared parser/printing, typed application encoding, prefix/application helpers, DB-closed/order checks, and the conservative no-WHNF applied-variable prefix rule; remaining higher-order work belongs to owner-bank cache-backed dereferencing/WHNF and full formula/term-bank ownership, not to the already-ported local helper API.
- `TermParseOperator` inherits the non-`STRICT_TPTP` C rule that an uppercase or underscore-starting identifier followed by `(` is treated as a free function symbol instead of a variable. Keep this for parser compatibility, but reconsider whether a strict-mode switch should expose the cleaner TPTP behavior after full parser compatibility is proven.
- `TermIsGroundCompute` checks only ordinary free variables, while cached `TermIsGround` trusts `TPIsGround` on shared terms. This means some higher-order/de Bruijn shapes can be treated as ground by collection helpers. Rust mirrors this, but higher-order groundness should be audited once lambda and DB-variable callers are fully ported.
- `TermFindIteSubterm` searches only child subterms, not the root term itself, and skips descent under lambda terms. Rust preserves that search surface; revisit root handling only with caller-level reference tests.
- `TermCopyUnifyVars` maps every free variable to `X0` with the variable bank's default individual type, ignoring the source variable types. Rust mirrors this for compatibility; revisit after typed callers that use `NSUnivar` are covered by reference tests.
- `TermAppEncode` assumes the original term and the directly applied argument already carry inferred types, then infers only the generated prefix term before allocating the typed-application symbol; the C allocation path does not visibly assign the generated application cell's result type even though `TermPrintTypes` later wants `term->type` when `EqnAppEncode` prints it. Rust preserves the input preconditions, stores the original return type on generated application cells so typed app-encode output has a concrete suffix, and should revisit whether upstream should set that field or guard `TermPrintTypes` if exact reference builds prove different behavior.
- `SubstBindAppVar` assigns the variable's type to the returned prefix before binding, even when `TermCreatePrefix` returns the original already-shared term or the hidden applied-variable head. Rust preserves this source mutation for compatibility; audit whether a future cleaned substitution API should avoid changing shared prefix type metadata at binding time.
- C stores eta-normalization policy as a process-global function pointer through `SetEtaNormalizer`; Rust mirrors that for compatibility and reapplies the parsed run configuration at every executable `run_config` entry so in-process runs cannot leak a previous `--eta-normalize` value. When higher-order normalization is owned by a proof-session API, make this state run/session-local instead of global.
- `SubstBacktrackSkolem` explicitly frees skolem terms in C. Rust clears the variable bindings and relies on handle ownership to drop unreferenced skolem terms; keep an eye on sharing once skolem terms become banked or indexed.
- `cte_termvars` fresh variables are only guaranteed fresh if direct f-code allocation is not mixed with fresh allocation. Rust mirrors the counter/stack behavior and exposes a shared temporary helper that copies live variable f-codes/types into short-lived fresh banks before skipping existing stacks for generation helpers; parser and clausification callers should avoid depending on stronger freshness than the C comments promise.
- `VarBankCollectVars` in C prints progress directly to stdout and loops with `i < max_var`, which misses a variable stored exactly at `max_var`. Rust preserves the loop-bound quirk and now exposes an explicit output-aware wrapper for the debug progress lines; keep ordinary variable collection on the quiet helper unless a caller needs C-compatible diagnostics.
- `cte_typebanks` hashes and orders shared compound types partly through raw argument pointer addresses, so traversal order for `TypeBankAppEncodeTypes` can depend on allocator layout. Rust prints application-encoding declarations in type UID order for deterministic output; compare against reference tests before deciding whether allocator-shaped order needs to be emulated.
- `TypeBankAppEncodeTypes` gets its `%-- ...` type-comment syntax from `TypePrintTSTP` and the process-global `problemType`, not from the type bank or formula set being rendered. Rust threads `ProblemType` explicitly through app-encode rendering; preserve that explicit dialect boundary in future type-printing APIs unless reference compatibility requires a global shim.
- `cte_typebanks` mutates incoming compound type objects in `force_arg_sharing` and frees duplicates after tree insertion. Rust keeps `Type` handles immutable and creates a normalized shared-argument replacement when needed, which avoids in-place ownership surprises while preserving shared-type identity for returned handles.
- `TypeBankParseType` depends on the global C `problemType` and has a few permissive first-order edge cases, including accepting a parenthesized product without a following return type as an arrow. Rust exposes an explicit-problem-type parser plus a global-state wrapper; revisit whether odd accepted shapes need exact reference tests before tightening syntax.
- `cte_typecheck.h` declares `TypeCheckEq`, `TypeCheckDistinct`, `TypeCheckArithBinop`, and `TypeCheckArithConv`, but the paired C source does not define them. Rust ports the functions that are actually implemented in `cte_typecheck.c`; revisit the header-only declarations when interpreted arithmetic and distinct-object checking are ported.
- `TypeCheckConsistent` in C iterates argument checks with `i < type->arity` even though arrow types include the return type as the last entry, which can read one past the term argument array for ordinary fully applied functions. Rust checks only actual term arguments and validates the return type separately; add reference tests around any caller that depends on this function before tightening compatibility further.
- `SigDeclareIsFunction` calls `TypeChangeReturnType` when a symbol's type is exactly `$o`, but `TypeChangeReturnType` asserts that the input is an arrow or `$i`; for arrow predicates, `SigDeclareIsFunction` fixes the symbol but leaves the Boolean return sort. Rust keeps these signature-helper shapes now that non-Boolean checked argument parsing is wired through the parser; revisit after full Boolean formula-argument parsing proves which ambiguity cases are externally observable.
- `cte_varhash` comments say its distribution walkers are called only with first-order arguments and do not change deref state. Rust exposes explicit deref-mode parameters, preserves ordinary variable dereference, and has the shared no-cache applied-variable expansion available from the termtype layer; cache-backed owner-bank metadata and any varhash caller that needs LFHO cache side effects remain deferred to the full term-bank owner slice.
- `cte_varsets` has a `valid` flag and comments saying reset invalidates the set, but `VarSetReset` and `VarSetCollectVars` do not mutate `valid` in the C implementation. Rust leaves the flag explicit and unchanged by reset/collect; revisit once all cache users are ported.
- `cte_termweightext` has a misspelled public enum name (`TermWeightExtenstionStyle`) and traverses subterms by pushing children left-to-right onto a LIFO stack, so callbacks see rightmost children first. Rust keeps a compatibility alias for the typo and preserves the traversal order; any future cleanup should happen behind clearer public wrappers.
- `cte_signature` has several compatibility quirks now mirrored in Rust: `SigSetPolymorphic` ignores its `value` parameter and always sets the bit, first-order duplicate names with different arities are silently renamed with an `_ARITYFIX... ` suffix, and `SigPopId`/`SigBacktrack` do not invalidate cached alpha ranks. These should be revisited only after signature users and reference behavior are covered.
- `SigSupportLists` is a process-global switch that changes every later signature allocation by inserting `$nil`/`$cons` into the fixed-code internal block. Rust records list support per `Signature`; if future executable options can flip list support dynamically, verify whether per-signature state or a process-global compatibility layer better matches reference timing.
- `SigGetNewTypedSkolem` is a macro over `SigGetNewTypedFCode` using the `esk` prefix but `newpred_count` and `FPDefPred`, not `skolem_count` and `FPSkolemSymbol`. Rust preserves that counter/property behavior; revisit the naming only behind compatibility tests.
- `SigSymbolUnifiesWithVar` contains broad `|| f_code != SIG_DB_LAMBDA_CODE || f_code <= 0` disjuncts, making almost every nonzero symbol report that it can unify with a variable. Rust mirrors this condition; audit whether the C intended a narrower DB-lambda/predicate check after higher-order unification callers are complete.
- `SigInterpreteNumbers` expands to `sig->null_code`, but the checked `SigCell` has no `null_code` field, the macro has no callers, and `eprover/DOC/NEWS` records removal of the old `--interprete-numbers` dead code. Rust intentionally does not add a placeholder; revisit only if a future reference source restores interpreted-number option plumbing.
- `SigFCodesCollectTypes` allocates a `PStack` named `to_process` but exits through `PTreeTraverseExit(to_process)` instead of a stack-freeing call. Rust uses normal vector ownership and does not mirror this deallocation mismatch.
- `SigPrintTypeDeclsTSTP` prints the raw internal `name`, while `SigPrintTypeDeclsTSTPSelective` prints `pname`; quoted symbols can therefore render differently between the two paths. Rust preserves this inconsistency until output consumers and reference tests define whether both should use printable names.
- `SigPrintAppEncodedDecls` numbers the first external symbol as `symboltypedecl2` because it prints `(i+1)-internal_symbols`, and it assumes every printed external symbol has a declared type. Rust preserves the numbering and precondition; revisit only after app-encoding output consumers are covered by reference tests.
- C `sig_print_operator` writes the no-type marker and trailing newline to `stdout` instead of the requested output stream. Rust now exposes an explicit C-compatible side-channel printer and uses it for `term2dag` file output, while ordinary in-memory signature debug helpers remain single-stream; compare any future executable callers against reference output before broadening the stream leak.
- `SigEnterLetScope` depends on a caller-provided `PStack` layout and reads only odd stack indices as f-codes, then mixes `SigFindFCode` quote stripping with raw `StrTreeFind`/`StrTreeStore` on the printable name. Rust exposes the f-code list directly and preserves ordinary shadow/restore behavior; quoted let-local names should be audited against the C reference before broad parser integration.
- `SigParseTFFTypeDeclaration` leaves the terminating full stop for its caller and routes declarations whose type is `$tType` or `$tType`-headed arrows into the type-bank constructor table. Rust preserves both rules with an explicit problem-type parameter instead of the C global `problemType`.
- The lazy negative branch of C `SigGetEqnCode` appears to set properties on `sig->eqn_code` instead of `sig->neqn_code`, which can assert if `$neq` is requested before `$eq`. Rust's lazy helper sets the properties on the created code to keep the API usable; compare against reference call order before deciding whether this bug needs a stricter compatibility shim.
- `cte_acterms` uses raw pointer ordering as a tie-breaker when storing structurally equal temporary AC terms in a tree. Rust keeps duplicate normalized arguments in a vector and sorts by AC comparison only; this preserves equality and rendering for indistinguishable duplicates, but revisit if later diagnostics or heuristics observe duplicate tie order.
- `cte_replace` stores a raw `struct clause_cell*` demodulator pointer in rewrite links. Rust currently stores an opaque nonzero demodulator id until clauses are ported; revisit the field type when clause ownership and `REWRITE_AT_SUBTERM` are represented directly.
- `TBTermPosReplace` from `cte_replace` is available for ordinary top and nested replacements through top copies and `TBInsertNoProps`, and its LFHO positive-`remains` prefix-rewrite branch now appends remaining original arguments before the final no-property insertion. Rust runs the available DB-lambda normalization subset there; exact C cache/owner-bank integration for normalized replacement paths remains pending.
- `cte_garbage_coll` registers raw `void*` clause/formula-set pointers in `PTree` sets. Rust currently models these as opaque nonzero IDs; replace them with typed stable handles once clause and formula set ownership is ported.
- `IndexFPfpCreate` stores the first positive function and predicate symbols it sees in process-static representatives, making later fingerprints order-dependent. Rust preserves this with explicit global state; later indexing code should decide whether reproducible representative selection is worth a compatibility switch.
- `TermFPSampleHO` strips lambda heads only while consuming a non-empty sampled position. Sampling the root of a lambda still returns the lambda f-code. Rust preserves that split; future LFHO diagnostics should decide whether root and non-root lambda treatment should be normalized behind a compatibility switch.
- `TermFPSampleHO` and `TermFPFlexSampleHO` classify any sampled index below `term->arity + TypeGetMaxArity(term->type)` as a DB-lambda/trailing-argument sample, even when the term is already fully applied. Rust keeps that bound and requires a typed term for this branch; a later higher-level sampler may want to use remaining arity instead.
- `FPTreeDelete` only checks `payload == NULL` at the exact key leaf; recursive parent pruning returns `count == 0` without checking a prefix node's payload. Rust keeps this delete shape for valid same-length fingerprint indexes, but variable-length discrimination-tree callers should be audited before relying on prefix payload retention.
- `FPIndexFindUnifiable` and `FPIndexFindMatchable` push the leaf payload pointer even when it is `NULL`, so the returned count can include empty leaves. Rust preserves that by collecting optional payload references; later public APIs may want a payload-only wrapper for ergonomic callers.
- `FPIndexPrintDot` uses raw pointer addresses as GraphViz node IDs and prints path/edge labels without DOT escaping. Rust preserves address-style IDs and raw labels for debug compatibility; stable node numbering and escaped labels would be better for reproducible user-facing graph output.
- `FPIndexPrintDot` renders payload nodes only for structural leaves returned by `FPIndexCollectLeaves`, while `fp_index_tree_print` prints every node that has a payload even if it also has children. Rust keeps the same split; a future high-level renderer may want to display internal payload nodes consistently.
- `FPIndexDistribPrint` divides entries by leaves without guarding an empty index. Rust preserves the direct floating-point division shape; a cleaned diagnostic wrapper should handle empty indexes explicitly.
- `PRINT_INDEX_STATS` in `eprover.c` guards null indexes for distribution summaries but not for the `pm_from_index` DOT graph. Rust's `print-index-stats` feature omits that graph when the index is absent; revisit only if byte-level compatibility with the C debug-build crash surface becomes necessary.
- C `PERF_CTR_ENTRY` uses one mutable start-time slot per timer, so nested or overlapping instrumentation for the same counter can overwrite the earlier start value. Rust's `instrument-perf-ctr` feature currently uses scoped RAII guards and accumulates nested elapsed spans for represented call sites; emulate the C single-slot behavior only if compatibility tests expose it, and add guards for remaining proof-pipeline call sites as their owners are ported.
- `ClauseInfoGetIdNameSpace` checks that the first namespace byte after `c_`/`i_` is a digit, but then calls `strtol(info->name+3, ...)`, skipping that digit. Rust preserves the resulting namespace values, such as `c_12_34 -> 2`; generated-name code should decide later whether this bug must stay externally visible.
- `ClauseInfoGetIdCounter` inherits C `strtol` acceptance for counters, including an empty counter becoming `0`, signs being accepted, and overflow saturating while `errno` is ignored. Rust mirrors those edge cases for compatibility with stored proof identifiers.
- `TPTPTypesCombine` only treats an exact `CPTypeAxiom` value as replaceable; an axiom value carrying other bits, such as `CPIsLambdaDef`, follows the later conjecture override or otherwise remains unchanged. Rust preserves the macro precedence and should revisit it when formula role merging is integrated.
- `ClauseSetCSSCPASource` clears only the four CSSCPA bits and then sets `prop*CP_CSSCPA_1` without range checking, so values outside `0..=15` can set unrelated clause flags. Rust keeps the unchecked encoding for now; callers should be audited before exposing a public source enum.
- `ClauseAlloc` partitions the incoming linked literal list into positives followed by negatives by relinking the existing cells, explicitly avoiding `EqnList` helpers for speed. Rust preserves the positive-then-negative order in an owned vector; benchmark before deciding whether later clause storage needs a specialized partitioning path.
- `EmptyClauseAlloc` does not initialize `ident`; only `ClauseAlloc` assigns from the process-global counter starting at `LONG_MIN`. Rust gives empty clauses an explicit `0` identifier and keeps allocated clauses on a monotonic atomic counter; revisit identifier initialization once parser/proof-output code decides which empty clauses are externally printed.
- `ClauseAddEvalCell` writes `evaluation->object = clause`, and clause copies deliberately drop both evaluations and source info. Rust now owns optional `EvalCell` storage on `Clause` and copy helpers drop it, but the object field remains an explicit optional handle until clause sets provide stable handles instead of raw back-pointers.
- `clause_copy_meta` preserves identifiers, literal counts, clause properties, dates, and proof-depth/size metadata, but deliberately drops evaluations, source info, derivation, feature vectors, set links, and list links. Rust copies the same core metadata and drops `ClauseInfo` plus derivation stacks; archive-copy helpers transfer those fields explicitly. Later proof reconstruction should make each omitted ownership boundary explicit.
- `ClauseNormalizeVars` asserts that the clause is not demodulation-indexed, builds a substitution, and only replaces the literal list if normalization created bindings. Rust preserves that mutation gate; future indexed-clause APIs should make the index-removal precondition impossible to miss.
- `ClauseCollectSubterms` differs from `EqnListCollectSubterms` by clearing `TPOpFlag` on all newly collected terms before returning. Rust preserves the cleanup in the clause wrapper; callers should prefer the clause helper when they need temporary collection without leaving visited flags set.
- `ClauseIsSemFalse` and `ClauseIsSemEmpty` return true for an empty clause because their C loops have no failing literal. Rust preserves the vacuous truth result; caller-level semantics should decide whether empty clauses deserve a separate explicit test in user-facing code.
- `ClauseStructWeightCompare` asserts that the cached `weight` field equals `ClauseStandardWeight` before comparing. Rust exposes the cached weight and comparison but does not force recomputation; callers that mutate literals should update `weight` or use a comparison path that recomputes it.
- `ClauseRemoveSuperfluousLiterals` and `ClauseRemoveACResolved` recompute literal counts but do not update the cached `weight` field in C, unlike `ClauseRemoveLiteralRef`. Rust recomputes weight after those cleanup helpers to preserve the local cached-weight invariant used by existing comparison/subsumption assertions; `ClauseRemoveSuperfluousLiterals` still preserves C's `DCNormalize` derivation push when cleanup removes at least one literal. Revisit the weight refresh if reference call paths depend on stale weights.
- `ClauseRemoveLiteral` removes by raw literal pointer in C. Rust currently offers value/index-based removal because literals are owned in a vector; replace this with stable literal handles if indexes or cloned literals become ambiguous in shared clause structures.
- `EqnPrint` and `ClausePrint` rely on mutable process-global output and equation-format flags. Rust's new first-order renderers take explicit format options and a term bank; keep the explicit shape unless final executable compatibility requires shared global state at the outermost process boundary.
- `ClausePCLPrint` temporarily changes the global `OutputFormat` to TPTP while printing a bracketed literal list. Rust preserves the resulting text through explicit TPTP equation options; avoid recreating the hidden global mutation unless a C-compatible FFI surface needs it.
- `print_initial` in `ccl_inferencedoc.c` writes two literal `XX` debug markers with `printf` in the PCL branch, so those markers go to stdout even when the documented output stream is redirected. Rust now preserves both the ordinary stdout interleaving and the separate `--output-file` stdout leak for compatibility; a future cleanup should only remove it behind an explicit compatibility decision.
- `PCLTypeStr` returns an empty string for every clause type except conjecture, question, and negated conjecture, collapsing axiom/plain/hypothesis/lemma/watchlist/unknown roles in PCL step prefixes. Rust preserves that compatibility surface; cleaned proof APIs should expose explicit role names only outside C-compatible PCL formatting.
- `pcl_print_end` uses `": 'wl,...'"` for non-compact watchlist comments but `" : '...'"` for ordinary non-compact comments and `" : 'wl'"` for watchlist-only output, so the watchlist-comment suffix has its own leading-space pattern. Rust preserves the exact spacing; a cleaned renderer should normalize spacing only outside C-compatible PCL output.
- `tstp_print_end` encodes watchlist plus comment as one quoted annotation atom such as `['wl,comment']`, rather than separate list entries. Rust preserves the byte-compatible shape; a later structured proof API should expose watchlist status and comments as separate fields before rendering.
- The C formula terminators duplicate the clause terminator comment handling but intentionally have no watchlist branch. Rust keeps the clause/formula split; a later proof-documentation model should make the documented target kind explicit rather than sharing an untyped suffix helper.
- Formula proof-documentation TSTP branches mix several hand-written spacing patterns, and formula definition introduction prints `introduced` in PCL but `introduced(definition)` in TSTP. Rust preserves those strings in reusable render helpers; a later formula-proof API should model operation identity separately from dialect-specific surface text.
- `DocClauseQuote`'s partner branch bypasses the ordinary suffix helpers and watchlist annotations, and `DocIntroSplitDefRest`/`DocClauseApplyDefs` ignore their comment arguments in favor of no comment or hard-coded `split`. Rust preserves these surfaces; a later structured proof-note API should keep partner references and caller comments as explicit fields before rendering.
- `DocClauseRewrite` trusts the term rewrite-link chain and asserts that it can reconstruct a nonempty demodulator sequence from the original term to the selected side. `DocClauseEqUnfold` ignores the actual demodulator positions and repeats one demodulator id for the stack length. Rust preserves both C shapes for proof-output compatibility, but a later structured proof API should make missing rewrite links and actual unfolded positions explicit.
- `eqn_li_normalform` couples normalization to verbose proof documentation through a global `OutputLevel >= 4` check, and each rewrite-doc line mutates the live clause id before the next side is normalized. Rust exposes this through an opt-in documented normalizer for compatibility; a cleaned API should separate rewrite computation, proof id allocation, and rendering once proof-control call sites all use the ported session model.
- Some exported proof-documentation helpers assign a new id only in PCL/TSTP branches, while `DocFormulaIntroDefs` assigns before unsupported-format fallback. Rust mirrors this branch timing so id streams stay C-compatible; simplify it only after output-format fallback behavior is no longer externally visible.
- `WFormulaGetId` can preserve input names through the mutable global `FormulasKeepInputNames`, while generated ids use the `c_0_...`/`i_0_...` namespace. Rust formula proof-documentation currently uses an explicit pre-rendered formula view and the session id source; revisit source-name preservation when real `WFormula` handles and formula source-name policy are represented.
- `ClauseParse` relies on process-global `ClausesHaveLocalVariables` and `ClausesHaveDisjointVariables` to clear external variable names before parsing. Rust exposes these as explicit parse options with the C defaults; model them as proof/session parser configuration rather than hidden process state.
- `ClauseSetParseList` uses `ClauseStartsMaybe` rather than a delimiter-aware list grammar, so a trailing term-start token causes a clause parse attempt and may fail later. Rust preserves this; add explicit list boundaries at higher parser layers rather than weakening the compatibility helper.
- `PStackClausePrint` uses the global `ClausePrint` output format but always requests full terms and appends suffix text before each newline; its TSTP branch also inherits the global problem type. Rust has a default LOP helper and an explicit output-format/problem-type wrapper; keep this boundary explicit rather than reintroducing hidden globals.
- `ClausePrintTPTPFormat` maps both `CPTypeConjecture` and `CPTypeNegConjecture` to the old TPTP role string `conjecture`, while `ClauseTSTPPrint` distinguishes `negated_conjecture`. Rust preserves that split in the TPTP and TSTP helpers; a cleaned API should make the selected output dialect explicit.
- `ClauseTSTPPrint` prints the role `axiom` only when the clause type is axiom and `CPInputFormula` is set; other axiom-typed clauses fall back to `plain`. Rust preserves this compatibility rule, but later internal renderers may want explicit source-role metadata instead of overloading an input-origin flag.
- `ClauseTSTPPrint` chooses `cnf`/`tcf`/`thf` from process-global `problemType` and routes typed first-order or higher-order non-empty clauses through formula closure. Rust makes `ProblemType` explicit and ports typed first-order plus represented higher-order clause-literal closure directly; full formula-owner decoding and rendering remain separate.
- `ClauseTSTPPrint`'s formula closure gets free variables from `TFormulaClosure`, where C first stores them in a pointer-keyed `PTree`, documents `PTreeToPStack` order as arbitrary, and then prints nested quantifiers from the resulting wrapping order. Rust uses deterministic variable-code ordering for this represented clause-literal subset; revisit only if future byte-for-byte tests require pointer-order emulation.
- `TFormulaTPTPPrint` prints literals through temporary `EqnAlloc`, flattens only the left spine of disjunctions, coalesces only adjacent repeated quantifiers with the same f-code, prints binary named-lambda cells as `^[...]`, and prints quantified variable names through `TermPrint` before appending required quantifier type annotations, so `TermPrintTypes` can duplicate type text such as `X:person:person`. Rust preserves these shapes in the explicit term-formula TPTP/TSTP helper, but a later non-compatibility renderer should avoid carrying these rendering and signature-mutation artifacts forward.
- `PStackClausePrintTSTP` and `PStackFormulaPrintTSTP` inherit the hidden `problemType` dependency from the C printers and have no item-level failure path in C. Rust's stack helpers take `ProblemType` explicitly, can render represented higher-order clause-literal closures, and expose diagnostics from the reusable clause/formula renderers.
- `PStackClauseDelProp` mutates clauses through raw pointers kept in the stack and assumes the stack does not own them. Rust preserves this with a `PStack<&mut Clause>` helper; future SInE selection code should use stable clause handles instead of cloning selected clauses if property deletion and set movement must affect owning sets.
- `PQueueStoreClause` and `PQueueStoreFormula` encode heterogeneous work items as adjacent raw queue entries rather than a typed pair. Rust keeps separate staged `IntOrP<&Clause>` and `IntOrP<&WrappedFormula>` queue shapes for compatibility tests and uses a typed `AxiomRef` payload for the mixed staged selector, but the compatibility queue still carries a separate tag and payload. A cleaned API should use a single enum work item at non-compatibility boundaries so consumers cannot desynchronize tag and payload entries.
- `DRelationTotalEntries` starts scanning at index 1, so any relation entry stored at function code 0 is ignored. Rust preserves that skip in the clause/formula D-relation shell; revisit only if signature initialization proves code 0 is impossible at this boundary.
- `compute_d_rel` in `ccl_f_generality` includes symbols with `f_code >= sig->internal_symbols`, whereas some neighboring reporting loops start at `internal_symbols+1`. Rust preserves the inclusive boundary; audit with full signature initialization before changing it.
- `compute_d_rel` assigns `least_gen*benevolence` from `double` to `long`, truncating fractional limits before comparing generality. Rust preserves the truncation; any cleaned API should make the rounding policy explicit.
- `GenDistribSizeAdjust` discards and zeroes the temporary `f_distrib` scratch array instead of preserving its contents. Rust mirrors that shape, but callers should keep the scratch array ephemeral and clear before resizing.
- `GenDistribPrint` prints the distribution cell address and uses `internal_symbols+1` for totals/row starts but `internal_symbols+limit` as the exclusive row bound, so `limit` prints one fewer external row than a count-style API would. Rust preserves the debug output shape; later user-facing diagnostics should avoid pointer identity and expose an explicit row-count policy.
- `TermTrimImplications` skips leading quantifiers and strips only a right-nested implication chain after seeing at least 10 implications. Rust preserves this for SInE symbol collection; after drop-in compatibility, replace the magic threshold/right-spine special case only behind reference tests for SInE axiom selection.
- `DRelPrintDebug` writes its final newline to `stderr` instead of the `FILE* out` stream after printing the `formulas:` line. Rust mirrors this by accepting a separate stderr writer; a cleaned debug API should keep all lines on the requested output stream.
- `SelectThreshold` returns the final cardinality of the result stacks, not the number newly selected, so a failed threshold can still return a nonzero value when the result stacks were pre-populated. Rust preserves that return shape for the clause-only and staged clause/formula helpers; later public APIs should name the return value explicitly.
- `SelectDefiningAxioms` checks `res > max_set_size` and `recursion_level > max_recursion_depth` only at the top of the queue loop, so it can select one object past the nominal max size and processes all entries already admitted at the current recursion marker before the next marker trips the depth check. Rust preserves that queue-marker behavior for clause-side and staged mixed GSinE selection; a cleaned API should expose whether limits are soft C compatibility limits or hard caps.
- `SelectAxioms` pushes no-symbol axioms into the result stack before `SelectDefiningAxioms`, but does not mark them `CPIsRelevant`; a no-symbol conjecture can therefore appear once from the no-symbol insertion and again as a seed. Rust preserves this duplicate in the clause-side and staged mixed selectors; revisit only with reference tests for no-symbol conjecture SInE behavior.
- C selected-axiom movement is raw-pointer based, so moving a duplicated selected pointer is effectively an owner transfer/idempotence question rather than a second allocation. Rust's executable represented GSinE path deduplicates selected clause identifiers and formula entry ids while moving owned axioms into fresh owner sets; revisit this once stable handles can model duplicate selected-object movement exactly.
- `SelectDefinitions` can distinguish original clause sets from formula sets, and Rust now exposes that exact staged formula-set selection helper. The supported `eprover` proof-search/CNF/prune path keeps parsed formula owners separate before SInE, and non-printing `--syntax-only` shares that represented input split before exiting, but remaining clause-bridge paths can still represent lowered formulas and original clauses in one axiom set, so those paths use aggregate raw-formula metadata and role bits; mixed CNF-plus-formula inputs can retain CNF conjecture/hypothesis clauses that C would ignore. Replace the remaining executable surrogates with exact owner handles once every parser/helper path is connected to executable formula ownership.
- C uses transient `CPIsRelevant` property bits on live clauses/formulas for duplicate suppression during SInE selection and clears them afterward. Rust's clause-side selector uses a local selected-pointer set instead of mutating clause properties, which preserves the final observable state and avoids borrowing conflicts; switch to handle-local mutation only if a later caller needs the transient flag side effect during selection.
- `ClausePrintDBG` uses a hard-coded `thf` wrapper, does not include the clause source number, and renders negative identifiers relative to `LONG_MIN`. Rust preserves that shape for compatibility; richer diagnostics should be added as separate non-compatibility output.
- `PermVectorComputeInternal` drops uninformative features but still forces a one-feature permutation when all features are uninformative by decrementing `start` after it reaches the vector size. Rust preserves that fallback; revisit whether an empty permutation would be clearer once FV-index compatibility tests exist.
- `FVCollectFreqVectorCompute` uses static reusable scratch storage through `RegMemProvide`; Rust currently allocates a local zeroed vector per call. Reintroduce scoped reusable scratch storage only after profiling the feature-vector index hot path.
- `FVPackedClause_p` is just a `FreqVectorCell*` in C, with `array == NULL` meaning an unpacked dummy and `clause` a raw owning pointer in packed-clause contexts. Rust uses an explicit `FvPackedClause` wrapper around an owned `Clause` plus an optional vector; replace the cloned clause payload path with stable clause handles when indexed proof-state clause-set ownership is complete.
- `FVIndexStorage` in C is an insertion-side storage counter: it includes `IntMapStorage` changes as successor maps grow or compact, charges new `FVIndexCell`s, can decrease when a successor map switches from tree to array, and does not decrement on deletion. Rust now preserves that constant-memory-estimate counter with a shadow C-shaped `IntMap` state beside the safe successor map; actual Rust heap usage should stay a separate profiling API.
- `FVIndexDelete` decrements the root and every traversed prefix count before confirming that the final leaf contains the clause, and it never prunes now-empty leaves or interior nodes. Rust preserves the count mutation and retained-empty-node shape; later index APIs should prevent accidental double-delete or missing-delete calls.
- `FVIndexPrint`/`fv_index_print` accept an output stream but print some recursive text and trailing newlines to `stderr`, while clauses go through global `ClausePrint`.
