Rust exposes compact debug output plus explicit-format combined tree strings; preserve or clean up the mixed-stream behavior only after output compatibility tests cover this path.
- `ccl_fcvindexing` stores raw clause pointers in final-leaf `PTree` sets, so identity is allocator-address based and duplicate inserts still increment prefix counts. Rust stores cloned clause snapshots keyed by the packed clause object's current identity; replace this with stable clause handles when clause sets own clauses.
- `ClauseCmpByLen` says in its comment that, at equal length, the clause with more positive literals is smaller, but the implementation returns `pos_lit_no(left)-pos_lit_no(right)`, so fewer positives compare smaller. Rust follows the implementation; revisit only with grounding-order reference tests.
- `ClauseSetEqlitRecode` returns the number of clauses in which at least one literal changed, not the number of recoded literals. Rust preserves the clause-count result; expose a separate literal-count helper later only if a caller needs it.
- `EqnEqlitRecode` changes an equational literal into a predicate literal and only clears `EPIsEquLiteral`, leaving any stale orientation/maximality-style literal properties untouched. Rust preserves all properties except the equational-literal bit; callers should recompute ordering/maximality before using recoded literals in search.
- `EqnCopy`, `EqnCopyRepl`, and `EqnCopyOpt` in C copy most literal properties after `EqnAlloc`; if dereferencing `$false` turns an equation into a Boolean predicate literal, debug builds can retain a stale `EPIsEquLiteral` bit that later trips `EqnIsEquLit` assertions. Rust keeps allocation-derived shape bits (`EPIsPositive`, `EPIsEquLiteral`, and pseudo-literal status) from the newly allocated literal while preserving caller-set metadata around them.
- `ClauseEliminateNakedBooleanVariables` uses term-variable bindings as temporary substitution state and rewrites each eliminated naked literal into a false sentinel before copying the whole list. The true-literal branch can leave C cached weights stale; Rust preserves the observable substitution and tautology behavior while making binding cleanup explicit and refreshing the local weight cache. After compatibility tests cover this path, consider replacing the binding side channel with a local assignment map.
- `ClausePrintDimacs` accepts an output stream but writes literal integers to `stdout` and only the trailing `0`/empty-clause workaround to `out`. Rust preserves this through explicit split-writer helpers while also keeping pure string renderers for callers that intentionally want one-buffer DIMACS output; later cleanup should choose one surface once executable DIMACS routing is fully wired.
- `ccl_groundconstr.h` declares `LitOccAddLitAdd` and `LitOccAddClauseAdd`, but `ccl_groundconstr.c` defines and internally calls the `Alt` spellings. Rust exposes both spellings as aliases over the same implementation; revisit the public names only if a future generated C-API compatibility layer needs to distinguish header declarations from implemented symbols.
- `LitOccTableAlloc` allocates `(maxarity + 1)` rows but initializes and later frees constraint trees only for `j < maxarity`, while accessors assert `pos < maxarity`. Rust allocates the same spare row but initializes all cells; preserve the inaccessible spare row only if raw storage accounting becomes observable.
- `LitOccTableAlloc` initializes every tracked predicate position as constrained with an empty allowed-term set. In constrained grounding, a variable position with no opposite-sign evidence can therefore receive no alternatives unless an opposite-sign free-variable literal marks that position unconstrained. Rust preserves this default-empty constraint behavior; relax it only with reference coverage for constrained grounding.
- `LitPosAddConstraint` sets a position unconstrained when it sees a free variable but does not clear any constraints already stored for that position. Rust preserves the stale set behind the unconstrained flag; callers must check the state before reading constraints.
- `ClauseCollectVarConstr` accepts `ground_terms` but never reads it. Rust keeps the parameter to preserve call shape; future APIs should either remove it or use it at initialization time with explicit tests.
- `varsetinstinitialize` starts every variable at the last alternative and `varsetinstnext` decrements the first variable that can move before resetting earlier exhausted variables. Rust preserves this reverse-stack, first-variable-fastest enumeration; changing it would reorder generated ground clauses.
- `VarSetInstFree` deliberately does not free the alternative stacks, while `VarSetConstrInstFree` does. Rust models this as optional owned alternatives on each cell plus a shared-alternatives setter; future APIs should make shared default alternatives versus owned constrained alternatives explicit before large-scale grounding.
- `GroundSetInsert` stores one `unit_terms[lit_no]` pointer for both positive and negative units while `unit_no` counts represented signs, so the later opposite-signed unit can overwrite the same term slot. Rust stores one term handle per literal code and the same sign-bit state; split term storage should wait until unit printing compatibility has reference coverage.
- `GroundSetFree` explicitly does not release dummy term references from the external `lit_bank`. Rust does not own a literal bank pointer in `GroundSet` and only keeps term handles; revisit bank-owned dummy references when full ground-instance creation is wired in.
- `GroundSetDimacsPrintMembers` adds the compact non-unit empty-clause count because each empty clause is printed as two complementary DIMACS clauses. Rust preserves that inflated print-member count; any later DIMACS cleanup must keep solver-compatibility tests for empty clauses.
- `GroundSetUnitSimplifyClause` deletes resolved literals and decrements cached positive/negative literal counts but does not update the cached clause weight or clear indexing-related clause properties. Rust recomputes literal counts while intentionally preserving the stale weight; future mutation APIs should decide whether exact cache staleness remains necessary after grounding callers are covered.
- `ClauseCreateGroundInstances` clears the active unit array and non-unit set when an empty ground instance is found, but leaves `unit_terms` and `max_literal` untouched. Rust preserves those inactive stale values; future `GroundSet` cleanup should only clear them after DIMACS/member/max-literal compatibility tests cover empty-instance discovery.
- `ClauseCreateGroundInstances` interleaves progress output and process-global `TimeIsUp`/`MemIsLow` checks with the instance loop. Rust public grounding helpers now mirror the global stop checks and completion-state updates, while the reusable helper keeps the stop source injectable for tests and internal callers. Progress output is available through explicit single-clause and clause-set output-aware wrappers with output-format dispatch rather than a hidden process-global renderer; `eground` uses those wrappers for its generation phase.
- `ClauseSetCreateGroundInstances` estimates the unconstrained instance count as `default_term_count ^ max_variables_in_any_clause`, and exits the process with `NO_ERROR` when `give_up` is exceeded. Rust exposes the same estimate as an explicit `EstimateLimitExceeded` outcome in the reusable helpers, and `eground` maps it back to C's direct success-status failure exit.
- `ClauseSetCreateConstrGroundInstances` checks `give_up` per clause as `GroundSetMembers(groundset) + varinstestimate(inst)`, after constraints have been computed. Rust preserves the per-clause estimate as an explicit outcome, and `eground` maps it back to C's direct `NO_ERROR` failure exit.
- `ccl_clausesets` stores set membership in each `ClauseCell` through raw `set`, `pred`, and `succ` pointers, so a clause can be linked into only one set and extraction can start from the clause pointer itself. Rust's initial plain owner stores exclusive `Clause` values and exposes id/order-based extraction; proof-state integration should move to explicit stable clause handles rather than recreating raw back-pointers.
- `ClauseSetRemoveSuperfluousLiterals` relies on `ClauseRemoveSuperfluousLiterals` mutating `clause->set->literals` through each clause's back-pointer. Rust recomputes the plain set's cached literal count after mutation; later indexed set owners should make cache updates explicit rather than hidden in clause-local cleanup.
- `ClauseSetApplyFun` is declared and documented as returning a `long` sum, but its C implementation stores the accumulator in `bool`, so repeated nonzero callback results collapse to `1` and negative callbacks can toggle the result back to `0`. Rust preserves this compiled behavior for compatibility; replace it with an actual sum only after call-site tests prove the quirk is unobservable.
- `ClauseSetNewTerms` drains clauses into a `PStack` and reinserts them by popping, so the set order reverses while copying terms to a new bank. It also asserts the copied clause weight equals `ClauseStandardWeight`, even though `clause_copy_meta` does not explicitly initialize `weight`. Rust preserves the order reversal and recomputes copied weights in the plain helper; revisit the C weight initialization once indexed clause-set copying is ported.
- `ClauseSetDerivationStackStatistics` prints directly to stdout, exposes the backing `PDArray` allocation size through zero-count bucket lines, and divides by the clause count without an empty-set guard. Rust preserves the printable histogram shape through an output-aware wrapper; a cleaned statistics API should return structured buckets and choose an explicit empty-set average policy.
- `ClauseConjectureOrder` is named as a conjecture scan, but the C implementation computes the maximum term order over every clause in the set. Rust preserves the full-set scan; split or rename only after proof-control callers make the intended domain explicit.
- `ClauseMarkMaximalTerms` sets `CPIsOriented` only after orienting and maximal-marking the current literal list, and relies on later literal mutations to clear stale orientation/maximality state. Rust preserves the all-or-nothing flag update; future mutation APIs should make invalidation explicit wherever literals can be replaced or reordered.
- `ClauseSetMarkMaximalTerms` is a straight set-order loop over `ClauseMarkMaximalTerms`, so it inherits each clause's orientation/maximality cache contract and performs no set-level cache validation. Rust preserves that layering; indexed or shared clause-set owners should keep cache invalidation at the clause/literal boundary.
- `ClauseNotGreaterEqual` is documented as side-effect free, but it clears and sets `EPHasEquiv`, `EPDominates`, and `EPIsDominated` while scanning and reserves a greater left literal for a later equal right literal. Rust preserves these temporary flags and the scan cursor behavior; future comparison APIs should separate pure comparison results from scratch marker state once all callers are audited.
- `ClauseSetFindFreqSymbol` resolves equal frequencies by taking the last matching non-special function symbol because both least and most scans update on `<=`/`>=`. Rust preserves that tie-break; change it only if heuristic reference tests show the tie is intentionally irrelevant.
- `ClauseSet` evaluation indices use safe sorted roots keyed by the C `EvalCompare` fields instead of storing the same raw `EvalCell*` nodes in per-position splay trees. Rust preserves best-clause ordering and root-clearing behavior; benchmark processed/unprocessed queues before deciding whether to recreate C splay locality for performance.
- C evaluation trees assume distinct live `EvalCell` values do not compare equal, relying on the process-global `eval_count` instead of a final object tie-breaker. Rust preserves C ordering for unique evaluation cells but adds the clause-set object handle as a final index tie-breaker so cloned evaluation cells cannot silently disappear from a safe sorted root; revisit the C invariant if evaluation cells are ever copied or shared deliberately.
- `RandWeightInit` stores its `long` range argument in an unsigned-int field and stores nonzero seed overrides in each random-weight evaluator, but `JKISSRandDouble` currently follows the C helper's process-global random state instead of consuming that per-evaluator state. Rust preserves the range wrapping and call shape; full heuristic-control tests should decide whether per-evaluator seeds need a compatibility switch or a cleaned implementation.
- `PARSE_OPTIONAL_AV_PENALTY` is compiled only under `ENABLE_LFHO` in C, so clause-weight parser support for the optional applied-variable multiplier depends on the reference build. Rust accepts the optional multiplier unconditionally because the current port includes higher-order applied-variable fields; revisit if a first-order-only compatibility mode is introduced.
- `LMaxWeightCompute` computes each literal's max-term temporary weight and applies the positive multiplier, but never adds that temporary value to `res`, so the function always returns `0`. Rust preserves the compiled behavior; change it only behind heuristic reference tests because fixing it would alter clause-selection strategy.
- `gd_term_weight` evaluates marked ground conjecture terms with `TermWeight(term, fweight, vweight)`, swapping the usual variable/function weight argument order, and stores the floating `goal_const + goal_multiplier * weight` expression in a `long`. Rust preserves both the swapped weighting and truncation; revise only behind goal-directed heuristic reference tests.
- `SimWeightInit` stores the parsed `equal_weight` parameter twice and `sim_eqn_weight`/`sim_weight` never read it; equal top symbols simply recurse over arguments. Rust stores and exposes `equal_weight` for source-compatible parsing but deliberately ignores it in scoring.
- `TPTPTypeWeightCompute` applies the conjecture multiplier only to exact `CPTypeConjecture`, not `CPTypeNegConjecture`, even though other helper predicates treat both as conjectures. Rust preserves the exact switch behavior; revisit only with clause-selection reference tests.
- `DepthWeightCompute` and `WeightLessDepthCompute` apply `app_var_mult` to left and right sides at different points in the depth/weight formula. Rust preserves this asymmetry; a cleaned formula would change higher-order applied-variable heuristic scores.
- `che_varweights` stores the OCB on the WFCB data cell and has the main type/signature/proof/depth/non-linear/PN-refined/symbol-type callbacks mutate clause orientation/maximality before scoring. Rust now exposes banked WFCB callbacks for proof-control evaluation and keeps explicit mutable-clause helpers plus immutable fallback scoring; remove the fallback split only after every scoring site can thread the OCB, mutable owner bank, and mutable clause without aliasing.
- `AxFilterAlloc` does not initialize the `threshold` field even though threshold filters later read it after parsing. Rust initializes it to `0` for safe construction; exact uninitialized-read behavior should not be exposed outside a compatibility harness.
- `AxFilterPrintBuf` prints `LambdaDef` but falls through into the default `assert(false)` branch because the `AFLambdaDefines` case has no `break`. Rust returns `LambdaDef` to keep the filter set printable; revisit only if reference builds expose the assertion as observable behavior.
- `AxFilterDefParse` formats anonymous names with `axfilter_auto%4lu`, which inserts leading spaces for small counters, and does not check generated names against user-provided names. Rust preserves the process-global counter and width-padding shape; a cleaned API could switch to collision-free names only behind parser/reference tests.
- `GeneralityMeasureNames` misspells the positive/negative formula names as `CoutPosFormulas` and `CoutNegFormulas`. Rust preserves those names for lookup and printing; cleaned aliases such as `CountPosFormulas` should be compatibility-mapped if added later.
- `GSinEParse` recognizes the full generality-measure table but rejects every measure except `GMTerms` and `GMFormulas` before the D-relation code, which would assert on the same unsupported measures. Rust preserves that diagnostic; implement or remove the apparent extra parser surface only after axiom-filter strategy tests cover it.
- `StaggeredWeightInit` casts `MAX(stagger_factor * ClauseSetMaxStandardWeight(axioms), 1)` to `long`, so an empty axiom set uses max weight `-1` and still gets a stagger limit of `1`; `StaggeredWeightCompute` then uses integer division. Rust preserves the truncation, empty-set limit, and integer-division result.
- `TermAddVarDistribution` and the clause variable-set helpers count variables by negated free-variable f-code, not by variable-cell identity. Rust preserves that code-based distribution; later typed variable APIs should keep this distinct from identity-based variable collection.
- `eqn_tptp_depth_info_add` treats predicate literals as conventional TPTP atoms by counting only atom arguments, not the predicate head or the `$true` right side. Rust preserves this depth statistic; any cleaned API should name the TPTP interpretation explicitly.
- `TermAddVarDistribution` asserts that every non-free term has a positive f-code, so DB-variable or malformed higher-order shapes would fail in this helper instead of being counted as variables. Rust keeps the same precondition while higher-order feature extraction remains incomplete.
- `TermComputeFunctionRanks` starts with rank counter `0` while `0` also means "unranked", so the first symbol encountered in post-order can be reassigned if it appears again and otherwise remains indistinguishable from an absent symbol. Rust preserves this positional-rank sentinel behavior for `che_fcode_featurearrays`; revisit only with ordering-generation compatibility tests.
- `FCodeFeatureArrayUpdateSymbKey` applies Skolem/definition-predicate modifiers by array position `i`, not by each cell's stored `symbol`. C expects this to run before sorting; Rust preserves the same positional precondition and should not silently support post-sort updates without a compatibility decision.
- `FCodeFeatureArraySort` compares `key0..key3` and `pos_rank`, but not `freq` or `symbol`, and starts sorting at `SIG_TRUE_CODE + 1`, leaving `$true` fixed while `$false` can move. Rust keeps that comparator and boundary; later cleanup could add an explicit final tie-breaker only if generated-ordering tests permit it.
- `che_clausesetfeatures` uses polarity macros for several names containing "axiom": `ClauseSetCountAxioms` means non-goal clauses, and `ClauseSetCountPositiveAxioms`/`ClauseSetCountGroundPositiveAxioms` do not inspect TPTP axiom type. Rust preserves the literal-shape semantics; later APIs should avoid TPTP wording unless they actually query clause type.
- `ClauseSetTermCells` sums `ClauseWeight(..., false)` into a `long`, relying on C's double-to-integer truncation. Rust preserves the truncating conversion for this feature counter.
- `SpecLimitsAlloc` initializes `perc_form_defs_large_limit` with `DEFS_PERC_MEDIUM_DEFAULT` instead of `DEFS_PERC_LARGE_DEFAULT`; `CreateDefaultSpecLimits` later overwrites it with the large default. Rust preserves both initializer shapes.
- In relative-limit mode, `SpecFeaturesAddEval` classifies non-ground-unit and ground-positive content from the ratio fields already present on the feature cell, then recomputes those ratios later in the same function. Rust preserves that order; later callers should avoid relying on stale ratio inputs unless compatibility requires it.
- `SpecFeaturesPrint` prints higher-order tail fields (`order`, definition counts/ratios, applied-variable literal ratio, boolean quantification, and defined choice), but `SpecFeaturesParse` still expects the older vector ending at `clause_avg_depth` before `): class`. Rust preserves the separate print and legacy parse surfaces; do not assume round-tripping without a compatibility adapter.
- `SpecFeaturesCompute` calls `ClauseSetComputeHOFeatures`, then overwrites both `order` and `goal_order` with `1` before scanning formula sets. When no formula set/archive is supplied, final order therefore ignores clause-level HO order; Rust preserves this in the clause-set helper, but a cleaned future API should expose the raw clause HO order separately from the strategy-classification order.
- `SpecFeaturesCompute` sets `num_of_definitions = -1` but does not assign `perc_of_form_defs`; C consumers that run `SpecFeaturesAddEval` classify the existing ratio value. Rust preserves this in the full wrapper and leaves the ratio untouched in the clause-set helper. Formula-definition ratios belong to raw-spec feature extraction or a future cleaned API, not to the C-compatible `SpecFeaturesCompute` surface.
- `SpecTypeString` accepts masks of length 13 through 22 but returns only the first 21 classification bytes; a 22nd mask byte can only touch the C buffer terminator and is not observable in the returned string. Rust accepts the same mask length range and masks only returned bytes.
- `SpecFeaturesParse` accepts `G`, `H`, or `U` for axiom class but only `H` or `U` for goal class, even though `SpecTypeString` can encode general goals as `G`. Rust keeps that parser restriction.
- `RawSpecFeaturesPrint` omits `has_choice_sym`, but `RawSpecFeaturesParse` expects that field before `order`; Rust mirrors the separate parser and printer shapes, so current printed raw-spec lines are not parse-round-trippable without an inserted choice-symbol flag.
- `RawSpecFeaturesParse` requires a 14-byte class string but reports "Raw class name must have 10 characters"; Rust preserves the accepted length and diagnostic text.
- `RawSpecFeaturesClassify` applies dash masks without checking the pattern length in C, which can overrun the fixed 16-byte class buffer for long masks. Rust applies masks only to the 15 class positions; later CLI validation should make the intended maximum explicit.
- C raw-spec auto classification reads pre-CNF formula-set data for sentence counts, conjecture/hypothesis counts, order, and formula standard weight. Rust now has represented formula owners for the supported `eprover` proof-search/CNF/prune/syntax-only parser path, but raw-spec classification and remaining bridge callers still preserve parser-side raw formula features for unsupported fragments and compatibility calibration; replace that fallback with exact `WFormula`/formula-term feature extraction once full parser/formula ownership is ported everywhere.
- `ClauseSetCollectSigFeatures` accumulates into the supplied vector without clearing it and adds every external signature symbol to the global signature block, whether or not it occurs in the clause set. Rust preserves both behaviors; callers should initialize explicitly before collection.
- `TermCollectSigFeatures` ignores variables and other negative f-codes entirely, and records maximum function-symbol depth only for non-predicate symbols. Rust preserves that term-feature surface.
- `EqnCollectSigFeatures` always collects both literal sides, so ordinary predicate literals also contribute the internal `$true` right-hand side to signature features. Rust preserves the literal-cell representation instead of filtering the truth side.
- `che_normsubst.h` declares `NormSubstFree`, but the paired C file in this checkout only defines `NormSubstAlloc`. Rust provides a consuming drop helper for the intended ownership shape; audit any future C-reference linkage expectations before relying on a separate free symbol.
- `ComputeTopGeneralizations` starts child traversal at argument index `1`, so symbols reachable only through the first child of a compound term do not get top-level generalizations. Rust preserves this traversal surface; revisit only with reference tests because it can change relevance weighting.
- `TuplePrint` prints a trailing comma before the closing parenthesis, including `(1,2,)`. Rust preserves this debug/output shape.
- `ComputeSubtermsGeneralizations` allocates a new fresh generalizing variable on every visit of a term entry and then includes all variables previously allocated for that entry in the local generalization list. Rust preserves the visit-history-dependent variable set.
- `TBIncSubtermsFreqs` skips only free variables before counting a subterm; other zero-arity cells such as DB-variable-shaped cells are not filtered by this helper. Rust keeps the same free-variable-only check.
- `OrderParmsParseInto` accepts any integer into the `LiteralCmp` enum field and only later users can notice invalid values. Rust stores the parsed value as an integer in the C-shaped parameter cell; revisit validation only after ordering-control compatibility tests cover malformed configuration behavior.
- `OrderParmsParseInto` uses `PARSE_STRING_AND_CONVERT` for `ho_order_kind` but passes the raw token literal to `STR2HOK`; a quoted string such as `"lfho"` is therefore rejected even though the macro accepts `String|Identifier`. Rust preserves this raw-literal behavior; revisit only if the public config grammar is intentionally widened.
- `TOPrecGenNames` and `TOWeightGenNames` shift when C is compiled without `ENABLE_LFHO`. Rust currently ports the LFHO-enabled layout used by the higher-order-capable configuration; revisit if the Rust build needs to emulate non-LFHO C binaries.
- `TOGeneratePrecedence` treats `PNoMethod` differently depending on whether a predefined precedence string was supplied: with a predefined string it leaves the parsed precedence untouched, otherwise it falls through to `PUnaryFirst`. The current Rust pure generator models the no-predefined fallthrough, while the OCB-backed installation path preserves the predefined branch.
- `generate_invfreq_conjmin_precedence` comments say conjecture symbols are larger, but the code assigns conjecture symbols key `0` and non-conjecture symbols key `1` before sorting low-to-high. Rust preserves the code path; revisit naming/comment cleanup only after generated-ordering reference tests exist.
- The LFHO type-frequency precedence methods allocate `type_counts` from the current type-bank size before `ClauseSetAddTypeDistribution` runs. Rust preserves that C-sized distribution precondition; revisit if later higher-order type traversal can introduce fresh type IDs during distribution.
- `init_oparms` in `che_to_autoselect` assigns only a subset of `OrderParmsCell` fields, leaving fields such as `skolem_mod`, `defpred_mod`, `rewrite_strong_rhs_inst`, `to_pre_prec`, `to_pre_weights`, and `to_defs_min` untouched or uninitialized depending on the caller. Rust preserves this by only mutating the same fields; revisit once every auto-ordering caller starts from a fully initialized parameter cell.
- `TOCreateOrdering` in `che_to_autoselect` uses only `pre_precedence != NULL` to select matrix-backed precedence. If `PNoMethod` and a precedence-dependent KBO weight method are combined, the C code lets weight generation consult the partial matrix via `OCBFunCompare`; Rust preserves this without inventing a total order.
- `TOCreateOrdering` in `che_to_autoselect` copies the raw `params->lit_cmp` integer directly into `ocb->lit_cmp`; Rust validates it against the typed `LiteralCmp` enum. If invalid strategy files are later found to rely on carrying arbitrary enum values, model the raw field at the boundary before conversion.
- `OrderEvaluate` in `che_to_autoselect` mutates the axiom clause set by marking maximal terms before computing the ordering score. Rust preserves the side effect, with a bank-backed evaluator for owner-bank ordering preparation; a cleaned scoring helper should make mutation explicit in its name or score a cloned/scratch clause set.
- `TOSelectOrdering` in `che_to_autoselect` accepts a `SpecFeature_p specs` argument but never reads it, and it sets `rewrite_strong_rhs_inst` from the original heuristic parameters after creating or optimizing the local ordering copy. Rust preserves the observable non-optimizing path; revisit the unused `specs` hook only when auto-schedule selection is wired.
- `OrderFindOptimal` in `che_to_autoselect` builds local `OrderParmsCell` values by assigning only `ordertype`, `to_weight_gen`, `to_prec_gen`, and `to_const_weight` before calling `TOCreateOrdering`; fields such as `lit_cmp`, `ho_order_kind`, and lambda/DB weights can therefore be indeterminate in C. Rust copies the initialized mask fields and then overwrites the four C search fields, avoiding undefined stack reads while preserving the candidate order. It also treats `Optimize` as the documented wildcard ordering mask because the C CLI path that would set `OPTIMIZE_AX` is currently commented out and a direct handoff would not be handled by `OrderFindOptimal`. Revisit this cleanup only if a reference path makes raw `OPTIMIZE_AX` optimizer behavior observable.
- `generate_autocasc_ordering` and `generate_autodev_ordering` in `che_to_autoselect` stack-allocate `OrderParmsCell` and initialize only selected fields before calling `TOCreateOrdering`; fields such as `db_w`, `lam_w`, `force_kbo_var_weight`, and modifier values may be indeterminate in C. Rust deliberately initializes the full parameter cell for CASC/Dev and then applies the visible C assignments; revisit only if reference strategy tests prove an accidental stack value affects observable behavior.
- `OrderNextConstWeight` documents a cycle up to `MAX_CONST_WEIGHT`, but its assertion permits any positive value and values above `2` are incremented rather than wrapped. Rust preserves that over-max increment behavior for compatibility with malformed/internal states.
- `OrderNextOrdering` calls the relevant `OrderNext*` helper a second time after a wrapped `false` result to reset the parameter to the first candidate. Rust preserves this reset artifact; cleaned iterators should expose it only behind an explicit compatibility adapter.
- `ConjectureLevDistanceWeight` parses edit costs as signed `int`, but the dynamic-programming columns and intermediate costs are `unsigned int`, so negative costs wrap to large unsigned values. Rust preserves this in the distance core; a future public parser should decide whether to reject negative costs before exposing a cleaned strategy format.
- `FunWeightParse` and `SymOffsetWeightParse` parse every trailing comma after the positional multipliers as a `symbol:weight` pair before the optional applied-variable multiplier macro is reached. Rust preserves this by rejecting a bare trailing numeric multiplier for these two parsers; a future cleaned strategy syntax could make the app-var multiplier explicit instead of positionally ambiguous.
- `ConjectureRelativeSymbolWeightParse` multiplies ordinary weights by a `double` and assigns the results to integer fields, so fractional products truncate toward zero despite the source comment saying they are rounded down. Rust preserves the cast; revisit only after heuristic-score compatibility tests can tolerate cleaned rounding semantics.
- `EqnTermExtWeight` always applies `max_term_multiplier` to the left side but applies it to the right side only for unoriented literals. Rust preserves this orientation asymmetry for term-extension clause weights; revisit only with clause-selection reference tests because changing it would alter all conjecture term-distance heuristics.
- `strc_terms_distance` in `che_strucweight` falls back to generalization/instantiation only when both top f-codes and arities differ. Different same-arity constants can therefore have zero structural distance, and same-symbol arity mismatches fall through to a left-arity argument loop. Rust preserves this condition and should only tighten it behind heuristic-score reference tests.
- `ted_lmld_kr` in `che_treeweight` treats only free variables and non-variable constants as leaves. A bare DB variable is non-free and non-constant, so it falls through to argument-0 traversal even though DB variables have arity zero. Rust preserves this as a panic on the missing argument; revisit with LFHO tree-distance reference tests before deciding whether DB variables should be leaves.
- `DetectAssociativity` checks `TermStandardWeight(lit->lterm)` twice and never checks the right term's weight before matching shape. Rust preserves the effective left-side-only weight gate.
- `ClauseScanAC` returns `true` for detected commutativity, but after detecting associativity it sets the signature property and then returns `false`. Rust preserves the return value, so callers should not treat `false` as proof that no signature mutation occurred.
- `ClauseContextualSimplifyReflect` keeps raw `Eqn_p` pointers on a stack while repeatedly sorting the same clause, so pointer identity survives reordering and deletion. Rust matches by literal properties and term handles while ignoring the mutable position field; revisit if duplicate literal identity becomes observable before superfluous-literal cleanup.
- `TFormulaSimplify` uses propositional constants encoded as `$eq($true,$true)` and `$neq($true,$true)`, applies duplicate rewrites with pointer identity, and gates redundant-quantifier removal behind cached formula weight. Rust preserves those choices with term-handle identity; a future formula layer should split constant representation, equality policy, and budgeted quantifier cleanup into explicit concepts.
- `TFormulaSimplifyDecoded` flattens decoded `and`/`or`, sorts the resulting term pointers with `PCmp`, removes pointer-identity duplicates, and then folds the sorted stack back into a binary formula. Rust mirrors this through stable term-handle identity so clause simplification stays C-compatible; a cleaned formula layer should prefer structural/deterministic canonicalization only after proof-search and proof-output reference tests show allocation-order changes are acceptable.
- Unary decoded `and`/`or` simplification first simplifies the single child, but if that child is neither the neutral nor absorbing Boolean constant, C returns the original unary cell rather than the recursively simplified one. Rust preserves that for compatibility; a future formula-normalization API should decide whether this discarded child simplification is intentional.
- Decoded quantifier simplification recognizes unary `$qex`/`$qall` cells containing a DB lambda even though the normal signature declares those quantifier symbols as binary. Rust accepts the same shape for compatibility; a future typed formula representation should distinguish decoded binder/lambda forms from ordinary signature arity.
- `WFormulaSetUnrollFOOL` calls `WFormulaReplaceEqnWithEquiv` before `TFormulaUnrollFOOL`, so complex Boolean `$eq`/`$neq` formula terms become equivalence/XOR or unwrap/negate around `$true` before the Boolean-subterm split only when the optional FOOL-unroll pass runs. `TFormulaUnrollFOOL` then expands encoded literals, and `do_fool_unroll` finds only the first nontrivial Boolean subterm per literal-side scan, preferring the left literal side before the right, and recurses over the generated `$true`/`$false` cases. It skips `$true`, `$false`, Boolean variables, and Boolean-variable/truth encodings, while its own comments still leave `$ite` handling as TODOs. Separately, `TFormulaSetLiftItes` calls `do_ite_unroll` in the LFHO preprocessing branch, rewriting formula-position `$ite(C,T,F)` as `(~C|T)&(C|F)` and term-position `$ite` by replacing the nearest enclosing formula position without running Boolean equality replacement when `--fool-unroll` is disabled. Rust mirrors the term-level FOOL split behavior and stages the formula-set ITE owner path for direct formula-position Boolean `$ite`, first literal-side term-position `$ite`, and left-before-right scan order; parser-supported proof-search/CNF/prune `$ite` owners now route through represented formula owners with higher-order promotion so they reach that formula-set ITE path, while remaining bridge-only FOOL spellings still use the temporary executable lowering. Full formula ownership should still make search order, lambda and applied-term cases, ignored cases, and pass ordering explicit before retiring the fallback bridge.
- The temporary bridge intentionally treats `$let` internals as a let-lifting concern rather than a FOOL Boolean-subterm concern; a future unified formula owner should keep that boundary visible before deciding whether to simplify the phase ordering.
- `TFormulaSetLiftLets` couples several concerns in one pass: variable renaming, f-code-keyed local-definition replacement, fresh `esk`/`epred` allocation over captured plus formal variables, Boolean-definition equivalence construction, `unencode_eqns`, formula-set insertion, and `DCIntroDef`/`DCApplyDef` provenance updates. Rust now mirrors this in the formula-set owner, including the generated-definition and source-formula derivation entries; full proof-state ownership should still split role handling, capture lists, replacement maps, and proof-document side effects into typed stages before deciding whether C's f-code-keyed replacement map is a compatibility requirement or just an implementation shortcut.
- `WTFormulaConjunctiveNF` and `WTFormulaConjunctiveNF3` interleave destructive formula mutation, proof-documentation calls, derivation-stack pushes, and the fresh-variable counter boundary. Rust now exposes term-level wrappers that return the transformed formula plus C derivation opcodes for changed phases, while preserving C's untracked literal-expansion side effect inside FOOL unroll; the final formula owner should attach proof side effects explicitly and keep this phase/change-check contract testable.
- `TFormulaLitAlloc`, `TFormulaClauseEncode`, `TFormulaClauseClosedEncode`, `TFormulaCollectClause`, and `TFormulaToCNF` combine C's problem-type-sensitive literal encoding, empty-clause false formula construction, left-to-right OR folding without closure, explicit universal closure for closed clause encoding, stack-order CNF collection, clause allocation's positive-before-negative grouping, silent fallthrough for unexpected non-literal leaves, formula-parent derivation metadata, represented split-clause proof-document output, naked Boolean-variable cleanup, and optional higher-order post-CNF formula encoding. Rust mirrors those artifacts in the staged helpers; a full formula/CNF owner should make invalid-shape handling, proof-document session ownership, closure ownership, and construction-order compatibility explicit.
- The C formula header exposes several important operations as macros over term internals: identity equality, no-properties/deref-always copying, term-bank GC marking, and maximum-variable scanning. Rust now has explicit formula wrappers for those macros; a full formula owner should keep identity handles, copied-term interning, and GC roots first-class rather than relying on macro-shaped aliases.
- `TFormulaIsComplexBool` appears to pass a term cell to `TypeIsBool`, so it effectively checks the logical term's f-code against the Boolean sort code rather than checking `form->type`. Rust preserves that macro artifact in the direct wrapper; the eventual formula owner should expose a real typed-Boolean predicate separately from this compatibility check.
- `TFormulaVarIsFree` is a faster direct query than `TFormulaCollectFreeVars`: it trusts cached `v_count`, checks term identity before descending, and treats only `$qex`/`$qall` as binders, so named-lambda binder variables are traversed as ordinary children. Rust preserves that mismatch and exposes the header-declared but commented-out `TFormulaVarIsFreeCached` as a compatibility alias to the direct query rather than inventing a term-bank free-variable cache.
- `TFormulaTPTPPrint` shares literal allocation and term-printing side effects with formula construction rather than being a pure renderer. Rust mirrors that in the direct helper by requiring a mutable term bank; full formula ownership should keep compatibility rendering separate from pure pretty-printing.
- `TFormulaAppEncode` coalesces only adjacent repeated quantifiers of the same f-code, flattens only the left spine of `|`, treats `TFormulaIsQuantified`'s non-existential cases as universal during printing, and shares `PreloadTypes`' dependence on discarded term app-encoding to populate intermediate type and typed-application declarations. Rust mirrors those artifacts in the staged helper; a full formula owner should split quantifier grouping, lambda handling, disjunction rendering, and declaration preloading into explicit phases.
- `TFormulaAddQuantors` converts the pointer-keyed free-variable `PTree` with `PTreeToPStack`, whose own C documentation calls the order arbitrary, before wrapping quantifiers. Rust's staged closure uses term-handle identity order from the local collector, while direct add-quantors callers provide an explicit slice order; a full formula owner should decide whether user-facing output wants deterministic quantifier ordering or exact allocator-shaped compatibility.
- `TFormulaStackToForm` destructively pops the last pushed formula as the initial result, folds remaining popped formulas on the left, and returns `$true` for an empty stack regardless of the requested connective. This makes empty or singleton helper-level `$distinct` expansions true even though executable `$distinct()` syntax is rejected by `TSTPDistinctParse` before expansion; Rust pins that direct empty/singleton helper fallback. The pair order depends on the pair-push order plus destructive stack fold, so keep that construction artifact explicit until formula-set `$distinct` processing has reference output coverage.
- `TFormulaTPTPParse` applies one same-precedence right-recursive binary parse and leaves formula-level `=`/`!=` as `$eq`/`$neq` formula operators instead of TSTP's Boolean equality-to-equivalence/XOR rewrite. Rust mirrors that in the direct parser; a full parser should keep old-TPTP dialect quirks separate from normalized formula semantics.
- `TFormulaTSTPParse` first parses an ordinary atomic predicate as a Boolean formula operand and only then lets the surrounding non-associative operator parser consume `=`/`!=`; `EqnParseInfix` also diverts typed Boolean-left equalities to `TFormulaTSTPParse` for the RHS. That token flow means an atomic left operand with a formula-only right operand, or any typed Boolean atomic left operand with `=`/`!=`, is Boolean equivalence/XOR, while a non-Boolean atomic left operand with a term-shaped right operand, including a visible `@` application such as `c = (h @ a) @ b` or an LFHOL user-`bool` partial application such as `(col @ (lam @ A)) = A`, can stay ordinary term equality. Rust mirrors this in the executable bridge with a narrow TSTP lookahead; the full parser should encode the distinction directly instead of relying on token-start classification or type names.
- `TFormulaTSTPParse` lets the `Application` branch follow any parsed literal, and `literal_tform_tstp_parse` can also manufacture zero-arity logical operator heads or parse named-lambda literals for immediate `@` application. Typed first-order formula starts and equality terms such as `p @ a`, `(p) @ a`, `(h @ a) @ b = c`, `c = (h @ a) @ b`, `(&) @ p(a) @ q(a)`, and `(^[X: $i]: p @ X) @ a` therefore flow through the same term-encoded formula application path as higher-order-looking syntax. Rust mirrors this in the staged term-bank parser, and plain represented FOF/TFF formula-owner routes now enter that parser directly when it accepts the full body, while ordinary represented TCF formula-owner routes enter the staged `TcfTSTPParse` helper. Parser-rejected lambda/FOOL spellings and remaining executable FOF/TFF/TCF bridge paths still use narrow lookahead to detour supported first-order wrappers into that parser; the full parser should own this as explicit formula application rather than preserving the executable lookahead shim.
- `TSTPDistinctParse` parses each argument with `FuncSymbParse`, not the scoped term parser, so variable-looking names are accepted as zero-arity constants and then only same-type constant-ness is checked. Rust mirrors this in the direct parser and in the executable formula bridge's supported `$distinct(...)` lowering; a full formula parser should make the constant-only syntax explicit before deciding whether strict TPTP variable-looking names are rejected outside compatibility mode.
- `TcfTSTPParse` accepts only leading universal quantifiers, recursively nests comma-separated binders, switches to a clause-specific literal parser for unquantified bodies and for parenthesized bodies under the `tcf` quantified path, and parses unparenthesized quantified bodies as one atom. Rust mirrors those direct-helper details; a future formula owner should make typed-clause parsing a distinct mode instead of carrying C's Boolean `tcf` parser flag forward.
- `TFormulaDefRename` and `TFormulaFindDefs` use shared term-cell `TPCheckFlag` markers and `entry_no` keys to track definitions, while later definition introduction reuses the same `NumXTree` slots for virtual definition id, real blocking id, and archived polarity-zero formula pointer. Rust preserves this with an explicit staged definition-entry cell; full formula-set ownership should replace marker flags and slot reuse with typed phase-specific handles.
- `tformula_rename_test` uses `pos==2` to decide the implication subformula sign even though callers pass `0` or `1`, so positive implication consequents are tested as negative subformulas for renaming. Rust preserves the observable candidate selection until definitional-CNF reference tests prove that fixing the condition is safe.
- `TFormulaCreateDef` depends on polarity flags previously written to shared formula cells and quantifies over variables collected from the generated definition atom rather than the defined formula itself. Rust mirrors this marker-state contract; full formula-set ownership should replace it with explicit polarity maps and definition dependency lists.
- `TFormulaMiniScope3` uses `tform_find_miniscopeable` to collect pointer-keyed maximal candidates, counts non-quantifier size as `size += size + child_size`, and installs candidate replacements through temporary `Term.binding` fields before copying the original formula. Rust preserves that identity-based, binding-based phase; a future formula owner should make candidate handles and the miniscope work budget explicit.
- `TFormulaSkolemizeOutermost` seeds dependencies with `TFormulaCollectFreeVars`, which mutates `TPIsFreeVar` flags and drains a pointer-keyed `PTree`, then `tformula_rek_skolemize` mutates existential variable bindings while recursively copying affected subterms. Rust uses local stacks and restore guards while preserving the dependency order by term-handle identity; replace this with explicit formula-owner dependency/substitution maps when full CNF ownership is ported.
- In C `tformula_rek_skolemize`, the non-logical Boolean-term branch assigns the copied formula to `handle` but never returns it through `form`. Rust returns the copied/dereferenced Boolean term so existential bindings are applied consistently with literals and `TFormulaVarRename`; keep this divergence covered by reference tests before deciding whether a strict-compatibility mode should reproduce the C bug.
- `ClauseSimplifyWithUnitSet` calls `ClauseSetProp(pos->clause, ClauseQueryProp(clause, CPIsSOS))`; because `ClauseQueryProp` returns a boolean, an SOS target sets bit `1` (`CPInitial`) on the subsuming unit instead of `CPIsSOS`. Rust preserves that property side effect for compatibility; revisit after unit-simplification proof-search reference tests decide whether it is an accidental bug.
- `FindSimplifyingUnit` contains a `problemType == PROBLEM_HO` early return immediately before the same return that first-order code reaches anyway. Rust omits the redundant branch while preserving the lookup result; revisit only if future higher-order indexed matching makes the branch observable.
- `PrioFunPreferMixed` does not call `ClauseIsMixed`; it applies `EQUIV(pos_lit_no, neg_lit_no)`, so the empty clause is preferred as having neither positive nor negative literals. Rust preserves the priority result; later scheduler-facing APIs should decide whether the name should mean strict mixedness or the C "both or neither" rule.
- `PrioFunByNegLitDist` starts at `PrioNormal`, adds `1` for each ground negative literal and `3` for each non-ground negative literal, but returns fixed priority `400` as soon as it encounters any positive literal. Since C clause allocation orders positive literals before negative ones, this effectively makes every non-goal clause `400`; Rust mirrors that order-sensitive behavior.
- `PrioFunPreferHOSteps` scans the derivation stack and computes `has_ho`, but never uses it, so it always returns `PrioNormal`. Rust preserves the current observable result; revisit when higher-order derivation priority tests exist.
- `PrioFunPreferEasyHO` discards its computed non-formula/non-pattern/formula preference through `prio = PrioPrefer ? PrioNormal : PrioDefer`, making the non-`DOArgCong` path always return `PrioNormal`. Rust preserves that normal-result behavior while recognizing represented `DCArgCong` derivations for the higher-order `PrioBest` case; a cleaned heuristic API should revisit whether the discarded preference was accidental only after scheduler reference tests are in place.
- The external priority name is `DeferNonUnitMaxEq`, but the C function is `PrioFunDeferNonUnitMaxPosEq` and the test is specifically "maximal positive equational literal." Rust keeps the external name table exact; a cleaned configuration API should use an unambiguous name only behind compatibility mapping.
- `RDAGWeightInit` stores `max_literal_multiplier`, but `RDAGWeightCompute` never reads it. Rust preserves the unused parameter so parse/control integration can remain source-compatible; decide later whether a cleaned API should reject or repurpose it.
- `RDAGWeight3Compute` names `pneq_multiplier` and `nneq_multiplier` as non-equational-literal multipliers, but the code applies them when `EqnIsEquLit` is true. Rust preserves the equational-literal condition; change it only behind clause-selection reference tests because it directly affects heuristic scores.
- `PropClauseMaxVar` and `PropClauseSetMaxVar` are named as variable scans, but they return the maximum `entry_no` of stored atom terms, and the stored term pointers mean later entry-number mutation is observed. Rust preserves this handle-based behavior; rename or split it only after grounding/DIMACS callers make the expected numbering domain explicit.
- `PropClauseSetInsertClause` destroys the ordinary clause after compacting it. Rust consumes the `Clause` value for the same ownership boundary, but stable clause-set handles may need a clearer transfer API once real clause sets are ported.
- `try_condensation` accepts a `swap` flag and `CondenseOnce` calls it for the swapped retry, but the C implementation always passes `false` into `LiteralUnifyOneWay`, making the swapped retry a duplicate directed attempt. Rust preserves this ignored parameter; revisit it only behind reference tests because enabling swapped condensation changes proof search.
- `ccl_clausecpos` encodes positions as accumulated `TermStandardWeight`/`EqnStandardWeight` offsets rather than as tree child indices or preorder ranks. Rust preserves that compact encoding; any later weight-function change must not be reused for persisted compact positions without a compatibility boundary.
- `PackTermPos` asserts that path superterms are not free variables or lambdas, but still carries phony-application/lambda special cases in the offset calculation. Rust keeps the assertions and phony-application branch; revisit the lambda branch only with LFHO position tests.
- `ClauseCPosSplit` asserts if a compact position lands exactly at or beyond the end of a non-empty clause after subtracting literal weights. Rust preserves that panic; public callers should validate compact positions before accepting untrusted data.
- `ClausePosCell` stores raw clause, literal, term-position, and user-data pointers and the C allocation helper leaves `clause`/`data` uninitialized. Rust initializes them to `None` and uses literal indices for clause-backed cursors; later indexing code should replace cloned clause snapshots with stable handles before clause positions become long-lived.
- `ClausePosGetSide` treats every side other than `LeftSide` as the right side, while `ClausePosGetOtherSide` treats every non-left side as the left side. Rust preserves that branch shape; future public APIs should reject `NoSide`/`BothSides` at boundaries if compatibility allows.
- `TermComputeRWSequence` says it returns true for a zero-length rewrite chain, but the C implementation initializes `res=false` and sets it true only after following at least one rewrite link. Rust preserves the implementation result and documents the comment mismatch for later cleanup.
- `term_follow_top_RW_chain` follows only rewrite links that carry a demodulator pointer and checks the restricted-rewrite bit before recording an SoS rewrite on that link. Rust preserves this ordering through `term_follow_top_rw_chain`; revisit only if later proof-state accounting expects skipped limited links to contribute SoS status.
- `ccl_clausepos_tree` compares `ClauseTPosCell` values by raw clause pointer, so two C clause objects with the same identifier are distinct associations. Rust keys cloned clause snapshots by stable clause identifier so value moves and wrapper-address reuse do not corrupt occurrence positions; long-lived indexes should move to explicit stable clause handles before duplicate identifiers or archive/requeue aliases become observable.
- `ClauseTPosTreePrint` delegates to full `ClausePrint` and `NumTreeDebugPrint`. Rust now has both the compact identifier renderer and explicit LOP/TPTP/TSTP clause renderers over sorted compact positions; exact `NumTreeDebugPrint` tree-shape output remains deferred because the Rust structure intentionally stores a `BTreeSet`.
- `WFormulaReturnFCodes` in `ccl_formula_wrapper.c` deduplicates by setting `FPOpFlag` on signature symbols and clears `TPOpFlag` on collected term cells, even though the query is logically read-only. Rust uses local collection state for the ported wrapper query; a cleaned API should avoid hidden scratch-flag mutation once reference coverage proves no caller depends on those side effects.
- `WAxiomAlloc` in `ccl_axiomsorter.c` initializes `weight` and the union payload but never assigns `ax->type`, even though `WAxiomAddRelEval` and `WAxiomCmp` switch/compare that field. Rust initializes the type to keep the helper usable; verify reference call paths before deciding whether this C uninitialized-read bug needs a compatibility shim.
- `ccl_subterm_tree` compares `SubtermOccCell` values by raw term pointer and keeps backward-rewrite and overlap payloads in a C union. Rust stores both payload shapes in one safe struct and keys terms by handle identity; future index code should separate payload modes or enforce them with types if mixed use becomes risky.
- `SubtermTreePrint`/`SubtermTreePrintDot`/`SubtermTreePrintDummy` depend on full `TermPrint`, raw pointer-address node labels, and optional tree-shaped DOT output. Rust now provides compact entry/f-code debug output, term-bank-backed term rendering, flattened DOT record-label output, and dummy term-count output with explicit problem-type/`DEREF_ALWAYS` printing; exact C splay-node pointer layout and optional tree-shaped `PRT_SUBTERM_SET_AS_TREE` DOT output remain deferred.
- `ccl_subterm_index` descends only one term level when `lambda_demod` is initially false because recursive child calls force `lambda_demod=true`, and initial `lambda_demod=true` suppresses all descent. Rust preserves that collection surface; revisit it only with rewrite-index reference tests because a more intuitive full traversal would change indexed clauses.
- `term_collect_idx_subterms` skips only free variables before considering index insertion, so DB variables are treated as non-free terms and are filtered only by the DB-closed test in lambda-demod mode. Rust keeps that distinction; future higher-order indexing code should make the DB-variable policy explicit at the API boundary.
- `SubtermIndexDeleteOcc` clears the fingerprint leaf only after the subterm-tree payload becomes `NULL`, letting `FPIndexDelete` prune empty branches. Rust mirrors this by clearing the leaf payload before pruning; later index ownership should avoid exposing empty-payload leaves as a public state.
- `ccl_overlap_index` position collectors skip lambda descent but the non-position term collectors recurse through all arguments. Rust preserves the asymmetry; revisit only with LFHO paramodulation reference tests.
- `ClauseCollectIntoTerms2` routes only the top negative non-equational atom term to the negative-atom index and stores its subterms in the normal term index. Rust preserves this split with an explicit `&TermBank` argument for `EqnIsEquLit`; replace the explicit bank parameter with typed literal ownership once available.
- Current overlap-index payloads store cloned clauses under stable clause identifiers through `ClauseTPosTree`. Replace these clones with stable clause handles before proof-state global indexes are long-lived or shared across clause-set mutations. The overlap-index leaf debug renderer now exposes C-shaped leaf/path/subterm/position content, but exact raw splay-node pointer layout remains deferred unless diagnostics require byte-identical tree output.
- `TBGCCollect` walks raw clause-set and formula-set pointers stored in `bank->gc`; the Rust proof state now registers stable handles for represented clause/formula owners, including the separate definition formula archive, in the term bank and resolves them during `collect_term_garbage`, while staged formula-set helper GC still uses explicit marker slices. Replace the remaining explicit marker slices, hard-coded proof-state handles, and arbitrary-handle gaps with the bank-owned registry once clause/formula set handles become typed owners instead of opaque IDs.
- `ClauseDisEqDecomposition` builds new disequalities by prepending each argument pair and then appending the whole temporary list, so decomposed literals appear in reverse argument order before clause allocation partitions signs. Rust preserves that order; change it only if proof-output and search-order comparisons prove it is unobservable.
- `ClauseDisEqDecomposition` immediately records `DCDisEqDecompose` derivation metadata in C. Rust now records the opcode and compact parent reference; replace that compact reference with a stable clause handle before proof-object traversal depends on parent identity.
- `ClauseRecognizeInjectivity` relies on temporary `TPOpFlag`/`TPCheckFlag` markings on shared variable cells and on standard-weight equality to enforce the "all arguments are variables" candidate shape. Rust preserves that flag choreography; a future cleaned API could use an explicit local identity set after compatibility tests cover injectivity preprocessing.
- `ClauseSetInjectivityIsDefined` ignores the generated inverse-Skolem head and compares only the definition arguments/RHS modulo renaming, while `ClauseSetReplaceInjectivityDefs` leaves duplicate original definitions in the active set after discarding the duplicate generated replacement. Rust preserves both behaviors; revisit duplicate handling only after proof-search and proof-output comparisons show whether archiving/deleting duplicates is unobservable.
- `EPIsSplitLit` in `ccl_eqn.h` is `65636`, not the next clean power-of-two bit. That overlaps `EPIsStrictlyMaximal`, `EPMaxIsUpToDate`, and `EPHasEquiv`; Rust preserves the literal value, keeps a raw C-style property helper, and uses a bank-aware proof-search split guard that also recognizes `FPClSplitDef` predicates so selected/evaluated split clauses are not recursively split after maximality/cache flags change.
- `EqnSplitModStandardWeight` gates on `EqnQueryProp(eqn, EPIsSplitLit|EPIsPositive)`, not on `EqnIsSplitLit`, and the checked C source snapshot references `SigGetSpecialWeight` without defining it elsewhere. Rust preserves the gate and leaves the special-symbol weight source explicit; later ordering/signature APIs should decide where that weight belongs.
- `EPDominates` is an alias for `EPIsDominated`, with comments saying the bit is double-used in potentially maximal/minimal clauses. Rust keeps the alias; later literal-ordering code should make the contextual meaning explicit at call sites.
- `EqnAlloc` does not initialize the `pos` field after `EqnCellAlloc`, leaving callers dependent on setting it before any read. Rust initializes `Eqn::position()` to `0`; later clause-list ownership should decide whether a sentinel such as `-1` would make unset positions easier to audit.
- `EqnTermsTBTermEncode` asserts that both side terms are findable in the term bank but then stores the original term pointers, not the representatives returned by `TBFind`. A structurally equal but unshared side can satisfy the `TBFind` assertion and still violate later top-insertion shared-argument assertions; Rust preserves the caller-supplied handles and should keep requiring already-shared inputs.
- `EqnHasUnboundVars` treats every `EqnSide` other than `LeftSide` as `RightSide`, so `NoSide` and `BothSides` are not rejected. Rust preserves this branch shape; later public callers should use a checked side API if invalid values become user-observable.
- `EqnSubsumeInverseRefinedCompareRef` breaks otherwise equal ties with raw `pos` subtraction. Rust preserves the comparator ordering but returns only a comparison sign to avoid importing signed-overflow behavior; later clause-list ownership should keep `pos` ranges explicit if byte-for-byte comparator magnitudes become observable.
- `EqnSubsume` implements its commutative retry by temporarily swapping the subsumer literal in place and then swapping it back. Rust performs the swapped match without transient mutation; future clause/literal APIs should avoid exposing that C implementation detail unless reference tests prove a caller observes it.
- `EqnUnifyDirected` asserts that both equations have the same owning term bank through `eq->bank`, but the current Rust `Eqn` cell does not store a bank back-pointer. Keep callers on same-bank equations where C requires it, and revisit a typed owner handle when clause/literal storage is ported.
- `LiteralUnifyOneWay` calls full `EqnSwapSides` twice when `swapped` is true, restoring the second literal's terms but clearing its orientation/max-up-to-date flags. Rust preserves that side effect; later literal APIs should decide whether this property clearing is intentional proof-search state or an avoidable artifact.
- `EqnSyntaxCompare` assumes both literals' terms are perfectly shared and orders by term-bank `entry_no`, not by structure. Rust preserves the entry-number order; later public comparison APIs should make the bank-sharing precondition explicit instead of accepting arbitrary unbanked terms silently.
- `EqnDAGWeight` mixes equation-wide and per-term duplicate accounting through `TPOpFlag`: `new_eqn` clears both sides once, while `new_terms` clears the left before the left DAG pass and asks the right DAG pass to clear its own side. Rust preserves this flag choreography; later heuristic APIs should make the sharing mode explicit instead of exposing two booleans with overlapping effects.
- `EqnCorrectedWeight` adds the equality-predicate `fweight` before applying the applied-variable multiplier to the right side, while `EqnCorrectedNonLinearWeight` applies the multiplier first and then adds `fweight`. Rust mirrors the formulas exactly; revisit whether the equality-predicate contribution should be multiplied consistently once reference heuristic scores are covered.
- `EqnMaxTermPositions` counts the right side only for unoriented equations, but `EqnInferencePositions` counts the right side only for oriented equations despite both comments describing maximal-term positions. Rust preserves this asymmetry; ordering/inference-position callers should confirm whether it is intentional before exposing a cleaned counter API.
- `LiteralCompareFun` orders terms with raw pointer `MAX`/`MIN` before `PCmp`, making the technical ordering allocator-address dependent. Rust uses stable handle identity as the equivalent compatibility signal; later tree/set APIs should avoid presenting this as semantic literal ordering.
- `EqnMap` normalizes `$false`/`$true`, recomputes the equational-literal bit, and flips polarity for mapped false sides, but it clears orientation/max-up-to-date only when the normalized left term changes. Rust preserves that left-side-only invalidation; revisit once callers show whether right-side-only rewrites can leave stale orientation metadata.
- `ClauseLocalRW` builds its temporary rewrite map with pointer keys, so structurally equal but separately shared terms are distinct rules; it also classifies every negative oriented literal as a rule source, including negative predicate literals if ordering marked them oriented. Rust preserves both behaviors with term identity ids and the same source-skip rule; a cleaned API should make this source classification explicit rather than relying on orientation bits alone.
- `ClauseLocalRW` pushes `DCLocalRewrite` derivation metadata in C. Rust records the no-argument operation entry, while full proof-documentation output and parent-aware proof-object traversal remain pending.
- C `ClauseLocalRW` recomputes literal counts and removes superfluous literals after a rewrite, but it does not directly refresh `clause->weight` unless cleanup removes something. Rust refreshes the cached weight after any local rewrite to keep the clause invariant used by current comparison and indexed-insertion assertions; revisit if full forward-contraction reference tests show stale local-rewrite weights are observable.
- `EqnOrient` returns immediately when `EPMaxIsUpToDate` is set and trusts the existing orientation bit even if the sides would compare differently after external term mutation. Rust preserves that cache contract; later mutation APIs should make orientation invalidation explicit wherever a side term can change.
- `compare_poseqn_negeqn` contains a lesser-case comment marked `Buggy, changed by StS` and classifies the mixed positive/negative case with a broad disjunction over both sides. Rust preserves the branch exactly; revisit only with reference proof-search tests because tightening it would change maximal-literal selection.
- `ccl_g_lithash` stores shared literal term pointers without taking term references and records a clause pointer only until the same literal term appears again; the duplicate path clears the clause even if the duplicate came from the same clause. Rust preserves the term-identity duplicate behavior with cloned handles; replace cloned clause payloads with stable clause handles once grounding clause-set ownership exists.
- `ccl_findex` stores raw `void*` instances in per-f-code `PTree` buckets and uses the same index for clauses, `PList` cells holding clauses, and `PList` cells holding formulas. Rust currently separates direct-clause payloads from typed `PList` clause-cell and wrapped-formula-cell handles and stores cloned direct clauses keyed by stable clause identifier; replace this with typed stable handles before long-lived mixed indexes rely on C's single raw-pointer tree shape.
- `SubstNormEqn`'s C comment says it returns the previous `vars->v_count`, but it actually returns the substitution stack position produced before normalizing the left side and then normalizes the right side too. Rust preserves the stack-position behavior; later docs should describe how to backtrack both sides without mentioning variable-bank counters.
- `ccl_eqnlist` stores list membership in each `EqnCell->next` pointer, so a literal can be in only one C list at a time without relinking. Rust uses an owned `Vec<Eqn>` wrapper; later clause ownership should decide whether stable literal handles are needed before indexes and derivation records share literals.
- `EqnListExtractByProps` removes matching literals from the source list in encounter order but inserts each at the front of the result, reversing the extracted sublist. Rust preserves this order; later APIs should avoid depending on the reversal unless reference tests show callers observe it.
- `EqnListToStack` and `EqnListFromStack` transfer raw literal pointers without copying or freeing literals in C. Rust clones values for stack-shaped conversions in this initial safe wrapper; revisit exact no-copy handle movement when clause lists have stable literal storage.
- `EqnListRemoveDuplicates` switched from a pairwise literal-equality helper to a syntax-comparison object tree, so duplicate detection assumes bank-shared terms and ignores deeper structural equality for unshared equivalent terms. Rust preserves the syntax-key behavior; public callers should keep the sharing precondition explicit.
- `EqnListMaximalLiterals` destructively relinks the candidate list while keeping a stack archive of original literal pointers, then reconstructs the list from that archive. Rust preserves the maximal and strictly-maximal flag effects with an index-based active-candidate list so callers never observe temporary relinking.
- `OCBDebugPrint` has an explicit C branch for `ocb->sig == NULL`, but the later precedence-matrix rendering still calls `OCBFunCompare`, whose distinct-symbol checks dereference `ocb->sig`. Rust keeps the signature outside the OCB and falls back to raw matrix cells when debug-printing without a signature; revisit if null-signature OCBs become observable outside diagnostics.
- `KBOVarGreater`'s C comment says it checks `vars(s)` as a multiset subset of `vars(t)`, but the implementation returns true when no variable has a negative `Var(s)-Var(t)` balance, which is the superset condition needed for `s > t`. Rust preserves the implementation; update comments/API naming only behind compatibility tests.
- Classic `KBOCompare` asserts it is not used for higher-order problems, while `KBO6` carries the active higher-order/LFHO branches. Rust keeps classic KBO first-order-shaped by guarding higher-order problem calls against DB/lambda/phony ordering surfaces; the cleaned ordering dispatcher should make this split explicit instead of exposing two similarly named KBO APIs without problem-type context.
- Classic KBO term-weight accumulation uses C `long` arithmetic with no overflow checks. Rust currently uses ordinary `i64` addition for this compatibility-oriented hot path; revisit checked or saturating behavior only with a clear resource-limit policy because overflow can affect simplification order.
- First-order `kbolincmp` initializes its result to `to_equal` and, for distinct non-variable heads with equal weight and neither greater nor lesser precedence, leaves that `to_equal` result in the release path. The slower `kbo6cmp` assertion path would classify distinct unordered heads as uncomparable. Rust preserves the linear release behavior; decide later whether matrix-backed KBO6 should reject this state or use the slower result.
- `KBO6Compare`/`KBO6Greater` reset OCB balance fields before comparison but do not reliably clear them afterward; debug builds with the C assertion can even leave the slower-check trace in the OCB. Rust preserves the entry reset and observable post-compare trace; a cleaned API could make balance state local to a comparison.
- Standard LPO uses file-static `recursion_depth` plus global `LPORecursionDepthLimit` in C, making the comparison routine non-reentrant and thread-hostile. Rust preserves the process-wide limit but keeps current recursion depth in a comparison-local context; keep that cleanup unless reference tests expose callers that observe the C static state.
- Context-term LPO4 in C mixes first-order cases with `ENABLE_LFHO` normalization (`WHNF_*`, beta/eta reduction, applied-variable instantiation, and head-class ordering). Rust preserves the head-class ordering and hidden-argument offsets for visible DB/lambda/quantifier/symbol/phony surfaces, and the explicit mutable-bank path now covers public `DEREF_ONCE` applied-variable instantiation plus recursive WHNF-step/WHNF-deref preparation, beta-normalized subterm checks, and eta reduction. Revisit C's per-term owner-bank lookup, cache-backed applied-variable/WHNF behavior, and legacy no-bank callers before treating higher-order LPO4 as complete.
- The debug `cto_lpo_debug` implementation predates the production LPO path and has no recursion-depth guard. Its same-head tail checks also iterate with `MAX(s->arity,t->arity)` while indexing only one side's argument vector, so mismatched-arity terms can run past the shorter tuple in C. Rust preserves the intended LPO tail semantics without out-of-bounds access; revisit only if byte-exact debug-path compatibility becomes observable.
- `CmpCache` keys in C are ordered by raw term-pointer addresses and stored in a splay tree for recent-access locality. Rust preserves identity-key behavior through stable term identity ids on top of the existing `QuadTree` abstraction, but exact address ordering and splay locality should be revisited if cached recursive ordering comparisons become a measured hot path.
- `TOGreater` has no explicit C `EMPTY` case: release builds fall through to the initialized `false` result after the default assertion. Rust returns `false` directly for `Empty`; a cleaned dispatcher can treat impossible/non-concrete ordering choices as typed errors once all call sites are audited.
- `SubstComputeMatch` checks repeated matcher-variable bindings with raw pointer identity, so unshared but structurally equal target subterms do not satisfy a repeated variable. Rust preserves this with handle identity; revisit only after parser and term-bank sharing make the observable caller surface clear.
- `SubstComputeMgu` documents that its two inputs must be variable-disjoint and has assert-only sort consistency checks for non-variable terms with matching function codes. Rust keeps those as internal preconditions; a later public API may want structured errors at untrusted boundaries.
- `TermCopy` assigns the returned handle's LFHO owner bank from the dereferenced source after copying, even for variables allocated through the target `VarBank`. Rust does not store owner-bank metadata yet; when owner handles are added for global applied-variable dereferencing, audit whether this source-bank overwrite is compatibility-required or should become a narrower cache/normalization boundary.
- `TermCheckConsistency` prints a start banner, one dot per visited dereferenced node, and an end banner to stdout while checking for branch cycles. Rust now has both an output-free reusable helper and an explicit output-aware compatibility wrapper; keep the wrapper confined to C-compatible diagnostics rather than ordinary structure checks.
- The initial `cte_termbanks` slice does not store a back-pointer from each term to its owning bank. The C field is only active under LFHO; add a typed owner handle before cache-backed applied-variable dereferencing and lambda normalization are treated as complete.
- C `applied_var_deref` caches expanded applied-variable terms in `binding_cache`, records the binding that made the cache fresh, and sets `TPIsDerefedAppVar` on the cached term. Rust currently expands applied free variables directly for global `term_deref`, the recursive term-bank insertion family, higher-order instantiated insertion, instantiated-deref insertion, deref-aware debug and conventional higher-order term printing, and the KBO6 LFHO comparison path without a cache; add the cache before relying on repeated LFHO dereference performance or cache-aware GC behavior outside these scoped paths.
- `NormalizePatternAppVar` from `cte_termbanks` first calls `LambdaEtaReduceDB`, then accepts applied free variables whose visible arguments are DB variables and pass `TermArrayNoDuplicates`. Rust now applies the already-normalized pattern-shaped branch during term-bank metadata setup, including the single-variable count/weight adjustment; eta-reducing normalization before the check remains part of the LFHO/lambda term-bank slice.
- `TBRefDelProp` in `cte_termbanks` builds a top copy with properties removed, but the current C `TermTopCompare` ignores properties despite older comments describing masked properties in the key. Rust preserves the observable current behavior: reinsertion reuses the existing cell and therefore cannot split a property-deleted variant unless the term-tree key is changed later.
- `TBGCMarkTerm` in C also marks `TermGetCache(term)` when LFHO term caches are enabled. Rust currently marks ordinary arguments and rewrite replacements; add cache marking when applied-variable dereference caches are represented.
- `TermIsSubtermDeref` dereferences the current superterm but then recurses into every child with the same post-root dereference mode instead of using the LFHO `DEREF_LIMIT`/`CONVERT_DEREF` prefix conversion that `TermIsSubterm` uses. Rust preserves this C behavior, including the way it can follow bindings inside an applied-variable binding prefix; revisit only after callers prove whether this unused-in-C helper should be compatibility-exact or cleaned up.
- Several `ccl_satinterface` constant-frequency comparator tie-breaks use assignment (`=`) inside conditions where equality may have been intended, mutating `conj_dist_array` while comparing candidates. Rust SAT grounding preserves that input mutation through the `TBGetFreqConstTerm`-shaped comparator callback; keep it isolated so a later cleaned grounding API can switch to ordinary equality only behind reference-tested strategy compatibility.
- Full `cte_termbanks` real parser integration, cache-backed global applied-variable `TermDeref`/WHNF integration, remaining executable-output integration for global type-printing and formula-owner entry points, and LFHO `MakeRewrittenTerm` callers outside the `TBTermPosReplace` retained-argument subset remain to be ported in later term-bank slices. Current Rust bank parsing covers the simple term parser, checked list literals, checked `$ite` term parsing with fixed non-predicate term-valued branches, scoped variable branches, untyped default-individual zero-arity branch recovery, and typed-argument fresh compound branch recovery, checked fixed non-Boolean `$let` definitions and bodies, checked non-Boolean argument parsing, a public TSTP application-term entry point for THF-style executable targets, and the first Boolean formula-argument bridge for term-encoded atomic/negated/binary/first-order quantified/`$ite`/`$let` formulas; current bank printing covers compact output, entry-ordered DAG output, top-position term printing, problem-type-explicit debug term printing with explicit deref modes, a first-order conventional term view for ordinary/list/FOOL/`$let` terms, and the higher-order conventional application, FOOL, and lambda surfaces for ordinary/phony/DB/`$ite`/Boolean-formula/lambda terms while preserving C's higher-order `$let` fall-through.
